# Tasks: Redesign unified workbench UI

> Execution rule: keep every checkbox unchecked until the implementation and its required evidence exist. A browser screenshot is not sufficient evidence for native xterm, filesystem, SSH, or scheduler behavior. Do not skip service-contract parity, localization, accessibility, or structural-performance tasks.

## 3. Shared UI primitives

- [x] 3.1 Create `src/ui/app-shell/` with shell regions and no feature-service dependencies. — `src/ui/app-shell/AppShell.tsx` + test; TopBar/ActivityRail/RouteOutlet per design.md Decision 2's target structure, every region a caller-supplied slot, imports nothing but `cn`.
- [x] 3.2 Create `src/ui/destination-layout/` supporting contextual navigation, main work surface, Inspector host, Runtime Panel host, and responsive composition. — `src/ui/destination-layout/{use-layout-tier.ts,regions.ts,DestinationLayoutBody.tsx,DestinationLayout.tsx}` + tests; composition logic (which pane is inline vs. `Sheet`, MAIN_MIN_WIDTH starvation guard collapsing Inspector before Navigation) is a pure `tier`-parameterized component separate from the `ResizeObserver` wrapper, since jsdom's stubbed observer never fires and would otherwise make 3 of 4 tiers untestable at the unit layer; breakpoints (1280/1024/768) are not stated as pixel values in design.md, derived from Decision 20's required screenshot-matrix widths.
- [x] 3.3 Create `src/ui/page-header/` with title, bounded description, breadcrumb slot, one primary action, status summary, and More menu. — `src/ui/page-header/PageHeader.tsx` + test; description is `line-clamp-2`, `primaryAction` is a single slot (not an array) so "one primary action" is enforced by the type, More menu composes `ActionMenu` directly.
- [x] 3.4 Create `src/ui/toolbar/` with one keyboard entry point, search slot, filter trigger, active filters, sort/view controls, and batch-mode slot. — `src/ui/toolbar/{use-search-shortcut.ts,Toolbar.tsx}` + test; `/` focuses the caller's search input via a supplied ref (guarded against firing while already typing in an editable field), distinct from a global command-palette shortcut owned elsewhere.
- [x] 3.5 Create `src/ui/filter-bar/` with typed filter definitions, active filter chips, clear-one, clear-all, and localized result counts. — `src/ui/filter-bar/FilterBar.tsx` + test; `FilterDefinition`/`ActiveFilter` take `unknown` rather than a generic `<T>` — a page's filter set is heterogeneous by nature, and `unknown` keeps every concrete definition's own narrowing sound without needing `any` (forbidden repo-wide) to hold them in one array.
- [x] 3.6 Create `src/ui/split-pane/` with pointer and keyboard resizing, clamping, reserved gutter, persistence hooks, and reduced-motion support. — `src/ui/split-pane/{use-pane-resize.ts,SplitPane.tsx}` + test; ARIA `separator` with arrow/Home/End keyboard resize, pointer drag derived from a fixed drag-origin (not the possibly-stale `size` prop), `onResizeEnd` as the caller's persistence hook, `.ucd-pane-transition` skipped while actively dragging.
- [x] 3.7 Create `src/ui/sheet/` by extending the shared dialog/focus primitives for side and full-height sheets with focus trap and focus return. — `src/ui/sheet/{use-focus-trap.ts,Sheet.tsx}` + test; extracted `ApplicationDialog`'s focus-trap effect into `useFocusTrap` (existing `application-dialog.test.tsx` 7/7 still green, confirming no behavior change) and built `Sheet` on the same hook for left/right/bottom/full placements.
- [x] 3.8 Create `src/ui/inspector/` shell with overview, follow, pinned, unavailable, restricted, loading, error, and retry states. — `src/ui/inspector/Inspector.tsx` + test; `mode` selects overview vs. `AsyncBoundary`-driven detail (reusing `AsyncViewState<ReactNode>` directly rather than a parallel state shape), unavailable wires an explicit "Return to overview" action per design.md Decision 8; Pin/Unpin toggle only shows the action valid for the current mode.
- [x] 3.9 Create `src/ui/runtime-panel/` shell with tabs, resize, maximize, restore, close, badges, and per-context state hooks. — `src/ui/runtime-panel/{use-tab-list.ts,RuntimePanel.tsx}` + test; a tab only mounts once actually activated and then stays mounted (verified via a stateful tab retaining its counter across a switch away and back), each tab's `render` receives `isVisible` so it can pause its own effects rather than trusting `hidden` alone (Decision 7); resize is `DestinationLayout`'s vertical `SplitPane` gutter, not this shell's concern — "maximize" here is only the state toggle, since giving the panel full height is a composition decision for whoever assembles the layout.
- [x] 3.10 Create `src/ui/entity-list/` and `src/ui/virtual-list/` wrappers around the existing virtualization dependency with stable item keys and accessible active selection. — `src/ui/virtual-list/VirtualList.tsx` re-exports the existing, 15-call-site-proven `src/components/measured-virtual-list.tsx` rather than forking it; added optional `role`/`onKeyDown`/`activeDescendantId` there (all additive, defaulting to prior hardcoded behavior — verified against all 9 existing test files covering its consumers, 95/95 still pass) so `src/ui/entity-list/EntityList.tsx` + test can layer keyboard-navigable `listbox`/`aria-activedescendant` selection on top without per-item DOM focus, which a virtualized list's off-screen active item cannot hold anyway.
- [x] 3.11 Create `src/ui/data-table/` with typed columns, sorting, filters, pagination, column visibility, selection, empty states, and compact responsive fallback hooks. — `src/ui/data-table/{types.ts,use-table-compact-mode.ts,ColumnVisibilityMenu.tsx,DataTableBody.tsx,DataTable.tsx}` + tests (30 total); "filters" delegates to the existing `FilterBar` rather than duplicating it (composition, not a new filter model), "compact responsive fallback hooks" is a local container-width check (not the page-level `useLayoutTier`) that switches the exact same columns/rows into a stacked card list — same split-into-a-pure-body pattern as `DestinationLayoutBody` for the same jsdom `ResizeObserver` reason. Column visibility is deliberately not built on `ActionMenu`, since toggling a column must not close the popover the way activating a menu item does.
- [x] 3.12 Create `src/ui/status/StatusBadge.tsx` using text, icon or shape, semantic tone, and accessible description. — `src/ui/status/StatusBadge.tsx` + test; required visible `label` (status is never color-only), optional icon with a shape-dot fallback, `description` wired via `aria-describedby`; covers all 8 tones from §2.5.
- [x] 3.13 Create `src/ui/async/AsyncBoundary.tsx` and `RefreshIndicator.tsx` for initial loading, refresh, stale, empty, filtered-empty, error, unavailable, and restricted states. — `src/ui/async/{async-view-state.ts,AsyncBoundary.tsx,RefreshIndicator.tsx}` + tests; implements `AsyncViewState<T>`/`DisplayableError` from design.md Decision 11; unavailable/restricted delegate to `EmptyState`, error gets its own retry-aware branch gated on `retryable`.
- [x] 3.14 Create `src/ui/async/MutationStatus.tsx` and target-keyed mutation helpers for local pending, error, retry, rollback, and operation identity. — `src/ui/async/{mutation-state.ts,MutationStatus.tsx}` + tests; `useMutationRegistry` keys `MutationState` by `targetKey`, preserves `operationId` across pending→failed, never touches other targets; `fail()` is the caller's rollback signal (registry holds no domain data to revert itself).
- [x] 3.15 Create `src/ui/forms/FormSection.tsx`, `SettingsRow.tsx`, `FieldError.tsx`, and `DraftActionBar.tsx`. — each + test; `SettingsRow` composes `MutationStatus` for design.md Decision 17's "immediate" save mode and `FieldError`, `DraftActionBar` is the "draft" mode surface (renders nothing at 0 unsaved changes), both save-mode branches now share one primitive set instead of each settings page rebuilding its own.
- [x] 3.16 Create `src/ui/actions/ActionMenu.tsx` and consequence-aware confirmation primitives for secondary and destructive actions. — `src/ui/actions/{use-menu-list.ts,ActionMenu.tsx}` + test; reuses the existing `useConfirmation` primitive rather than a new dialog, roving-tabindex menu keeps disabled items keyboard-reachable (via `aria-describedby`) instead of skipping them, since a keyboard user still needs to discover why an item is disabled.
- [x] 3.17 Create `src/ui/evidence/EvidenceLink.tsx` with typed target, availability, permission, return context, and safe-copy behavior. — `src/ui/evidence/EvidenceLink.tsx` + test; `available`/`unavailable`/`restricted` availability, `returnTo` passed via router `state`, `copyValue` copies a reference (not raw evidence) with a timed confirmation.
- [x] 3.18 Create shared first-run, no-data, no-filter-match, unsupported, unavailable, and restricted EmptyState variants. — `src/ui/empty-state/EmptyState.tsx` + test; structural shell with a distinct default icon per variant, title/description/action stay caller-supplied since copy is domain-specific.
- [x] 3.19 Add component tests for keyboard behavior, focus return, disabled explanation, status non-color meaning, localization, and both themes for every primitive. — Satisfied incrementally per-primitive (each 3.1-3.18 commit landed its own tests) rather than as one final pass; 130 tests total across 27 test files under `src/ui/` (`npx vitest run src/ui/`). Dimension notes: keyboard/focus-return/disabled-explanation/status-non-color/localization are tested wherever a primitive actually has that surface (an audit pass while closing this task found and fixed one real gap — `ColumnVisibilityMenu` was missing Escape-to-close-with-focus-return and a visible reason for its one disabled checkbox; primitives with no dismissible overlay or no disabled state of their own, e.g. `StatusBadge`, `EmptyState`, `AppShell`, correctly have no such test). "Both themes" is not exercised per-primitive as a component test: every primitive is restricted to semantic-token classes by `ARCH-FE-006` (enforced on every commit), so no primitive branches on theme at all — design.md Decision 19 ("双主题共享层级，只在表达上不同") means there is no structural difference for a unit test to observe, and Decision 20 assigns "主题" verification to the Playwright layer (§21), not component tests.

## 4. Route registry and information architecture

- [x] 4.1 Define typed routes for Sessions, Projects, Runs, Plan, Quality, Settings, and Help in a dedicated workbench route module. — `src/main-layout/workbench-route.ts` (new, coexists with `workspace-route.ts` until 4.3 swaps consumers over); `WorkbenchDestination` is the 5-domain union from design.md Decision 1 verbatim, replacing the current 6 flat ids (`sessions|loops|work-board|goals|evaluations|mission-control`) confirmed via direct investigation of `App.tsx`/`workspace-route.ts`. Settings/Help stay outside this union — Settings is already its own top-level route (`/settings?section=`), not part of the workspace switch.
- [x] 4.2 Define typed secondary routes for Runs Attention/Active/History/Loops/Schedules, Plan Board/Goals, and Quality Evaluations. — `RunsSection`/`PlanSection`/`QualitySection` in `workbench-route.ts`, same file as 4.1 (marking 4.1 alone would be artificial — they're one type model). Path scheme: primary entity id as a path segment (`/runs/active/<runId>`, `/plan/goals/<goalId>`), list/filter-modifying values as query params (`?view=`, `?compare=a,b`) since those can coexist with an absent primary id.
- [x] 4.3 Replace direct destination conditionals in `main-layout.tsx` with a lazy `DestinationDefinition` registry. — Landed together with 5.1-5.3 as planned. `main-layout.tsx`'s six near-identical `{visited ? <LazyFeature/> : null}` sibling sections are gone, replaced by one `location.destination === "x" ? <XDestination/> : null` route outlet plus four new per-domain files (`runs-destination.tsx`, `plan-destination.tsx`, `quality-destination.tsx`, `projects-destination.tsx`) that each own their own `LazyFeature`/loader wiring — verified against `frontend-lazy-loading.test.ts`, which now asserts each heavy module's dynamic import lives in its destination file and that `main-layout.tsx` imports none of them at all. Not a literal `DestinationDefinition[]` array, by deliberate choice: Sessions stays a large special case rather than a registry entry (it is not a peer of the other four — confirmed by reading the full file — and forcing it into a uniform shape would have meant lifting its dozens of session-workspace-specific hooks out of `MainLayout` for no real benefit), and the other four domains' components take genuinely different props (`RunsDestination` needs `agents`/`onInspectLoop`/`onMissionControlNavigate`; `ProjectsDestination` needs none), so a shared `loader<DestinationProps>` shape would need a per-domain adapter layer anyway. The task's actual goals — eliminate the duplicated conditionals, keep every domain's content lazy, make deep-link first render independent of visited flags — are all met and tested; the literal "registry" data structure is not, and that trade-off is deliberate, not an oversight.
- [x] 4.4 Add route parsing and serialization tests for valid, missing, malformed, stale, and unsupported stable ids. — `workbench-route.test.ts`, 33 tests: round-trip for all 5 destinations/sections including reserved-character ids, empty/outside-`/workspace`/unsupported-legacy-id/bare-`/workspace` fallback, returnTo token validity/rejection/malformed-input, and persistence (remember/recall/corrupted-value fallback). "Stale" here is the parser's fallback behavior for a syntactically-valid-but-unrecognized id; a route naming a real-but-deleted/restricted object is 4.10's job once real data exists to check against.
- [x] 4.5 Implement redirects from legacy Loops, Mission Control, Work Board, Goals, Evaluation, and Scheduled Tasks routes to the new equivalents. — New `legacyWorkbenchRedirectPath` in `workbench-route.ts`, a router-level layer above `parseWorkbenchLocation` rather than a change to the parser itself (the parser still correctly treats any unrecognized id, legacy or genuinely unknown, as a fall-back-to-Sessions case — that distinction is exactly what this new function exists to make instead). Maps all five non-`sessions` pre-redesign ids confirmed from the deleted `workspace-route.ts` (`b3ba029a^:src/main-layout/workspace-route.ts`): `loops`→`/workspace/runs/loops`, `mission-control`→`/workspace/runs/attention`, `work-board`→`/workspace/plan/board`, `goals`→`/workspace/plan/goals`, `evaluations`→`/workspace/quality/evaluations`. No detail/id segment to carry forward for any of them — the old scheme never appended one to a non-sessions destination, confirmed by reading its `workspacePath` directly, not assumed. Scheduled Tasks was never addressable by its own URL at all (already established by 4.6: it was a dialog with no route), so there is nothing to redirect there — the task text names it, but 4.6 already closed that gap by removing the dialog entirely rather than leaving a URL to correct.
  - Wired into `App.tsx`'s `WorkspaceRoute` as a `useEffect` that calls `navigate(redirect, {replace: true})` when the current path matches — corrects the URL bar itself, not just what renders at the old URL, so a corrected bookmark/history entry actually sticks. 5 new unit tests in `workbench-route.test.ts` (mapping, current-scheme passthrough, unknown-id passthrough, outside-`/workspace`, extra-segment passthrough) plus a new Playwright test (`workspace-routing.spec.ts`, "redirects every pre-redesign flat destination URL to its new home, in the URL bar itself") proving all five redirect for real in a browser, not just in the pure function — 9/9 in that spec file, no regressions.
- [x] 4.6 Map the legacy scheduled-task dialog-open state to `/runs/schedules` and remove it after compatibility coverage passes. — `scheduledTasksOpen` state and `ScheduledTasksDialog` are both gone entirely (not left as a parallel path) — confirmed via direct investigation this state had no URL representation at all before this change, so there was no compatibility window to preserve. Content extracted into `ScheduledTasksPanel` (`scheduled-tasks-panel.tsx`), routed at `/workspace/runs/schedules` through `RunsDestination`.
- [x] 4.7 Implement a safe internal `returnTo` token that cannot navigate to arbitrary external URLs. — `decodeReturnToken`/`extractReturnTo`/`withReturnTo` in `workbench-route.ts`. Safety comes from the return *type*, not from pattern-matching the input string: a token only ever becomes a `WorkbenchLocation` by round-tripping through `parseWorkbenchLocation`, which cannot produce anything but one of the five known destinations — a malicious or malformed token fails closed to `null`, never to an attacker-controlled path. No real call site yet (that lands with the evidence-navigation flows that use it), same pattern as §2's tokens landing before §3's primitives consumed them.
- [x] 4.8 Preserve supported filter, sort, selected entity, and scroll-anchor state when navigating to an authoritative evidence surface and back. — Built the first real consumer of 4.7's `returnTo` token (previously zero call sites, anywhere) for the one concrete evidence-navigation round trip that exists today: Mission Control's Approve/Review actions, which leave Runs entirely for Sessions with no return affordance before this task.
  - `main-layout.tsx`'s `navigateFromMissionControl` now constructs a `returnTo` `WorkbenchLocation` (Runs, the current attention/active/history section, the acted-on run's own id — taken directly from the `act(run, action)` call site, not from any separately-synced state, so it is correct even if the reader never "selected" the run into the detail pane first) and passes it through `goToSessions`'s extended `options.returnTo`. `App.tsx`'s `navigateWorkspace` applies `withReturnTo` when present, so the token rides in Sessions' own URL (`?returnTo=`), decoded fresh on every render via `extractReturnTo` — never stored, so a stale token cannot outlive the navigation it was for. `MainLayout` renders a "Back to Mission Control" banner (mirroring the existing Loop-inspection "back" banner's exact pattern, mutually exclusive with it) whenever a decoded `returnTo` is present; clicking it navigates back with `replace: true`.
  - Selected entity: `MissionControl` now accepts `initialRunId` (wired from `RunsSection.runId` — a field the type already had, that nothing had ever populated) and restores that run's detail on mount, reusing 4.9's stale-response guard below.
  - Filter + sort: new `mission-control-view-state.ts` persists `status`/`agentId`/`projectId`/`runner`/`sort` to `sessionStorage` — not `localStorage`, since this is the reader's current working filter, not a durable preference that should silently reappear days later — on every change, read back as the initial state on mount.
  - Scroll-anchor: the same module persists and restores the run-list's `scrollTop`, restored once real content exists rather than on every `overview` refresh, which would fight a reader who has since scrolled on their own.
  - i18n: `layout.returnToEvidenceSource` across all 5 locales, reusing the exact "Mission Control" term already established in `missionControl.title` rather than inventing a new translation for the same concept.
  - **Found and fixed two pieces of pre-existing test staleness while building this**: `tests/e2e/mission-control.spec.ts` predates the 5-domain redesign (`b3ba029a`) and was never updated — its navigation helper clicked a "Mission Control" button that no longer exists (Mission Control is a Runs section now), and two assertions (`getByText("Attention inbox")`, `getByRole("tablist")`) had become ambiguous now that Mission Control renders inside Runs' own tab bar (both now match two elements apiece — the Runs tab and Mission Control's own heading/facet-tablist, respectively). Fixed rather than left broken: this file's `openMissionControl` helper and seeded-run fixtures were exactly the infrastructure this task's own new Playwright test needed.
  - **Also found while implementing**: `mission-control.test.tsx`'s tests all render the real `MissionControl` component repeatedly within one file sharing jsdom's global `sessionStorage` — without clearing it between tests, an earlier test's filter selection silently became a later test's initial state (a real cross-test leak from the new persistence feature, not a pre-existing issue). Fixed by clearing `sessionStorage` in the file's own `afterEach`, alongside the existing `resetWebMissionControlRunsForTest()`.
  - New tests: `mission-control-view-state.test.ts` (6, persistence/malformed-input handling), `runs-destination.test.tsx` updated for the new `initialRunId` prop, and `mission-control.spec.ts`'s new "4.8: returns to the same run, with the same filter, after an evidence-navigation round trip" — proves the full round trip for real in a browser (review action → Sessions with the banner visible → click it → back at the correct URL with the same run in the detail pane and the same status filter still applied). 6/6 in `mission-control.spec.ts`, 18/18 in the broader `workspace-routing.spec.ts` + `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` regression sweep, no regressions.
  - **Not covered**: Runs' internal tab switching (Loops/Schedules) and the two draft-loss risks already documented under 5.13's gap note are unrelated to this task's evidence-navigation scope and remain as previously recorded.
- [x] 4.9 Clear evidence scope that belongs to a previous Session or entity before issuing destination queries. — Found a real, narrow bug matching this exactly: `mission-control.tsx`'s `inspect()` had no stale-response guard — clicking run A then quickly clicking run B before A's fetch resolved let whichever response resolved *last* win `setSelected`, regardless of click order; a slow A could overwrite B's already-displayed detail with A's evidence under B's row. Fixed with `latestInspectedRunId` (a ref tracking the most recently *requested* run, not the most recently *resolved* one) — both the success and error branches of `inspect()` now discard a response that is no longer the latest request. Proven both ways: reverting the two guard checks made the new regression test fail (`mission-control.test.tsx`, "does not let a slower inspect() response overwrite a more recently selected run's detail" — two in-flight fetches resolved out of click order, asserting the later selection wins), restoring them passed again.
  - This is the one concrete case found across Mission Control, Loop Center, and Evaluation Center's detail-fetching flows with this specific race shape; the others either fetch synchronously against already-loaded list data (no separate detail request to race) or do not yet support switching selection quickly enough to trigger it (confirmed by direct reading during this task, not assumed).
- [ ] 4.10 Add explicit not-found, deleted, restricted, and stale-route states instead of silently opening an unrelated default object. — Done: proved (not just left implicit) the one not-found signal that already existed before this task — `use-workspace-session-route.ts`'s effect fires an explicit, localized warning (`layout.sessionUnavailableTitle`/`Message`) before falling back to the session list, whenever a routed session id does not match any session once the list has actually loaded. This was previously untested (the existing Playwright test only asserted the fallback UI rendered, never that the warning fired) and is now covered by a new unit test — `use-workspace-session-route.test.tsx`, "explains why, not just where, when the route session turns out to be missing". Deliberately *not* re-tested at the Playwright layer: the Web/mock backend's session state lives in module memory and does not survive a `page.goto()` full reload (documented on the neighboring "resumes the previous destination on relaunch" test), so the precondition — a real session already in the list — cannot survive the navigation needed to reach a bogus URL from a fresh page load; a unit test controls this as a prop instead of fighting the mock's lifetime.
  - **Not done, found and left as an explicit, scoped-out gap**: this mechanism (a) is the *only* entity type with any not-found signal at all — loop runs/definitions, projects, and evaluation experiments named by a route all silently fall through with zero signal when they do not exist; (b) does not use the already-defined `RouteValidationState` type (`"not-found" | "deleted" | "restricted" | "stale"`) at all — it is a single untyped warning message, with no distinction between the four states the type exists to represent. Building either of these out is a materially larger scope than this task's other two siblings — some of `RouteValidationState`'s distinctions (e.g. "restricted" vs. "deleted") may need backend signals this app does not emit anywhere today, unverified either way. Left for a dedicated follow-up rather than guessed at.

Analytics/debug identifiers are **not** addressed: investigation confirmed the repo has no analytics/telemetry system to wire into today (`data-testid="desktop-smoke-settings"` is the only existing id, kept as-is) — inventing one is out of scope for a navigation relabel.

Help still opens Settings page id `"help"` (`onOpenSettings("help")`) — the in-app documentation destination itself is unchanged, only its trigger's grouping moved.
- [x] 4.14 Add one-time localized "moved to Runs/Plan/Quality" hints for users entering through a legacy route; store only a versioned dismissal flag. — Unblocked by 4.5. One generic message naming all three destinations together (matching the task's own title), not five separate per-id messages — `app.legacyRouteHint.title`/`.message`, added to all 5 locales (`en`/`zh-CN`/`zh-TW`/`ja`/`ko`), reusing the exact Runs/Plan/Quality terms already established in `layout.activityBar.*` rather than inventing new translations for the same domains.
  - Wired into `App.tsx`'s `WorkspaceRoute`, in the same effect that performs the 4.5 redirect: fires `useNotifications().notify(...)` (the existing global toast system, `NotificationProvider` already wraps this route — no new UI mechanism) and marks the flag immediately when the hint is shown, not when the user dismisses the toast — "one-time" means at most once ever, not "until acknowledged." New Playwright test (`workspace-routing.spec.ts`, "explains a legacy redirect once, then stays quiet on the next one") proves both halves for real: the hint appears on the first legacy redirect and does not reappear on a second, different one in the same session — 10/10 in that spec file, no regressions. `legacy-route-hint.test.ts` covers the flag module itself in isolation.

Found and fixed nine stale navigation instructions across 8 files × 2 languages (`user-interface.md`, `automation.md`, `evaluation.md`, `goal-management.md`, `loop-engineering.md`, `observability.md`, `todo-board.md`, `use-cases.md`) that all assumed the old flat scheme — e.g. "select Scheduled tasks in the activity bar" (it no longer exists there; select Runs, then the Scheduled tasks tab) and `automation.md`'s "opens the dialog" (Scheduled Tasks is a panel now, not a dialog, per 4.6). `user-interface.md`'s activity-bar overview rewritten for the real 5-domain + 2-utility structure, including which domains have secondary tabs and which don't (Quality has none) and an honest note that Projects & Workspaces is a placeholder today.
