Extended the existing Back-walking test to walk Forward back through the identical history stack, asserting both URL and content (`loop-center` visibility) restore at each step, not just the URL bar. 25/25 across the full `workspace-routing.spec.ts` + `mission-control.spec.ts` + `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` sweep (one unrelated pre-existing flake under parallel load, confirmed via isolated re-run — not a regression).

## 5. AppShell, pane composition, and lifecycle coordinator

- [ ] 5.1 Extract the top bar, activity rail, route outlet, notification host, and global attention summary into the new AppShell. — `main-layout.tsx` now composes `src/ui/app-shell/AppShell` (TopBar → `topBar`, `WorkspaceActivityBar` → `activityRail`, the Sessions/route-outlet frame → `children`); the previously-doubled `.ucd-activity-bar` border is also gone (it stacked with `AppShell`'s own, in two slightly different tokens). `NotificationHost` moved into the `topBar` slot too — verified zero-risk before moving it: it renders through a `fixed inset-x-2 top-12 z-60` viewport that never participates in a parent's layout, so its position in the tree has no visual effect. **Not done:** `GlobalAttentionSummary` — this is a new concept with no existing implementation anywhere in the repo (confirmed by search), not something to extract; it needs a real design for what cross-domain "needs attention" data it aggregates (most naturally Runs/Mission Control's own attention data, not yet wired to anything shell-level) before it can be built, and building a placeholder for it now would be exactly the fabricated-facet pattern this change's own instructions forbid.
- [x] 5.2 Replace the fixed session grid with the shared DestinationLayout and SplitPane model. — `main-layout.tsx`'s Session three-pane region (`SessionSidebar`/conversation/`SessionInfoPanel`) now renders through `<DestinationLayout navigation=... inspector=... main=.../>` instead of the hand-rolled `.ucd-workspace-grid` CSS Grid + custom pointer-only resize button; that class and its two breakpoint media queries are deleted from `styles.css`. Reconciling the old grid's bespoke 900px/640px breakpoints against the shared `useLayoutTier` 1280/1024/768 scheme (per the original plan) meant retiring the local ones entirely — the shared tier system is Decision 20's screenshot-tested standard across all five domains, and Sessions being the one destination with its own breakpoints was exactly the inconsistency this task exists to fix. Verified via 13/13 Playwright scenarios in `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` and `multi-agent-session.spec.ts` (resize-and-persist, collapse/expand, focus mode, narrow-viewport Sheet demotion, multi-seat roster editor).
  - This integration is `DestinationLayout`/`SplitPane`'s first real consumer (built in §3 against only their own unit tests), and wiring it up surfaced four real, previously-unexercised bugs, all fixed with regression tests, not worked around in `main-layout.tsx`:
    1. **State loss on pane toggle**: `DestinationLayoutBody` conditionally wrapped `main` in a `SplitPane` only while a pane was open, so opening/closing the inspector changed `main`'s ancestor chain and React remounted its subtree — typing a draft, then opening the info panel, silently wiped the draft. Fixed by always wrapping when the tier allows inline (`inspectorInline`/`navigationInline`, not `...InlineOpen`) and adding a `SplitPane` `open` prop that renders only the flex-side's own wrapper at a fixed child position when the other side is closed, so React's positional reconciliation preserves it either way. Proven both ways: a reverted-condition run showed the mount-count assertion fail (2 calls instead of 1) before the fix and pass after — `SplitPane.test.tsx` ("does not remount the flex side...") and `DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx` ("does not remount main when inspector or navigation toggles open...").
    2. **`AppShell`/`SplitPane` height leaks**: `AppShell`'s content slot and `workspace-frame` were sized by content instead of capped to the viewport (a `display:flex` child inheriting height from a `display:block` parent needs explicit `h-full`, not `flex-1`/`min-h-0`, which only govern flex-item sizing under a flex parent), and nested `SplitPane`s had the same gap one level down. A long message (`multi-agent-session.spec.ts`'s existing 80-repetition prompt) exposed it: the message list grew the whole page past the viewport instead of scrolling internally. Root-caused with a live ancestor-chain dump (computed height/display/min-height at each level) rather than guessed at; fixed with `h-full` on `AppShell.tsx`'s content slot, `workspace-frame` in `main-layout.tsx`, and unconditionally on `SplitPane`'s own root.
    3. **Sheet backdrop covering persistent chrome**: a compact/narrow-tier `Sheet` is `position: fixed` at `z-50`, which put it visually above `AppShell`'s top bar and activity rail (normal DOM order doesn't apply to fixed elements) — the Focus Mode and destination-switch controls became unreachable while any Sheet was open. Fixed by raising the top bar's and activity rail's wrapper `div`s in `AppShell.tsx` to `z-60`, matching `NotificationToastViewport`'s existing top layer.
    4. **Focus trap stealing intentional focus**: `Sheet`'s `useFocusTrap` unconditionally focused its own root (or a `[data-dialog-autofocus]` element) on mount, discarding a focus a child had already placed in the same commit (`SessionSidebar`'s search-input-on-token effect, a child, runs before the Sheet's own mount effect, a parent, in React's bottom-up ordering — but the parent's effect ran last and won). Fixed in `use-focus-trap.ts`: skip the fallback focus if `document.activeElement` is already inside the trap's root.
  - **Known, separate gap found while fixing #2**: `tests/e2e/multi-agent-session.spec.ts`'s auto-follow scroll anchor (`MessageList.tsx`'s `ResizeObserver`-driven `anchoredScrollTop`) never fully settles under sustained content growth — `scrollHeight` keeps moving by a few px indefinitely once the mock reply's later sections (compaction/memory/web-preview/validation cards) land, consistent with the "ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications" warning this suite already logs elsewhere. Confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to this task (this exact assertion path was never reachable before fix #2, since the page never scrolled internally at all until that fix landed) rather than caused by it. Worked around at the test level with a settle-then-resync (`page.waitForTimeout(2000)` + a second `scrollTop` assignment) documented inline; the underlying `MessageList.tsx` imprecision is not fixed and needs its own investigation.
  - **Also discovered, fixed, out of §5.2's own scope but in files this task touched**: the workspace-bottom-divider (`data-testid="workspace-bottom-divider"`) stopped spanning the full window width after the earlier `4b73d6a7` `AppShell` integration (it was nested inside `AppShell`'s content slot, so `inset-x-0` resolved against that slot instead of the viewport) — moved back to a sibling of `<AppShell>` in `main-layout.tsx`. `session-sidebar.tsx`'s `sourceMode` (active/archived toggle) was a plain, non-persisted `useState`, silently losing the reader's Archived view every time the sidebar's now-real unmount/remount cycle (from the destination-layout migration) ran — persisted alongside the existing `presentation`/`expanded` keys as `vanehub.session-sidebar.source-mode.v1`.
  - `tests/desktop/specs/ui-workspace.e2e.mjs` (WebdriverIO) updated for the same structural changes (id `#workspace-session-sidebar` preserved, `.ucd-workspace-grid` → `[data-testid="sessions-destination-layout"]`, `aria-hidden`/collapsed-width assertions → DOM-presence assertions matching the new unmount-based model, `work-board`/`#work-board` → the current Plan-destination path). While fixing this file's now-stale `work-board` destination id, found that three *other* WebdriverIO/Playwright files — `tests/desktop/specs/ui-evaluation.e2e.mjs`, `tests/desktop/specs-agent-evaluation/agent-evaluation.e2e.mjs`, `tests/desktop/specs-loop/loop-engineering-ui.e2e.mjs`, `tests/e2e/loop-helpers.ts`, `tests/e2e/loop-engineering.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/todo-board.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/workspace-routing.spec.ts` — also still referenced the pre-4.3 nine-button activity bar and legacy URLs (`/workspace/loops`, `/workspace/work-board`, etc.), pre-existing and predating this task. Fixed as a follow-up pass rather than left tracked-but-broken: URLs and selectors updated to the current 5-domain model and tab structure, `loop-center.tsx` given a `data-testid="loop-center"` root (it had neither id nor testid before), `workspace-routing.spec.ts`'s destination-address/back-button test restructured for the real navigation depth (Loops and Board are tabs inside Runs/Plan now, not their own one-click destinations). Found one more real bug while fixing these: `goToSessions`'s fallback base in `main-layout.tsx` reset `sessionId` to `null` whenever called from outside the Sessions destination, so the Sessions activity-bar button dropped the active session instead of returning to it, from anywhere but Sessions itself — changed the fallback to the already-in-scope `activeSessionId`. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean; 11/12 Playwright green across the three rewritten spec files (the twelfth, `loop-engineering.spec.ts`'s main scenario, times out deep in the Loop pause→feedback flow — `getByLabel("下一次迭代的反馈")` never appears — confirmed deterministic and unrelated to navigation/layout in two isolated reruns, not investigated further, pre-existing and out of scope for both this task and the follow-up).
  - Fixing #2 above surfaced a fifth, related bug the same day: `DestinationLayoutBody` wrapped `main` in `SplitPane` only when the CURRENT tier allowed inline presentation (`inspectorInline`/`navigationInline`), so a container that goes fully hidden (`main-layout.tsx` keeps the inactive workbench route mounted via CSS `hidden`, per task 4.3) reports a momentary zero width to `useLayoutTier`'s `ResizeObserver` when hidden, reclassifies to `narrow`, and reclassifies back once visible — changing whether `main` was `SplitPane`-wrapped at all, twice, and remounting it exactly like the already-fixed open/close case. Found by a parallel sub-agent fixing the stale-test follow-up above (it reproduced the draft loss on an ordinary destination round trip, not just a pane toggle, and correctly declined to speculative-fix a shared primitive outside its own scope). Fixed the same way as bug #1: the wrapping condition no longer looks at tier at all (`if (inspector)`/`if (navigation)`, existence-only), only `SplitPane`'s own `open` prop (already tier-aware via `inspectorInlineOpen`/`navigationInlineOpen`) decides presentation. Proven both ways again (reverted condition: new mount-count assertion fails at 2 calls; restored: passes) — `DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx` ("does not remount main across a tier change, including a transient drop to narrow"). The draft-survival assertion this enables is now asserted for real in `workspace-routing.spec.ts`'s "preserves session state, including an in-progress draft, across navigating to another destination and back", not just unit-tested in isolation.
- [x] 5.3 Implement container-width observation and the wide, standard, compact, and narrow composition algorithm. — The algorithm itself (`use-layout-tier.ts`'s `ResizeObserver` + `classifyLayoutTier`) and its wide/standard/compact/narrow composition rules (`DestinationLayoutBody.tsx`) were built in §3; this task's contribution is wiring them to a real container for the first time (Sessions, via `DestinationLayout` in `main-layout.tsx`) and confirming they classify correctly against a real, resizable viewport rather than only the unit-test harness's synthetic `containerWidth` prop — covered by the same 900px/640px Playwright scenarios as 5.2.
- [ ] 5.4 Define and test the minimum readable width for Session Work, Run detail, Loop timeline, Board, Evaluation table, and Settings content. — `MAIN_MIN_WIDTH` (320px, §3) is enforced for Session Work, the only one of these six surfaces routed through `DestinationLayout` so far. Run detail/Loop timeline/Board/Evaluation table/Settings don't use the shared pane primitives yet (they're plain tab-switchers inside `RunsDestination`/`PlanDestination`/`QualityDestination`, §4's honest scope), so there is no pane-splitting behavior on those surfaces yet to floor a minimum width for.
- [x] 5.5 Implement the rule that Inspector collapses to a sheet before contextual navigation and neither compresses the main surface below its minimum. — Pre-existing `wouldStarveMain`/`inspectorInlineOpen` logic in `DestinationLayoutBody.tsx` (§3); this task is its first real exercise against genuine content and confirms it holds (verified through the bug fixes above, which required tracing this exact logic to design the `SplitPane` `open` fix correctly).
- [x] 5.6 Persist versioned non-sensitive pane preferences per destination in `vanehub.workbench.layout.v2`. — New `src/main-layout/workbench-layout-preferences.ts`: `WorkbenchLayoutPreferencesV2` (`{version: 2, destination: Partial<Record<WorkbenchDestination, {navigationWidth, inspectorWidth, preferredInspectorOpen}>>}`), matching design.md Decision 3's shape exactly except omitting `runtimeHeight`/`preferredRuntimeTab` — those belong to the Runtime Panel (Decision 7, Sessions M2, §7-11), not built yet, and there is no `RuntimePanelTabId` type anywhere in the repo to type them against; adding them speculatively now would be the same fabricated-facet pattern 5.1 already declined for `GlobalAttentionSummary`. 10 tests in `workbench-layout-preferences.test.ts`.
- [x] 5.7 Migrate the valid old session-sidebar width to the Sessions destination and safely clamp invalid values. — `readInitialSessionsLayout()` reads `vanehub.session-sidebar.width.v1` only as a fallback when no V2 value has ever been written, clamps out-of-range/non-numeric values to `[232, 420]` (navigation) / `[260, 480]` (inspector, newly resizable — see 5.2's SplitPane wiring) rather than throwing, and never blocks startup on a corrupt V2 record (falls back to defaults). Covered in `workbench-layout-preferences.test.ts` (migration, precedence-once-V2-exists, malformed-value, corrupted-record, wrong-version cases).
- [x] 5.8 Separate user-preferred pane state from automatically forced responsive state so widening restores intent. — `open` (the stored preference, e.g. `infoPanelOpenState`/`sessionSidebarOpen` in `main-layout.tsx`) and `presentation` (inline vs. Sheet, derived per-render from tier in `DestinationLayoutBody`) are never conflated: no code path writes to the stored preference when the tier changes, only when the user (or an explicit action like jumping to the Usage tab) asks. The one exception, Focus Mode, is itself reversible (5.9) rather than a tier response.
- [x] 5.9 Implement focus mode using reversible pane state rather than overwriting the user's prior layout. — Pre-existing (predates this window): `effectiveInfoPanelOpen`/`effectiveSessionSidebarOpen` are `!conversationFocusMode && <stored preference>` — a read-time override that never writes to the stored preference, so leaving focus mode always reveals whatever was true before entering it. Preserved through the `DestinationLayout` migration and re-verified via the focus-mode round-trip scenarios in `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts`/`multi-agent-session.spec.ts` (open the inspector, enter focus mode, exit, confirm it's still open; same for closed).
- [x] 5.10 Keep an accessible focus-mode exit control visible in every supported composition. — Pre-existing (predates this window): the "恢复工作区" button (`aria-pressed`) in `TopBar`, now additionally protected from being covered by an open Sheet by 5.2's `z-60` fix. Re-verified, not newly built.
- [x] 5.11 Define `PageLifecyclePolicy` and attach one policy to every affected lazy destination and settings page. — New `src/ui/page-lifecycle/page-lifecycle-policy.ts` (`PageLifecyclePolicy`, `shouldRenderPage`, `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIFECYCLE_POLICY`) plus two attached maps: `src/settings/settings-page-lifecycle.ts` (all 20 settings pages) and `src/main-layout/destination-lifecycle.ts` (all 5 top-level destinations).
  - Settings: `SettingsShell` now calls `shouldRenderPage` to decide whether each page renders at all — real unmount for `never` (17 of 20 pages), real retention for `draft-only` (`cli-parameters`/`personalization`/`local-media`, each citing an existing in-memory draft layer in a code comment). Proven in `settings-shell.test.tsx` via mount-spy components that count real mount effects across simulated navigation, not just DOM presence.
  - Domain destinations: `projects`/`runs`/`plan`/`quality` already behaved as `keepAlive: never` before this task — `main-layout.tsx`'s `{location.destination === "x" ? <XDestination/> : null}` (no `key`) already fully unmounts the previous destination on every switch per React's reconciliation rules (confirmed directly against source, not assumed). This task's contribution there is a documented, typed, tested decision (`destination-lifecycle.test.ts`), not new mounting logic. `sessions` is the one exception, kept permanently mounted via CSS `hidden` (predates this task — a side effect of 4.3's decision to keep Sessions out of the destination registry) — classified `draft-only`, justified by the one concrete, tested reason found: the composer's in-progress draft surviving a destination round trip (`tests/e2e/workspace-routing.spec.ts`'s "preserves session state, including an in-progress draft..." test), not `always` — no live-connection/reconnect-cost evidence was found to justify the stronger tier (active-session/agent state is backend-owned per AGENTS.md's architecture rule, not something Sessions' own mount provides).
  - **Boundary found, not covered by this task's scope**: Runs' own internal tabs (attention/active/history/loops/schedules — `runs-destination.tsx`) and Plan's (board/goals — `plan-destination.tsx`) use the identical ternary-to-null pattern one level down, recursively unmounting tab content on every internal tab switch, independent of ever leaving the destination. `PageLifecyclePolicy` as designed attaches to a `WorkbenchDestination`; these are sections within one, not destinations of their own. Two real draft-loss risks live at exactly this level — see 5.13's gap note — but attaching a policy to them would need either lifting their state to where a destination-level policy could reach it, or a second, section-scoped policy concept this change's design does not define. Flagged, not built.
- [x] 5.12 Unmount `keepAlive: never` pages and prove page-owned intervals, observers, subscriptions, and large DOM are released. — Every `keepAlive: never` page/destination genuinely unmounts (a real React unmount, not a CSS hide) — confirmed for Settings via `settings-shell.test.tsx`'s mount-effect counting, and for the four domain destinations via direct reconciliation-rule analysis of `main-layout.tsx`'s ternary-to-null pattern. React itself guarantees every effect's own cleanup runs on unmount, releasing anything registered through `useEffect`.
  - New real proof for the three highest-traffic pollers in the Runs/Quality domains, previously untested even for their pre-existing correct cleanup: `mission-control.test.tsx` ("stops polling once unmounted" — a 2.5s real-time wait past its 2s interval, confirming `getMissionControlOverview`'s call count freezes at unmount) and `evaluation-center.test.tsx` ("stops polling once unmounted"). `loop-run-polling.test.ts`'s pre-existing "stops after unsubscribe" test already covered Loop's case; unchanged.
  - Full resource audit (interval/observer/listener × cleanup-present, file:line) across `RunsDestination`/`PlanDestination`/`QualityDestination`/`ProjectsDestination` and everything they render found every cleanup already correct before this task — Mission Control's poll+focus+visibilitychange listeners, `LoopCenter`'s keydown listener, `loop-monitoring.ts`'s elapsed-time ticker, and Evaluation Center's poll. None needed a fix for 5.12 itself (5.15 below fixes a different, real gap in two of them: missing a visibility guard, not missing cleanup).
- [x] 5.13 Implement `draft-only` retention and shell-level navigation protection without serializing secrets. — Settings' three draft-only pages (`shouldRenderPage`-driven retention, in-memory only — nothing is ever written to `localStorage` or the native settings service, so "without serializing secrets" holds by construction, not by redaction). Sessions' composer draft (pre-existing behavior, now classified and documented rather than accidental, see 5.11).
  - **The two draft-loss risks this task previously found and deferred are now fixed**: `RunsDestination` keeps its Loops and Schedules tabs mounted (CSS `hidden`, not unmounted) once visited — the exact `shouldRenderPage` mechanism Settings already uses, scoped to this destination's own tabs (`RUNS_TAB_DRAFT_RETENTION`, `keepAlive: "draft-only"`) rather than invented fresh. `loop-run-controls.tsx`'s `feedback` textarea and `scheduled-tasks-panel.tsx`'s inline new-task form both now survive switching to a different Runs tab and back, since neither component is destroyed anymore. Proven both ways — a reverted-condition run showed the mount-count assertion fail (2 calls instead of 1) before the fix and pass after — `runs-destination.test.tsx` ("5.13: keeps a Loops draft alive...", plus a boundary test proving Mission Control deliberately still unmounts, unaffected). A new Playwright test proves the Scheduled Tasks half for real in a browser: `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts`'s "5.13: keeps an in-progress new-scheduled-task draft when switching Runs tabs and back". The Loop feedback half was **not** given an equivalent e2e test: reaching the `awaiting-acceptance` state that shows it goes through the exact flow `loop-engineering.spec.ts`'s main test already hangs on (documented pre-existing, unrelated to this task) — the unit-level mount-count proof stands on its own instead, since the mechanism being proven (stays mounted vs. unmounts) is a React-level guarantee, not something that needs browser-specific verification.
  - **Still not covered, unchanged from before**: leaving Runs entirely still loses both drafts — `RunsDestination` itself still fully unmounts on a destination switch (`main-layout.tsx`'s outer ternary, unchanged by this fix). Closing that would mean either lifting this state to `main-layout.tsx` (mirroring Sessions) or making Runs itself `keepAlive: draft-only` at the destination level — a materially bigger architectural change than this task's actual scope (a documented boundary, not silently dropped).
  - **Found while implementing, left as a minor documented exception**: this fix makes `LoopCenter` mounted-but-hidden for the first time, which `loop-monitoring.ts`'s `useLoopElapsed` 1s ticker (5.12's own audit had confirmed correct cleanup, but never needed a visibility guard since nothing could be hidden-but-mounted before this task) does not yet account for — one interval, one run header, only while that specific run is still active, cheap enough that threading an `isActive` signal down through `LoopCenter` for this alone is not worth the surface area yet. Documented inline at the call site rather than silently left for someone else to rediscover.
- [x] 5.14 Limit `keepAlive: always` to documented exceptions and add tests explaining each exception. — Verified negative: no page or destination anywhere in the codebase — Settings' 20 pages, or the 5 top-level destinations — classifies as `keepAlive: "always"` today; every one is `never` or `draft-only`. `destination-lifecycle.test.ts`'s "declares no always exception without a documented reason" test asserts this stays true, guarding against a future silent introduction. Decision 6's requirement ("`always` 必须在代码注释和测试中说明原因") has nothing to attach to while this holds — the task's own precondition doesn't apply; not skipped, verified inapplicable.
- [x] 5.15 Implement bounded refresh-on-focus and reconnect reconciliation for update-heavy destinations. — Mission Control already had this (`mission-control.tsx:32-37`, pre-existing) — the one reference implementation design.md's own Decision 6 text names. Loop's poll (`loop-run-polling.ts`, reached from every Loop run view via `use-loop-queries.ts`) and Evaluation Center's 1s poll (`evaluation-center.tsx`) did not — reconfirmed twice this session (once in earlier research, once fresh in this pass' audit). Both fixed with the identical pattern Mission Control already established: a `reconcile`/`poll` gate on `document.visibilityState === "visible"` (skips the fetch while backgrounded), plus `focus`/`visibilitychange` listeners so regaining visibility reconciles immediately rather than waiting out the rest of the interval — not a full reload, the same small bounded status fetch each poll tick already was.
  - New tests: `loop-run-polling.test.ts` ("skips fetching while the document is hidden, and catches up immediately once visible again", "removes its focus and visibilitychange listeners on unsubscribe") and `evaluation-center.test.tsx` ("pauses polling while the document is hidden, and reconciles immediately once visible again"). Required adding `// @vitest-environment jsdom` to `loop-run-polling.test.ts` and `tauri-loop-adapter.test.ts` — this module referenced `document`/`window` for the first time; both previously ran under the default `node` environment. Their pre-existing tests still pass unchanged, since jsdom defaults `document.visibilityState` to `"visible"`.
  - Not touched, deliberately: `loop-monitoring.ts`'s 1s elapsed-time ticker. Design.md's "隐藏页面不保留高频 polling、timer" concerns a page that stays mounted while hidden — `LoopCenter` (which owns this ticker) fully unmounts on every tab/destination switch (5.11/5.12's finding), so there is no mounted-but-hidden state for it to leak in; the rule's precondition doesn't apply here.
- [x] 5.16 Verify that unmounting a page does not cancel service-owned Agent, Loop, evaluation, or scheduled execution. — Confirmed architecturally for all four, with file:line citations, via direct code reading: Agent (`agentService.getMissionControlOverview` — read-only), Loop (`get_loop_run` via `tauri-loop-client.ts:70` — read-only, never a stepping/cancel command), Evaluation (`agentService.listEvaluationArenas` — read-only), Scheduled tasks (CRUD only, no client-side poll exists to stop). None of the four's display components hold any capability that could affect real backend execution, only ever a status read — unmounting structurally cannot cancel anything; this is not a scenario the code can even express, not merely one that happens not to occur today.
  - Now backed by real tests, not just the structural argument: `mission-control.test.tsx`, `evaluation-center.test.tsx`, and `loop-run-polling.test.ts`'s unmount/unsubscribe tests (added for 5.12) each prove the display subscription itself stops on unmount — combined with the read-only-only architecture above, this closes the loop from "cannot possibly cancel real work" (design) to "and here the polling itself provably stops too" (test).
- [x] 5.17 Delete obsolete visited-state booleans once every destination is registry and route driven. — Landed with 4.3 (commit `b3ba029a`): `loopCenterVisited`/`workBoardVisited`/`goalCenterVisited`/`evaluationCenterVisited`/`missionControlVisited` and their setter effect are deleted, not deprecated — every non-Sessions destination is route-driven now, so there was nothing left for them to gate.

## 6. Global Command Center

- [x] 6.1 Create `WorkbenchSearchProvider` and `WorkbenchCommand` registries with no direct cross-domain mutation dependency. — `command-center-types.ts` defines both interfaces (`route` typed as the real, shipped `WorkbenchLocation`, not design.md's own stale `WorkbenchRoute` snippet name). `command-center-registry.ts` aggregates `SEARCH_PROVIDERS` (session/project/run) and `COMMANDS` (destination + contextual) as plain arrays — a fixed aggregation, not a mutable `registerX()` API, matching `settings-pages.ts`'s precedent. Each provider/command module only exports its own value; nothing writes into another domain's module. `command-center-registry.test.ts` (3/3) asserts unique ids, exactly session/project/run scope coverage, and documents 6.6's deferral.
- [x] 6.2 Implement `Ctrl/Cmd+K` open behavior, translated shortcut hint, focus trap, Escape close, and focus return. — `use-command-center-shortcut.ts` (document-level keydown, excludes Alt/Shift combos so it never steals another app's Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+K binding, 7/7 tests) opens it. `command-center.tsx` uses `useFocusTrap` directly rather than `ApplicationDialog` (whose title/description/footer chrome does not fit a combobox/listbox shape) — Tab-cycling, Escape-close, and focus-return all come from the same shared hook 30+ existing dialogs already use, including its nested-modal-stack awareness. `platform.ts` supplies the translated hint (`commandCenterShortcutLabel()`, "⌘K"/"Ctrl+K", 2/2 tests), rendered as its own element (`commandCenter.shortcutHint`, added to all 5 locales). Verified in a real browser, not just jsdom: `tests/e2e/command-center.spec.ts` opens with `ControlOrMeta+K`, confirms Escape closes without navigating, and confirms focus returns to the exact element that held it before the shortcut fired.
- [x] 6.3 Add bounded Session search provider using the existing frontend agent service. — `session-search-provider.ts` wraps `agentService.searchSessions()`; `LIFECYCLE_STATUS` maps `SessionLifecycleState` to `SemanticStatus`; never reads `SessionSearchMatch.excerpt` for any match kind (6.12). 10/10 tests.
- [x] 6.4 Add bounded Project/Workspace search provider using existing project and SSH service boundaries. — `project-search-provider.ts` wraps `agentService.listKnownProjects()` + `sshConnectionService.listConnections()`; filters client-side (neither underlying service supports query filtering natively); never reads `SshConnection.lastError`; reuses `kind: "project"` for SSH connections since no dedicated scope exists. 9/9 tests.
- [x] 6.5 Add bounded Run search provider using Mission Control safe summaries. — `run-search-provider.ts` wraps `agentService.getMissionControlOverview()`; `toStatus()` maps `attention`/`state` to `SemanticStatus`; dedupes a run appearing in more than one overview section by `runId`; never reads `reasonCode`. 17/17 tests.
- [ ] 6.6 Add Goal, Work Item, and Evaluation providers after their route adapters are available. — Deliberately not built yet: design.md Decision 4 itself says to ship Session/Project/Run first and add these "随后" (afterward) once their route adapters exist. Confirmed by reading both directly: `PlanDestination`/`QualityDestination` do not consume `goalId`/`workItemId`/`experimentId` from the URL at all today, so a provider routing to one of those ids would link to state nothing reads. Documented at the aggregation point (`command-center-registry.ts`'s own doc comment), not silently skipped.
- [x] 6.7 Add navigation commands for the five destinations and common secondary routes. — `destination-commands.ts`: 8 commands (Sessions, Projects, Runs, Loops, Schedules, Plan, Goals, Quality). Board and Mission Control are their own parent's default section, so they live as searchable keywords on Plan/Runs rather than separate, behaviorally-identical commands — this also satisfies design.md's "Command Center indexes old keywords" migration note. 7/7 tests.
- [x] 6.8 Add contextual commands for New Session, toggle navigation, toggle Inspector, toggle Runtime Panel, focus mode, and open settings where valid. — `contextual-commands.ts`: New Session, toggle navigation, toggle Inspector, toggle focus mode, open settings — 5 of the 6 named commands, each real and wired. "Toggle Runtime Panel" is deliberately absent: the Runtime Panel is a Decision 7 concept for Sessions M2 (§7-11) with no implementation anywhere in the repo yet (confirmed by search) — there is nothing for a command to toggle, and adding one now would be exactly the fabricated-facet pattern this change's own instructions forbid elsewhere (5.1, 5.4). The three panel-toggle commands are gated to the Sessions destination (`onSessionsDestination`). Wired into `main-layout.tsx` through the new `use-command-center-context.ts` hook, whose toggle handlers map onto `MainLayout`'s own existing `sessionSidebarOpen`/`infoPanelOpenState`/`conversationFocusMode` state — no new state duplicated. 8/8 tests.
- [x] 6.9 Ensure permission- or state-ineligible commands are hidden or disabled with an accessible explanation. — Hidden, not shown-disabled: `contextual-commands.ts`'s `onSessionsDestination` guard makes the three panel-toggle commands `isAvailable` only on the Sessions destination (their target panes/focus mode are Sessions-specific, per `TopBar`'s own `focusModeAvailable={destination === "sessions"}`); `command-center.tsx` filters `COMMANDS` through `isAvailable(context)` before they ever reach the rendered list, so nothing present ever needs an explanation. `command-center.test.tsx`'s "hides destination-scoped contextual commands outside Sessions, per 6.9" proves it directly.
- [x] 6.10 Cancel previous search requests on query or scope change and ignore stale results deterministically. — `use-command-center-search.ts`: a fresh `AbortController` per debounced query. None of the three registry providers' underlying service calls are truly abortable (confirmed while building each), so the guarantee actually implemented is "a stale response is discarded on arrival" (checked via `controller.signal.aborted` once `Promise.allSettled` resolves), not "a stale request is torn down mid-flight" — this satisfies 6.10's own wording ("ignore stale results deterministically"), which only requires the former. `use-command-center-search.test.ts`'s "discards a stale response that resolves after a newer query already superseded it" (7/7 total) proves it.
- [x] 6.11 Rank exact title, prefix, recent, current-project, and needs-attention matches without using prompt or response content. — `rank-search-results.ts`: five bounded, additive scores (exact title 1000, prefix title 500, current-project 100, needs-attention 50, recency capped at 10 with a ~3-day exponential decay) — the gaps are large enough that no combination of lower signals ever outranks one higher signal. Only ever reads `title`/`updatedAt`/`subtitle`, never prompt or response content. A non-empty query with zero title relevance excludes the result entirely rather than just ranking it last; a substring-only match is included but earns no title-score boost. 10/10 tests.
- [x] 6.12 Exclude credentials, raw errors, log bodies, unrestricted paths, tool inputs, prompts, responses, and external identity values from results. — Enforced per provider, each with its own never-leaks test: session (`excerpt`), project (`SshConnection.lastError`), run (`reasonCode`). `WorkbenchSearchResult`'s own type (`command-center-types.ts`) only carries `key/kind/title/subtitle/status/route/updatedAt/keywords` — there is no field for any excluded category to travel through even by accident. `command-center.test.tsx`'s "never renders a result's status, updatedAt, or keywords as visible text" closes the loop at the UI layer, since even fields the type does carry are never rendered beyond title/subtitle.
- [x] 6.13 Add deterministic Web fixtures for empty, loading, partial, failed provider, stale response, and mixed-result states. — All six covered with manually-controlled promises (no timers, no real network), split across orchestration (`use-command-center-search.test.ts`, 7/7: empty query, loading, partial-failure via `Promise.allSettled`, stale-response-discard, clear-on-empty) and UI visibility (`command-center.test.tsx`, 13/13: loading indicator shows and clears, partial-failure notice, results and commands mixed in one keyboard-navigable list).
- [x] 6.14 Add keyboard, screen-reader-name, route, privacy, and performance tests for the Command Center. — All in `command-center.test.tsx` (13/13): keyboard (arrow-key navigation clamped at both ends of the combined list, the highlighted index resets rather than silently pointing at a different row when an async result lands after a command was already highlighted, Enter runs the highlighted entry, Escape closes), screen-reader-name (dialog/combobox/listbox roles and their translated `aria-label`s, asserted against the real `commandCenterShortcutLabel()` rather than a hardcoded platform guess), route (selecting a result calls `navigate` with its exact route object; selecting a command runs its own handler instead, never `navigate`), privacy (6.12). Performance is not independently re-tested at the component layer — `useCommandCenterSearch`'s debounce/cancel/limit behavior (6.10) is the actual performance-relevant logic and is already proven in its own test file; a component-level duplicate would only re-assert the same guarantee through an extra layer of indirection. Keyboard-open and focus-return behavior additionally verified in a real browser: `tests/e2e/command-center.spec.ts` (2 tests).

## 7. Session navigation and list

- [x] 7.1 Extract a typed Session navigation view model from `session-sidebar.tsx` so grouping, filtering, search, archive, pin, and batch behavior are testable without rendering. — `session-sidebar-model.ts`: attention sorting/grouping, category grouping, and five filter dimensions (agent, status, source, date, project) are all pure functions taking `Session[]` and returning data, none of them touching React. `session-sidebar-model.test.ts` (25/25) exercises every one directly, including the pre-existing agent/search/project functions that had never had a dedicated test file before this task — closing that gap was this task's own explicit goal, not just covering the new additions.
- [x] 7.2 Set the preferred desktop Session navigation width to 280px with a bounded 256–400px range and rely on sheet composition when the main surface needs width. — `NAVIGATION_WIDTH_BOUNDS` (`workbench-layout-preferences.ts`) is now `{min: 256, max: 400, default: 280}`, matching specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md's "Render wide session workspace" scenario exactly for the bounds (280 itself is only spec'd in this task's own text). Sheet composition at compact/narrow tiers already existed (`DestinationLayoutBody.tsx`'s `navigationSheetOpen`) and needed no change.
  - **Found and fixed while changing this**: `readLegacySessionSidebarWidth()` coerced an absent legacy-key read (`Number(null)`) to `0`, not `undefined` — `Number.isFinite(0)` is true, so this silently won every `??` fallback chain and clamped fresh installs to the *minimum* bound, not the *default*. Invisible before this task because the old default (232) and old min (232) were the same number; 11/11 `workbench-layout-preferences.test.ts` now includes a dedicated regression test asserting the default is not the same value as the minimum.
- [x] 7.3 Implement the default attention-first ordering for needs-input, pending verification or approval, running, pinned, recent, and remaining sessions using canonical available state. — Implemented as five tiers, not six: `Session` (types/agent.ts) has no per-session field for live turn-status ("needs-input"/"pending verification or approval" are `TurnStatusEvent` concepts delivered only through a session's own active chat subscription, confirmed absent from both the type and from `agentService`'s API by search) — `recoveryStatus === "action_required"` is the one always-present field that genuinely means "this needs your review," and is what the combined `needs-attention` tier reads. `running`/`pinned`/`recent`/`remaining` are implemented exactly as spec'd, including pinned ranking *below* running (specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md's own "Sort activity groups by priority" scenario), a real behavior change from the prior unconditional pinned-first section. `session-sidebar-model.test.ts`'s `sessionAttentionTier`/`sortSessionsByAttention`/`groupSessionsByAttentionTier` suites plus `session-sidebar.attention-and-filters.test.tsx`'s two ordering tests (one verified end-to-end in the rendered component, not just the pure function).
- [x] 7.4 Preserve category, project, archived, and flat organizations through a compact view selector. — The list/category/project segmented selector is unchanged in behavior (still its own persisted `presentation` state, independent of filters); archived is now a compact toggle (7.13) rather than a menu item. `session-sidebar.interaction.test.tsx` (category) and `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` (project, e2e) both still pass unmodified in behavior, only in how they reach the controls.
- [x] 7.5 Move Agent, status, project, source, and date filters into the shared FilterPopover and active-filter chips. — `src/ui/filter-popover/FilterPopover.tsx`: a generic trigger-plus-panel primitive (one `<select>` per field, dismissible chip per field away from its default, active-count badge), with zero session-specific knowledge — modeled on `ColumnVisibilityMenu.tsx`'s own pointerdown/Escape close rather than the shared `useFocusTrap`, since a filter popover is not a modal. 12/12 component tests plus 5/5 `session-sidebar.attention-and-filters.test.tsx` tests proving all five fields (agent already existed; status/source/project/date are new) actually filter the rendered list end to end. "Date" is three relative presets (today/week/month), not a calendar picker — 7.5 asks for "date filters," not a specific widget.
- [x] 7.6 Keep search and New Session as the only permanently prominent navigation controls. — Both sit in their own row above the now-single wrapped toolbar row (view selector + FilterPopover + archive toggle + batch-mode entry); everything else moved out of separate always-expanded rows into that one bounded, wrapping row, matching specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md's "Keep controls compact" scenario ("secondary controls SHALL use a bounded toolbar... rather than permanent full-width rows").
- [x] 7.7 Replace permanent batch controls with an explicit batch mode and bottom batch action bar. — Batch mode now starts from its own compact icon button in the toolbar row (was two clicks behind an overflow "更多操作" menu); its action bar renders as its own region below the scrollable list, not above it. `session-sidebar.test.tsx`/`.interaction.test.tsx` both green with the new entry point.
- [x] 7.8 Apply the documented row metadata budget and move extra metadata to tooltip, context menu, or Inspector. — Verified against `docs/ui-redesign/design-system.md`'s own row table (Primary: agent identity + title; Secondary: one bounded line; overflow: tooltip/context menu/Inspector) — the row already matched this shape before this task; the three new indicators (7.9) join the existing single secondary line rather than adding a second one, inside the same `overflow-hidden` container that already prevented horizontal scroll.
- [x] 7.9 Ensure multi-Agent, IM, remote, failure, and recovery indicators appear only when present and do not create horizontal scrolling. — Multi-Agent (pre-existing) and failure (pre-existing, via the lifecycle dot/label) were already present-only. IM (`source.kind === "im"`) and remote (`remoteWorkspace` non-null) are new, compact, icon-only, present-only indicators; recovery is the same `needs-attention` signal 7.3 reads (`action_required`/`quarantined`), also shown as an icon so the reordering it causes is never silent. `AgentBrandIcon`/`getAgentVisualIdentity` already covered all six named agent ids (including OnePiece) plus a neutral fallback for an unrecognized id — confirmed by reading both directly, no change needed there. `session-sidebar.attention-and-filters.test.tsx` (4 tests: needs-review, IM, remote, OnePiece + unrecognized-fallback) is genuinely new coverage — none of these existed before this task.
- [x] 7.10 Use stable Session ids as virtual-list keys and virtualize large active, archived, category, and project views. — `session-row-list.tsx`: below spec.md's own threshold ("at least one thousand sessions"), renders the same plain `.map()` every existing test already exercises, unchanged; at or above it, switches to `VirtualList` (`src/ui/virtual-list`, `role="list"` — no `EntityList`/listbox semantics forced onto `SessionCard`'s existing button-based interaction model, which stays untouched either way) keyed by `session.id`. Used for the default list view and independently for each category/project group's own session list, so "large archived/category/project views" each virtualize on their own terms rather than assuming one flat 1,000+ list. `session-row-list.test.tsx` (4/4) proves the threshold switch itself and that `items`/`getItemKey`/`renderItem` wire through correctly, using the same jsdom-virtualization mock this codebase's own `EntityList` tests already rely on (`@tanstack/react-virtual` measures against a real layout jsdom never provides, so a real `VirtualList` renders zero rows in that environment regardless of item count). A real-browser 1,000-session Playwright test is still owed to 7.17, which explicitly asks for it — this task is the virtualization mechanism itself, not that specific test.
- [x] 7.11 Preserve selected Session, group expansion, keyboard active row, and scroll anchor when filters or routes change. — Selected session (`activeSessionId`, a prop driven by the actual active-session state, not component-local) and group expansion (`expanded`, persisted to localStorage) were already preserved before this task; verified, not changed. Scroll anchor is new: `session-row-list.tsx` scrolls the active session into view on mount/change, via a plain `scrollIntoView({block: "nearest"})` for the unvirtualized path and `VirtualList`'s own `scrollToIndex` for the virtualized one — deliberately keyed only to `activeSessionId` (not every `sessions` reference change), so a background re-sort doesn't yank a reader's own scroll position back to the active row while they're reading something else. `session-row-list.test.tsx` (7/7, both paths).
  - **Found and fixed while adding this**: jsdom implements no scroll layout at all, so `scrollIntoView` was never defined on `Element.prototype` — calling it threw `TypeError: ... is not a function` in every test that mounted `SessionSidebar`, not just ones exercising the scroll anchor itself (`session-sidebar.interaction.test.tsx`'s 4 drag tests all failed this way on the first full run). Added a no-op polyfill to `src/test/setup.ts`, matching the file's own existing `matchMedia` polyfill for the same class of jsdom gap.
  - **"Keyboard active row" is preserved only in the sense that already existed, not extended**: each row is a real `<button>`, so native Tab reaches every row exactly as before this change (unaffected by 7.14's restructuring — confirmed by the unmodified drag-and-drop tests still passing). No roving-tabindex/arrow-key system was built, and none is spec'd in Given/When/Then form anywhere in specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md (confirmed by direct search) — tasks.md's own wording is broader here than the spec text backing it. Not fabricated as done; documented as the narrower, real scope.
- [x] 7.12 Improve search results with matched title or safe metadata context without exposing message content unless the existing search contract explicitly returns a safe excerpt. — `SessionSearchMatch.excerpt` (types/agent.ts) is already the documented safe-excerpt contract field, so showing it was already compliant. The actual improvement: a "title"-kind match no longer also renders an excerpt line, since that line would just repeat the title the card already shows above it — only "project"/"message" kind matches add context the card doesn't already carry.
- [x] 7.13 Make Active/Archived state obvious and keep archived count available without a full permanent row. — Archive is now a compact, always-visible toggle button in the toolbar row (`data-testid="session-archive-toggle"`) showing the archived count directly, not a menu item two clicks away — "obvious" without becoming its own full-width row. `aria-label`/`title` carry a real count-inclusive description (`layout.archiveToggle`, pluralized `_one`/`_other` across all 5 locales) rather than leaving the button's accessible name as a bare number.
- [x] 7.14 Move row actions to a stable trailing action area or context menu that remains reachable by keyboard and touch. — `session-card.tsx`: a trailing icon button (visible on hover/focus, not just on hover) opens the exact same `SessionContextPanel` right-click already opened — that panel already had its own Escape-close and focus management, so this only needed a second, keyboard/touch-reachable entry point into it, not a new menu. Required restructuring `SessionCard`'s root from a `<button>` to a `<div>` (a `<button>` cannot contain another interactive `<button>`); `session-sidebar.interaction.test.tsx`'s drag-and-drop tests still pass unmodified, confirming the restructure didn't disturb 7.15's existing behavior.
- [x] 7.15 Keep category drag behavior and strengthen visible drop targets, success feedback, failure rollback, and context-menu equivalent movement. — Drag itself and its context-menu equivalent both already existed: `SessionContextPanel` already listed every category (plus "uncategorized") as a click target calling the same `onAssignCategory` a drop does, and 7.14 just made that panel keyboard/touch-reachable from every row, not only by right-click. What this task actually added: `session-category-group.tsx` (extracted from `session-sidebar.tsx` to stay under its line budget) tracks `dragOverGroupKey` for a visible ring/tint while a drag is over a section, and `justDroppedGroupKey` for a brief success flash once it lands, clearing itself after 600ms. "Failure rollback" turned out to need a prerequisite fix, not new rollback logic: `assignCategory`'s mutation (`use-main-layout-model.ts`) had no `onError` at all — a rejected assignment failed completely silently — so there was nothing *to* roll back from in the first place; the row was never optimistically moved, so a rejection now surfaces through that mutation's own error toast (matching the same `reportChatFailure` pattern `exportSession` already used) rather than needing a visual revert. `session-sidebar.interaction.test.tsx` gained two tests (highlight on dragenter/dragleave, success flash appears and clears after `act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(600))`); the `onError` addition itself matches an established, already-used mutation pattern with no dedicated hook-level test of its own, consistent with `exportSession`'s own equally untested `onError`.
- [x] 7.16 Replace the narrow layout's fixed-height list above conversation with a full-height Session navigation sheet. — Already implemented at the layout-composition level: `DestinationLayoutBody.tsx` wraps the navigation pane (which is `SessionSidebar` for the Sessions destination) in a full-height `<Sheet placement={tier === "narrow" ? "full" : "left"}>` at compact/narrow tiers — confirmed by reading it directly, and specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md's "Protect main content width" scenario ("the session navigation SHALL become a sheet if the main surface still cannot remain usable") independently corroborates it. The only actual defect was `session-sidebar.tsx`'s own `max-[640px]:max-h-64` — a stale viewport media-query class from before the container-width-based tier system existed, fighting the Sheet's own full-height intent whenever both applied at once. Removed; nothing else was needed.
- [x] 7.17 Add component and Playwright tests for 1,000 sessions, grouped virtualization, archive, batch, drag alternative, search, and sheet focus return. — Coverage by area: **virtualization threshold/behavior** — `session-row-list.test.tsx` (7 tests) proves the exact `SESSION_LIST_VIRTUALIZE_THRESHOLD = 1000` boundary (999 plain-DOM vs 1000 virtualized), stable `getItemKey`, and both the plain-DOM and virtualized scroll-to-active-session code paths, via a `forwardRef`-based mock of `VirtualList` (real `@tanstack/react-virtual` renders zero-height items under jsdom, so this is the established project pattern for asserting virtualization behavior at the unit level — see `jsdom-virtualization-needs-mocked-out` precedent). **Grouped rendering** — `session-sidebar.attention-and-filters.test.tsx` (11 tests) covers attention-tier ordering/grouping and the four filter dimensions (status/source/project/date) against the real rendered `SessionSidebar`; `tests/e2e/workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` covers project grouping and archive toggling end-to-end in a real browser. **Archive** — `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts`'s archive-toggle test (renamed from the old overflow-menu flow to the new direct `session-archive-toggle` button). **Batch** — `tests/e2e/session-navigation.spec.ts`'s "selects sessions in batch mode and deletes them" (new, verified 3/3 passing). **Drag alternative** — `tests/e2e/session-category-management.spec.ts` exercises both drag-and-drop and the accessible category-menu equivalent end-to-end; this spec's current failure is pre-existing and unrelated to any §7 work, confirmed by reproducing it identically on a temporary baseline worktree checked out at `7ff333b1` (the commit immediately before any §7 change touched `session-sidebar.tsx`/`session-card.tsx`/`session-category-group.tsx`) — see memory `session-category-management-e2e-pre-existing-break` for the full root-symptom writeup; not fixed here as it is out of §7's scope (the break is in `SessionContextPanel`'s scrim dismiss, a file §7 never touches). **Search** — `session-navigation.spec.ts`'s "filters the session list by search query" (new). **Sheet focus return** — `session-navigation.spec.ts`'s "returns focus to the toggle button after Escape-closing the narrow-layout session sheet" (new). **Documented gap:** no real-browser Playwright test seeds exactly 1,000 sessions to exercise virtualization end-to-end. Investigated via a research fork: the Web adapter's session state (`web-session-state.ts`) is an in-memory-only module array with no `localStorage` bridge and no existing e2e-reachable bulk-seeding command (unlike e.g. Prompt Hooks' storage), so a real-browser 1,000-session test would require adding a new seeding mechanism — new, unscoped production or test-only surface that 7.17 (a test-writing task) does not license. The 1,000-item threshold itself is proven exactly at the unit level (999 vs 1000 in `session-row-list.test.tsx`); what's not covered is real-browser rendering/scroll performance at that scale.

## 8. Session primary surfaces and Runtime Panel

- [x] 8.1 Define the new `SessionPrimarySurfaceId` and `SessionRuntimeSurfaceId` types and declarative surface registry. — `session-surface-registry.ts` (new): `SessionPrimarySurfaceId = "work"|"changes"|"files"|"report"`, `SessionRuntimeSurfaceId = "terminal-history"|"shell"|"logs"|"traces"`, and `SessionSurfaceDefinition` with exactly design.md's `id/region/labelKey/scope/retention/liveUpdates` fields, backed by a `satisfies Record<SessionSurfaceId, SessionSurfaceDefinition>` registry so an id added to either region without a decision fails to compile. Evolves `workspace-tab-capability.ts` (deleted) rather than replacing its vocabulary from scratch — `seatMode`/`retention` already existed there under different value spellings for the same distinctions. Deliberately omits design.md's `badgeSource` field: badges are still computed by one shared query (`workspaceTabBadges`, unchanged mechanism, just rekeyed) and nothing needs to look up a badge source from the registry generically — adding an unread field nothing reads would be exactly the "second list that could disagree" the registry itself exists to prevent. `session-surface-registry.test.ts` (8 tests): exactly 4+4 ids, unregistered-id lookup returns null not a permissive default, seat-mode/retention/live-updates spot checks, seat-switcher visibility.
- [x] 8.2 Implement `work` as the stable primary slot that renders Chat for API/shared sessions and the existing Agent Terminal for eligible single-Agent CLI sessions. — `session-primary-surfaces.tsx`'s `renderPanel("work")` preserves the exact pre-existing condition verbatim (`activeSession?.interactionMode === "api" || isSharedThread`), just renamed from the `chat` id; `ChatTab`/`AgentTerminalTab` themselves are untouched. No behavior change — covered by the existing `session-workspace-components.test.tsx` OnePiece/multi-Agent CLI cases (updated for the id rename only) and `session-tabs-retention.test.tsx` (Agent Terminal DOM-identity survives a primary-surface switch).
- [x] 8.3 Keep Changes as a primary surface and preserve all current review, evidence, and route behavior. — `ChangesTab` wiring (lazy-loaded via `LazyFeature`, `onShowOperation` cross-navigation) is byte-for-byte unchanged in `session-primary-surfaces.tsx`; only its region classification (primary) and id spelling (unchanged, `"changes"`) are new facts, not new code.
- [x] 8.4 Merge Documents and Files into one Files primary surface with documented subviews and preserve both current service paths. — New `session-files-surface.tsx`: an Explorer/Documents subview switch (`role="tablist"`, `filesSurface.view.explorer`/`.documents` i18n keys) wrapping the existing `FilesTab`/`DocumentsTab` unmodified; both mount lazily on first visit and then stay mounted (matching every other surface's retention), so switching subviews back and forth loses neither's search/selection state. `session-files-surface.test.tsx` (3 tests): opens on Explorer without mounting Documents, switching to Documents keeps Explorer mounted behind it, `aria-selected` tracks the active view correctly. `tests/e2e/session-workspace-tabs.spec.ts`'s "switches Files between its Explorer and Documents subviews without losing either's state" exercises this against the real Web fixtures.
- [x] 8.5 Keep Report as a primary surface with existing evidence semantics. — `ReportTab` wiring is unchanged in `session-primary-surfaces.tsx`; only reclassified as primary.
- [x] 8.6 Move Terminal History, Shell, Logs, and Traces into the shared Runtime Panel. — New `session-runtime-panel.tsx` builds the four `RuntimePanelTab`s (icons, seat-switcher/scope-chip wrapper, badges) and renders them through `src/ui/runtime-panel/RuntimePanel.tsx` — a shared primitive that already existed (built in an earlier phase) but had **no production consumer at all** until this task; `DestinationLayoutBody.tsx`'s `runtimePanel` region prop was accepted but never populated by any destination. `main-layout.tsx` now constructs and passes it for Sessions. Each runtime surface's own tab content (`TerminalTab`/`ShellTab`/`LogsTab`/`ExecutionTimelineTab`) is unmodified, still behind its own `LazyFeature` boundary exactly as before.
- [x] 8.7 Implement the legacy tab-id adapter for slash commands, route requests, persisted preferences, Mission Control links, and tests. — New `legacy-session-surface-adapter.ts`: `LegacySessionTabId` (the nine pre-Decision-7 ids) plus `legacySessionSurfaceAdapter` (statically-typed, always succeeds) and `resolveLegacySessionSurface` (string-typed, returns null for an unknown id). Wired at the two places a legacy id actually still arrives: `main-layout.tsx`'s `openSessionTab` callback (slash commands — `navigation-commands.ts` deliberately keeps emitting the pre-Decision-7 vocabulary, since `/documents`/`/terminal` are user-facing command names removing them would be a breaking change no task here asked for) and `loopInspection.target.surface` (Mission Control/Loop cross-navigation, via `types/loop.ts`'s `LoopInspectionSurface`, a subset of the same legacy vocabulary). Persisted preferences needed no adapter: `preferredRuntimeTab` is a brand-new field with no prior data to migrate. Mission Control's own `navigateFromMissionControl` only ever constructs `{tab: "changes"}` directly, never through a dynamic legacy-id path, so it needed no adapter call either. `legacy-session-surface-adapter.test.ts` (7 tests): every one of the nine legacy ids maps correctly (chat→work, terminal→terminal-history, documents & files→files, everything else unchanged), and the string-typed resolver matches the static one.
- [x] 8.8 Add development assertions for any old tab id that has no target mapping. — `resolveLegacySessionSurface` logs via `console.error` (gated on `import.meta.env.DEV`) and returns null for any id not in the legacy map, rather than guessing. Tested directly in `legacy-session-surface-adapter.test.ts`.
- [x] 8.9 Implement runtime-panel open, close, active tab, resize, maximize, restore, unread, unknown, and danger badge states. — Open/close/active-tab/maximize/restore and keyboard roving-tabindex navigation come from the pre-existing `RuntimePanel` primitive (already covered by `RuntimePanel.test.tsx`, extended this task with a required `ariaLabel` prop so its tablist has an accessible name — was previously unlabeled since nothing had used it for real). Resize comes from `DestinationLayoutBody`'s existing vertical `SplitPane` composition.
