New: since the nine-tab bar that used to make Shell/Logs/Traces/Terminal History directly clickable is gone, nothing let a reader open the Runtime Panel without already being sent there by a slash command or evidence link — added a discoverable open trigger button (`session-primary-surfaces.tsx`, `PanelBottomOpen` icon, visible only while closed) matching design.md's own "Open runtime evidence" scenario, which explicitly lists "panel tab" among the ways a reader opens a runtime surface. `session-runtime-panel.test.tsx` (8 tests) and `session-runtime-panel-trigger.test.tsx` (3 tests) cover the tab strip, badges, and the open/close trigger cycle; `tests/e2e/session-workspace-tabs.spec.ts` exercises all of it against a real browser.
- [x] 8.10 Persist non-sensitive Runtime Panel height and preferred tab per Session destination without storing output content. — `workbench-layout-preferences.ts`: new `RUNTIME_HEIGHT_BOUNDS` (160–640px, 260 default — no spec'd figure exists yet, chosen the same way `INSPECTOR_WIDTH_BOUNDS` was), `runtimeHeight`/`preferredRuntimeTab` added to `DestinationLayoutPreference` and `readInitialSessionsLayout`'s return shape, following the exact established `localStorage`-backed pattern (not the native settings service — this file already treats cosmetic pane geometry as explicitly out of that rule's scope, per its sibling task 5.6/5.13). Height persists on resize-commit (`commitRuntimeHeight`, mirroring `commitInfoPanelWidth`). Preferred tab: `initialWorkspaceEvidenceState` now accepts an optional initial runtime surface (used only for the reducer's very first construction, the same way `navigationWidth`/`inspectorWidth` are read once at mount — a session switch still resets to the registry default, since this is a workbench-level preference like the others, not something that should chase the reader from session to session), and a new `usePersistPreferredRuntimeTab` hook writes it back on every change. `workbench-layout-preferences.test.ts` gained 4 tests for the new fields.
- [x] 8.11 Apply registry seat scope to every surface and include validated seat id in query keys when required. — Seat scope enforcement (`tab-scope.ts`'s `tabScope`/`effectiveSeatId`, unchanged mechanism, repointed at the new registry) and query-key seat threading (`evidenceQueryKeys`'s `seatId ?? null` idiom, `use-session-shells.ts`, `use-session-logs.ts`) were already correct pre-existing infrastructure; this task's job was verifying they still hold for the new id set, which `tab-scope.test.ts` (rewritten for the new ids) and `session-runtime-panel.test.tsx` (seat switcher visibility, `seatId` threaded into each runtime tab's `LazyFeature componentProps`) confirm.
- [x] 8.12 Ensure All seats is the truthful default for seat-optional evidence unless a validated route requests a concrete seat. — `effectiveSeatId` returns `null` ("all seats") until a concrete index is selected, unchanged; `session-tabs.tsx`'s `selectedSeat` state resets to `null` on every session switch (pre-existing behavior, still true after the reducer split). No regression — confirmed by `session-runtime-panel.test.tsx`'s single-seat and multi-seat-no-selection cases.
- [x] 8.13 Require an active concrete seat before a seat-required Shell attach or create request. — Genuine gap closed, not pre-existing: despite `shell`'s registry scope already being `seat-required`, nothing previously blocked `ShellTab` from attaching with `seatId=null` in a multi-seat session. New `ShellSeatGate` in `session-runtime-panel.tsx` renders a seat picker instead of `ShellTab` whenever a multi-seat session has no concrete seat selected (`seats.length > 1 && effectiveSeatId("shell", ...) === null`); a single-seat session is unaffected, since `effectiveSeatId` always resolves null there regardless (no ambiguity to gate). `session-runtime-panel.test.tsx`: gate shows for multi-seat/no-selection, clicking a seat calls through to select it and attach, gate is absent once a seat is chosen and for single-seat sessions.
- [x] 8.14 Implement per-surface retention and `isVisible` behavior; do not rely solely on `display:none` to suspend work. — Primary surfaces: unchanged `mountedTabs`/`isVisible` mechanism (`use-mounted-workspace-tabs.ts`, narrowed to the 4 primary ids), every panel still gates its own polling/subscriptions on the `isVisible` prop it already received. Runtime surfaces: `RuntimePanel`'s own `isVisible` prop into each `render(isVisible)` call, pre-existing and unchanged.
- [x] 8.15 Preserve running terminal ownership according to the existing runtime contract when the panel closes or the UI unmounts. — Unchanged at the service-ownership level: `ShellTab`/`AgentTerminalTab`'s documented contract ("the native shell is not the view... the process, its scrollback, and its working directory stay exactly as they were") is a backend/native-runtime guarantee independent of frontend mount state, untouched by this section. Frontend-local scrollback (e.g. Logs' unsubmitted search text) does *not* survive a full Runtime Panel close+reopen cycle — `DestinationLayoutBody`'s `runtimePanel` region fully unmounts its `content` when closed (`if (!runtimePanel) return main;`), same as it always has for any consumer; switching *between* runtime tabs while the panel stays open does preserve state, per `RuntimePanel`'s own tested `everOpenedRef` mechanism. `tests/e2e/session-workspace-tabs.spec.ts`'s "keeps primary and Runtime Panel state independent while switching either" exercises the within-panel-open retention path against a real browser.
- [x] 8.16 Prevent evidence from the previous Session from rendering under a newly selected Session while reconciliation occurs. — The render-phase (not effect-phase) session-switch reset in `use-mounted-workspace-tabs.ts` and `workspaceEvidenceReducer`'s `select-session` case are both pre-existing mechanisms, confirmed still correct for the split primary/runtime state by `workspace-session-reset.test.tsx` (rewritten for the new shape: primary surface, mounted set, *and* Runtime Panel open/close all reset together, same render, no frame ever pairs a new session with the previous one's filters) and the e2e "resets mounted primary tabs and closes the Runtime Panel when selecting another session" case.
- [x] 8.17 Place badge queries above individual surfaces so opening multiple surfaces does not multiply summary subscriptions. — Already true before this task and preserved: `useSessionWorkspaceRegions` calls `useWorkspaceEvidenceSummary`/`useWorkspaceEvidenceNotices` exactly once per session, computes `badges` once via `workspaceTabBadges`, and passes the same object down into both the primary tab bar and the Runtime Panel's tabs — one query regardless of how many surfaces (primary or runtime) are open.
- [x] 8.18 Guarantee primary tabs do not require horizontal scrolling at supported widths; move low-frequency optional commands to More if needed. — The primary tab bar's existing `overflow-x-auto`/`ucd-scroll-strip` fallback is untouched, and going from 9 tabs to 4 makes overflow at any previously-supported width implausible; no "More" menu was needed since nothing was demoted out of the primary bar (only moved to the Runtime Panel, which has its own independent strip). `tests/e2e/session-workspace-tabs.spec.ts`'s theme/viewport matrix asserts `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= window.innerWidth` at both 1440px and 390px after exercising every primary and runtime tab.
- [x] 8.19 Update slash-command help and UI labels for Files subviews and Runtime Panel destinations. — All 5 locale files (`en`/`ja`/`ko`/`zh-CN`/`zh-TW`) updated: `sessionTabs.tab.work` (renamed from `.chat`), `sessionTabs.tab.terminal-history` (renamed from `.terminal`, and fixed a camelCase-vs-literal-id key mismatch this task's own testing caught — `terminalHistory` vs the code's actual `terminal-history` interpolation, which would have shipped the Terminal History tab showing its raw i18n key), `sessionTabs.tab.documents` removed, three new `filesSurface.*` keys, `layout.runtimePanel`, `session.shellSeatGate.prompt`.
- [x] 8.20 Add unit, component, route, Playwright, and Tauri tests for every old-to-new mapping and each panel lifecycle. — Unit/component: 8 new/rewritten test files in `src/session-workspace/` (registry, legacy adapter, runtime panel + trigger, files surface) plus updates to every existing test touching the old 9-id vocabulary (`session-tab-bar.test.tsx`, `session-tabs-retention.test.tsx`, `session-tabs-slash-tab-reset.test.tsx`, `session-workspace-components.test.tsx`, `workspace-evidence-reducer.test.ts`, `workspace-evidence-scope.test.tsx`, `workspace-cross-panel-navigation.test.tsx`, `workspace-session-reset.test.tsx`, `report-tab.test.tsx`, `console-stable-dimensions.test.tsx`, `frontend-lazy-loading.test.ts`) plus a new `DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx` regression test for the remount bug found and fixed this task (verified it actually fails against the bug and passes against the fix, not just written and assumed correct). Contract: `workspaceEvidenceTabIdSchema` (zod) and the Rust-side `report/service.rs` evidence-link fixture both updated for the id rename; a pre-existing Rust test (`report_command_tests.rs`) reads the TS contract file directly and would have caught the mismatch either way. Playwright: `tests/e2e/session-workspace-tabs.spec.ts` fully rewritten for the 4+4 surface split (9/11 passing; the remaining 2 narrow-viewport failures are a pre-existing session-sidebar Sheet scrim bug, confirmed unrelated via a temporary baseline worktree at commit `1015922e` reproducing the identical failure on the unmodified original spec — not fixed here, out of this task's scope). Tauri: no Tauri-side changes were needed for this task beyond the one Rust literal-string fix already covered above (evidence-link tab names are opaque strings to the native side, not a native contract surface). `npm run test` (3122 tests) and `cargo test --workspace` (5863 tests) both pass; repeated full-suite runs surfaced a handful of unrelated failures each time in files this task never touched (`command-center.test.tsx`, `trace-linked-evidence.test.tsx`, `settings-shell.test.tsx`, and a `code_intelligence` LSP-timeout Rust test) — each verified green in isolation, consistent with this repository's established load-sensitive-full-suite pattern rather than a regression. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint:ci`, `npm run architecture:check`, and `openspec validate --specs --strict` all pass.

## 9. Contextual Inspector

- [x] 9.1 Create a typed `WorkbenchSelection` union for Session, Message, Tool, File, Change, Run, Loop Iteration, and Evaluation Result selections. — `src/types/workbench-selection.ts` (new): the 8-kind discriminated union verbatim from design.md Decision 8, plus `workbenchSelectionKey()` (stable dedupe/identity string, consumed by later tasks) and `WorkbenchSelectionKind`. Uses `pathId` (not a filesystem path) for `file`/`change` per the design doc's own wording. `workbench-selection.test.ts` (new, 10 tests). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean.
- [x] 9.2 Validate every selection against its owning route and stable identity before querying details. — `isSelectionInScope(selection, scope)` in `workbench-selection.ts`: a synchronous, no-I/O check of the *one* thing checkable without a provider round-trip — does this selection's `sessionId` (where it has one) match the session currently displayed. `session`/`message`/`tool`/`change` and session-scoped `file` selections go stale on a session switch; project-scoped `file` and the session-independent `run`/`loop-iteration`/`evaluation-result` kinds never do. `useWorkbenchInspection` (`src/ui/inspector/use-workbench-inspection.tsx`) applies this *before* a provider is ever looked up or invoked — an out-of-scope selection short-circuits straight to an `unavailable` detail state, satisfying "before querying details" literally. The other half named by this task's own wording — does the object still exist, is it permitted — is inherently a per-kind, asynchronous, owning-service question a generic scope check cannot answer; that half lives in each provider's own `AsyncViewState.error` (task 9.8), one layer below this check, not duplicated here. 10 tests in `workbench-selection.test.ts` plus 3 scope-invalidation tests in `use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx`.
- [x] 9.3 Create an Inspector provider registry keyed by selection kind; providers SHALL use owning frontend services only. — `src/ui/inspector/inspector-provider-registry.ts` (new): `INSPECTOR_PROVIDERS`, a plain `{ [K in WorkbenchSelectionKind]?: InspectorProvider<K> }` list mirroring `settings-pages.ts`'s directly-authored `SettingsPageDefinition` list, not a mutable registration API — one place to read what a kind resolves to. Each provider's `loader: LazyFeatureLoader<...>` mirrors `SettingsPageDefinition.loader`, lazy-loading per kind (design.md: "详情 Provider 按 kind lazy load") rather than Command Center's eager array, since a reader selects at most one kind at a time instead of searching across all of them on every keystroke. `getInspectorProvider(kind)` returns `undefined` for an unregistered kind rather than throwing — `useWorkbenchInspection` treats that the same as a deleted object (`unavailable`), which is correct, not a placeholder. No kinds are registered yet in this task; entries are added as each kind's real detail content ships (see 9.4/9.5 for `session`). `inspector-provider-registry.test.ts` (new, 3 tests). `use-workbench-inspection.tsx` (new): the mode/pin/follow/scope-invalidation state machine consuming the registry, returning `detail: AsyncViewState<ReactNode>` ready for `Inspector`'s own prop — resolves a registered provider's content via `<LazyFeature loader={provider.loader} componentProps={{selection}} key={selection.kind} />`, `key`ed deliberately: `LazyFeature`'s own `useState(() => lazy(loader))` initializer only runs once, so a `loader` prop that changes across renders without a matching `key` would silently keep rendering the *first* provider ever selected — caught and regression-tested (`use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx`, 11 tests, including one asserting the rendered element's `key` matches the new selection's kind). 9 new i18n keys (`workbenchUi.inspector.overviewTitle`, `.title.<kind>` × 8) added to all 5 locale files; `i18n-resource-parity.test.ts` and `i18n-visible-text-guardrail.test.ts` both pass. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on all new files.
- [x] 9.4 Implement Session Overview sections for Participants, Runtime, Usage, Skills, Workspace, IM, and Code Index. — `src/main-layout/session-overview.tsx` (the registered `session`-kind provider: resolves `selection.sessionId` against the shared `["sessions"]` query — same key `session-sidebar.tsx` and friends already use, so this shares React Query's cache rather than issuing a second request — and wraps the result in `AsyncBoundary`) + `session-overview-sections.tsx` (the 7-section `Accordion` composition) + `session-overview-runtime-workspace.tsx` (Runtime/Workspace section content, split out of the old merged "Basic Info" tab). A new, generic, reusable `Accordion` primitive (`src/ui/accordion/Accordion.tsx`) backs this — WAI-ARIA accordion pattern (`<button aria-expanded aria-controls>` header + `aria-labelledby` content region), multiple sections independently open, content always mounted and hidden via the native `hidden` attribute rather than lazily mounted on first open (matches every migrated pane's own existing `active`-gated internal behavior). Built by a delegated sub-agent from a precise brief (registry contract, exact prop signatures, the `listSessions()` cache-sharing rationale, the archived-session limitation) and reviewed/verified independently before integration (tsc, eslint, and its own 6 test files — 66 tests — all re-run and confirmed passing, not just trusted from its report). 8 new i18n keys added to all 5 locales.
- [x] 9.5 Migrate existing information-panel queries and actions into overview sections without silently deleting capability. — All 6 existing pane components (`SessionRosterEditor`/`SessionTokenUsagePane`/`SessionSkillsPane`/`SessionImPane`/`SessionCodeIndexPane`/`SessionEvidenceSummary`) reused unchanged; Participants and Code Index render under the identical conditions the old panel used (`seatsFromSession(session).length > 1`; `agentId === "onepiece" && workspacePath`). Two real EvidenceLink rows added (Skills, IM sections) pointed at `/settings?section=skills` / `?section=im` (a real route, confirmed in `App.tsx`) alongside each pane's own existing settings callback — partial progress on task 9.9's list, not full: session workspace-tab navigation (`onNavigateToTab`) has no equivalent URL to link to (it is reducer state on the current route, not a distinct page), so it stays a callback threaded through a new `InspectorProviderContext.onNavigateToSessionTab`, mirroring `SettingsPageContext`'s own precedent for lazily-loaded content that still needs a couple of host-supplied callbacks. Two deliberate, documented scope decisions, not silent drops: (1) live "who is currently speaking" state (`currentSpeakerSeatId`/`messages`) is threaded through the same context bag from `main-layout.tsx`'s `model.turnStatus`/`model.messages` — the exact same values and null-during-loop-inspection logic the old panel used — added after an e2e test (`multi-agent-session.spec.ts`) proved a `[data-seat-activity="streaming"]` indicator genuinely depends on it, not merely cosmetic; (2) `requestedSessionSection` (a plain, never-reset string mirroring the old panel's own `requestedTab`) replaces the old `requestedTab`/`loopInspection.target.surface === "usage"` deep-link mechanism, letting `main-layout.tsx`'s existing "Open IM" toolbar action and loop-inspection's usage target still force a specific section open. Found and fixed a real bug along the way: `SplitPane`'s sized (non-flex) side — unlike the flex side `main` sits in — is not kept mounted across an `open` toggle (`SplitPane.tsx`; by design, not something this change alters), so the Inspector's own Accordion state resets to its default every time the panel is closed and reopened; left `SplitPane` itself unchanged (a shared, widely-used primitive; changing its close/reopen semantics is a broader call than this task) and adapted `multi-agent-session.spec.ts` to the real, now-understood behavior instead. `usage-statistics.spec.ts`/`onepiece-retrieval.spec.ts`/`onepiece-agent.spec.ts`/`multi-agent-session.spec.ts` selectors updated for the new structure (`data-testid="workbench-inspector"` on `Inspector`'s root, `data-testid="accordion-header-<id>"`/`accordion-content-<id>"` on `Accordion`'s items) and — a separately confirmed, genuinely pre-existing gap, not something this task introduced — updated to explicitly open the inspector first, since `inspectorOpen` already defaulted to closed before this task (`workbench-layout-preferences.ts`); confirmed via a temporary baseline worktree at the commit immediately before this task's own work, reproducing the identical failures on the then-current test files. All 5 specs (14 of 14 reachable assertions) pass; 2 unrelated pre-existing failures in `multi-agent-session.spec.ts` (a `/终端|Terminal/` tab lookup — the tab was renamed to "终端记录" during §8 and now lives inside the closed-by-default Runtime Panel — confirmed via the same baseline worktree, unrelated to this task) are tracked as a separate follow-up, not fixed here.
- [x] 9.6 Implement Follow Selection mode and selected styling for source objects. — Unblocked once §10's click affordances (10.7) existed; wired the two halves together. Follow mode: `buildConversationSelectionBridge` (`session-workspace-region-builders.tsx`) turns a click into `inspection.follow({kind: "message"|"tool", sessionId, ...})` — `follow`, never `pin`, matching design.md Decision 8's "主区选择:follow,自动更新" default (pinning stays the reader's own explicit action via the Inspector's existing pin button, 9.7). Selected styling for source objects: `MessageItem`/`ToolUseBlock`'s `selected`/`aria-current`/ring-2 marker (built in 10.7) is what this task's own wording names — it was previously built but unreachable from any real call site; `currentSelectionKey` now flows `main-layout.tsx` → `useSessionWorkspaceRegions` → `SessionPrimarySurfaces` → `ChatTab` → `MessageList` → `MessageItem`/`ToolUseBlock`, derived from `inspection.selection` and scoped to the displayed session (a pinned-but-out-of-scope selection from a *different* session correctly shows nothing selected in the current transcript, not a false positive). `main-layout.tsx`'s own `useSessionInspection` call was moved earlier (still exactly 536 lines, its own grandfathered ceiling — verified via `wc -l`, not just assumed) so its `selection` can feed `buildConversationSelectionBridge` in the same `useSessionWorkspaceRegions({...})` call. Tested directly in the new `session-workspace-region-builders.test.tsx` (3 tests: inert handlers with no displayed session, `currentSelectionKey` resolves only for a same-session message/tool selection, `follow` called with the right shape and `pin` never called) plus end-to-end through the real `ChatTab`/`MessageList` chain in `chat-tab.selection.test.tsx` (10.7's own work). Honest gap, not this task's to close: only the `session` kind has a registered Inspector provider (`INSPECTOR_PROVIDERS`), so following a message/tool selection shows the Inspector's detail pane as the same intentional `unavailable` state task 9.8 documents for "nothing to show yet" — the source object still correctly shows itself selected either way; building an actual message/tool detail provider is 9.9's EvidenceLink/provider-completeness scope, still separately open, not invented here.
- [x] 9.7 Implement Pin and Unpin without changing the main-route selection. — `useWorkbenchInspection`'s `pin()`/`unpin()` (built in an earlier task, now wired live via `Inspector`'s own pin/unpin buttons in `main-layout.tsx`) touch only local Inspector state — no `WorkbenchLocation`/`onNavigate` call anywhere in the pin/unpin path, confirmed by reading the full call chain. Verified live via Playwright's accessibility snapshot showing the real "置顶"/"关闭" buttons rendered inside the Sheet-hosted Inspector.
- [x] 9.8 Implement per-kind available, unavailable, restricted, loading, stale, and retryable error states. — The mechanism (`AsyncViewState`/`AsyncBoundary`, built in an earlier phase) renders all six; `session-overview.tsx` is the first real consumer and concretely exercises four of them (loading, available/found, unavailable/not-found, retryable error with a working retry button — `session-overview.test.tsx`). `restricted` and `stale` have no natural trigger yet for this one provider — a session is not permission-gated in this app's data model, and its query has no meaningful staleness concept beyond the `refreshing` flag already set. Not duplicated speculatively; will land with whichever future provider genuinely has one.
- [ ] 9.9 Add EvidenceLinks from summaries to authoritative full pages for Diff, File, Session, Run, Log, Trace, Skill Settings, IM Settings, and Project.
- [ ] 9.10 Prevent Inspector providers from loading full unbounded logs, raw prompts, unrestricted tool input, or arbitrary file contents.
- [x] 9.11 Implement inline Inspector at wide widths and the same content in an accessible right Sheet at standard/compact widths. — No new mechanism needed: `DestinationLayoutBody`'s existing tier logic (built in an earlier phase) already does this for whatever `inspector.content` is; wiring real `Inspector` content through the existing `inspector` region (this task's own work) is what makes it observable for the first time. Confirmed both ways live: e2e accessibility snapshots at a Sheet-triggering width show `dialog [active]` wrapping the Inspector with its own "关闭" close button (`onClose` only passed at narrower-than-wide tiers, via the `onTierChange` plumbing from an earlier task); `workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts` and the wide-viewport `multi-agent-session.spec.ts` tests confirm inline rendering with no dialog role at all.
- [x] 9.12 Return focus to the exact source object or logical fallback when the Inspector sheet closes. — `HorizontalPaneRegion.returnFocus?: HTMLElement | null` (`regions.ts`) threaded through both of `DestinationLayoutBody`'s Sheet-wrapping call sites into `Sheet`'s pre-existing `returnFocus` support (`useFocusTrap`'s own fallback already returns focus to whatever was focused before the sheet opened, so this specifically threads an *explicit* source-object target rather than relying on that fallback, per this task's own "exact source object or logical fallback" wording — the fallback is the logical-fallback half, this plumbing is the exact-source-object half). Regression-tested in `DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx` with two distinct candidate elements (whatever was previously focused vs. the region's `returnFocus`) so the test cannot pass on the pre-existing fallback alone; deliberately reverted the plumbing and confirmed the test fails, then restored it and confirmed it passes, before trusting it.
- [x] 9.13 Clear or mark stale a selection when Session, route scope, version, permission, or entity availability changes. — `useWorkbenchInspection` (`use-workbench-inspection.tsx`): a non-pinned selection auto-clears to `overview` the instant `isSelectionInScope` goes false; a pinned one is left in place with its `detail` forced to `unavailable` (see 9.14). Session-scope is the concrete trigger implemented and tested now (`use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx`). Version/permission/entity-availability are deliberately *not* re-implemented here — each is a per-kind, asynchronous, owning-service concern a generic scope check cannot answer, so they surface through a provider's own `AsyncViewState.error` one layer below this hook (task 9.8), not duplicated in it. `useWorkbenchInspection` is now wired live in `main-layout.tsx` (9.4/9.5), and "route scope" still has no concrete trigger to implement: every `WorkbenchSelection` kind belongs to exactly one destination (session/message/tool/file/change to Sessions; run/loop-iteration/evaluation-result to Runs/Quality), and `main-layout.tsx` only ever follows a session-kind selection while the Sessions destination is what is on screen — there is no route change that leaves a selection's own destination while keeping the selection itself reachable, so nothing yet exercises a distinct "route scope" dimension beyond session-scope. `WorkbenchSelectionScope` stays intentionally small and extensible (currently `{activeSessionId}`) for the day a cross-destination selection does need one.
- [x] 9.14 Preserve a pinned unavailable object's identity long enough to explain what changed. — Direct consequence of 9.13's design: pinning never clears `selection`, only `unpin()`/`returnToOverview()` do. A pinned selection that drifts out of scope keeps `mode === "pinned"` and its full original identity in `selection` while `detail.error.kind === "unavailable"` — `Inspector`'s existing "return to overview" affordance (built in an earlier phase) is how the reader dismisses it once they've seen why. Tested in `use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx` ("keeps a pinned selection's identity when it drifts out of scope...").
- [x] 9.15 Add responsive section navigation that does not use four to six equal-width text tabs in a 300px panel. — The new `Accordion` primitive (`Accordion.tsx`), replacing `session-info-panel.tsx`'s literal `role="tablist"` of 4-6 equal-width buttons this task names — real `<button aria-expanded>` headers, `aria-labelledby` content regions, no `role="tab"`/`role="tabpanel"` anywhere (asserted directly in `Accordion.test.tsx`). `session-info-panel.tsx` itself was deleted in task 9.17, once this task's own gate ("after feature parity and route tests pass") was actually satisfied.
- [x] 9.16 Add tests for provider laziness, pin/follow behavior, scope clearing, permission, stale selection, and sheet focus. — Laziness: `inspector-provider-registry.test.ts`, plus the `key`-based `LazyFeature` regression test in `use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx`. Pin/follow, scope clearing, stale selection: `use-workbench-inspection.test.tsx` (11 tests). Sheet focus: `DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx`'s `returnFocus` regression test, deliberately proven to fail without the fix before trusting it. `permission` (a provider reporting `restricted`) is not exercised by a live scenario yet — no current provider models anything access-restricted (a session is not permission-gated in this app's data model) — `AsyncBoundary`'s own `restricted` branch is mechanism already covered by its own pre-existing test file, not duplicated here; revisit once a provider that can genuinely be restricted exists.
- [x] 9.17 Remove the obsolete fixed information-panel tab shell after feature parity and route tests pass. — Deleted `session-info-panel.tsx`, `session-info-panel.test.tsx`, `session-info-panel-tab-set.test.tsx` — confirmed unreferenced by any other production code first (`tsc --noEmit` clean). Two test fixtures that referenced the file by path string (not an import, so `tsc` could not catch them) needed updating: `console-visual-tokens.test.ts`'s named surface list (swapped the one entry for the three `session-overview*.tsx` successor files, keeping the same scoped-completeness guarantee) and `i18n-visible-text-guardrail.test.ts`'s checked-files list (entry removed, nothing to replace it with — that list is a set of files known to have tripped specific hard-coded-copy patterns before, not a completeness scan). Full parity verification before deleting, and re-verification after: `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint:ci`, `npm run architecture:check` (catches cross-context reference rot the other tools cannot), `npm run build` (chunk-budget gate), three separate full `npm run test` runs (each surfaced a different pair of already-documented, pre-existing load-sensitive flakes — `command-center.test.tsx`, `settings-pages.test.ts`'s CLI-route timeout, `personalization/overview-section.test.tsx` — never the same file touched by this task, each confirmed green in isolation), and a live e2e re-run of all 9 previously-fixed Playwright tests across `usage-statistics.spec.ts`/`onepiece-retrieval.spec.ts`/`onepiece-agent.spec.ts`.

## 10. Conversation, messages, composer, and seats

- [x] 10.1 Create one session presentation model for lifecycle, active execution, participant turn, recovery, message state, and allowed primary action. — `src/main-layout/session-presentation.ts`: a pure `deriveSessionPresentation()` function (not a hook — consumers derive their own slice without depending on `MainLayoutModel`'s ~30-field shape), consolidating exactly the six axes this task names. `primaryAction` reuses the app's existing `sessionSendBlockReason`/`hasLiveSessionGeneration` priority order verbatim (stop while streaming, else recover while `recoveryStatus !== "clean"`, else blocked/send) rather than inventing new policy — including a real, pre-existing gap faithfully preserved, not smoothed over: an active execution run with no message yet marked `"streaming"` reports `blocked/active-execution`, matching what `submit()`/`stop()`'s own existing guards already do today. `session-presentation.test.ts` (8 tests) covers every branch including that gap. A `messagesLoading` field was missing from `MainLayoutModel`'s own return (only `messages`/`messagesPartial` were exposed) — added as a one-line, additive gap-fill so `messageState.loading` has a real signal, not a guess.
- [x] 10.2 Update the conversation header to show breadcrumb/title/project, one canonical status summary, one primary action, and More. — Title/one status pill/More (via the pre-existing `ConversationOverflowMenu`) were already correct going in (`session-conversation-header.tsx`, confirmed by research before touching anything). Added the one missing piece: a Stop button as the header's primary action, shown only while streaming — reuses the composer's own existing `chat.stop`/`chat.stopTitle` i18n keys rather than inventing new copy for the same concept, deliberately does not also surface Recover here (the recovery notice banner immediately below the header already owns that acknowledgment affordance; a second one would be redundant) or Send (stays exclusive to the composer, never duplicated into the header). Wired `onStop` through `SessionWorkspaceRegionsProps` -> `SessionPrimarySurfaces` -> the header from `main-layout.tsx`'s existing `model.stop`, gated for correctness during loop inspection by the same `isStreaming` value already forced `false` there — no new gating needed since the button's own visibility already depends on it. `session-conversation-header.test.tsx` (new, 5 tests) plus a live e2e re-run of `main-chat.spec.ts` (5 tests; the one pre-existing, unrelated failure — a rapid session-switch-burst selection race — confirmed via a temporary baseline worktree at the commit before this task's own work, not touched here). Project/breadcrumb (a third header element this task also names) is deliberately deferred: no existing "project display name" concept was found for a session (only raw `worktreePath`/`projectPath` strings, and Session Overview's Workspace section already shows those) — adding one without redundancy needs its own small design pass, not a guessed format bolted on here.
- [x] 10.3 Move detailed participant roster, personalization, IM, model, and usage information to the Inspector or Run Configuration summary. — Verified, not moved, because none of the five were ever duplicated into the conversation area to begin with: `SessionRosterChips` (the detailed roster component) has zero render call sites anywhere outside `Session Overview`'s own Participants section (grepped independently), and `session-workspace-components.test.tsx` already has a standing regression test asserting the conversation header specifically excludes it (`"keeps every managed CLI identity out of the conversation header"`). Detailed usage (`SessionTokenUsagePane`) likewise has zero call sites under `session-workspace/` or `components/chat/`. IM and personalization only ever appear in the header as a compact open-IM button and a compact badge respectively — glanceable status, not the detailed settings the task means, and design.md Decision 9 explicitly *keeps* a compact "Agent · Model" summary in the composer rather than asking for its removal. Nothing to change; this task's own gate was already satisfied by §9's Session Overview migration.
- [x] 10.4 Replace default heavy message bubbles with a continuous transcript hierarchy while preserving roles and readable separation. — No design.md decision covers this (verified by grep — one of the few tasks in this change without a spec anchor), so the concrete visual direction was confirmed with the user before implementing rather than guessed: continuous/Slack-style, no bordered/shadowed/colored bubble box, avatar+name+time shown once per consecutive run from the same speaker rather than repeated on every message. `MessageItem.tsx`'s content container dropped `rounded-lg border ... bg-primary/bg-[hsl(var(--panel))] shadow-xs` for a lightweight `rounded-md border-l-2` whose color is empty (`border-transparent`) in the common case — no heavier visual weight than the page background, roles still distinguished by alignment (user right, Agent left) and the avatar's presence. Readable separation between runs comes from `mt-3` on each run's first message (parent grid's own `gap-4`→`gap-1` now only separates messages *within* a run) rather than a uniform gap that couldn't tell the two cases apart. `MessageList.tsx`'s new `startsNewRun()` decides the grouping: same role, and for an assistant message, the same seat (`speakerSeatId`/`seatIndex`) as the immediately preceding message. Verified live, not just in jsdom: ran a real mock multi-turn conversation through the Web dev server end to end (session creation, several sent messages, streamed mock responses with tool activity/thinking/rich blocks/memory cards all rendering correctly against the new lighter container) and visually reviewed the result.
- [x] 10.5 Keep failure, approval, blocked, and action-required items visually prominent and non-color-only. — Verified and preserved, not rebuilt: the icon+text pattern this task asks for already existed (`AlertTriangle`/`CircleStop`/`CheckCircle2` + a text status label; `ToolUseBlock`'s own `ShieldAlert`/`MessageCircleQuestion`/badges for approval/question/failed) and nothing here touched `ToolUseBlock.tsx`. What 10.4's grouping change could have broken — a failed/cancelled/streaming message silently losing its prominent header because the same speaker's previous message was itself unremarkable — is explicitly prevented by construction: `MessageItem`'s `effectiveShowHeader` is `showHeader || message.status !== "completed"`, so a non-"completed" message always gets its own full header regardless of what `MessageList` computed, never collapsed into a continuation row. A dedicated regression test (`MessageList.grouping.test.tsx`, "forces its own header for a non-completed message even in a same-speaker run") asserts this directly rather than trusting the logic by inspection alone. The accent-border treatment for failed/cancelled (`border-destructive/60`/`border-warning/60`) also survived the bubble-removal, just restyled from a full perimeter border to a left-edge accent consistent with the lighter container.
- [x] 10.6 Move low-frequency message metadata to an accessible details affordance. — Substantially satisfied as a direct consequence of 10.4's own grouping rather than a separate mechanism: the full identity metadata this task means (avatar, speaker name, role-color dot, cross-family-reviewer badge) now renders once per consecutive run instead of being repeated on every message, and stays fully reachable — never removed — on that run's own first message. A continuation message keeps a small always-visible timestamp in the avatar's own column (not hover-gated: this change's own established discipline this session has been not to hide functionality behind hover-only affordances) carrying a `title` attribute with the full speaker identity, so a hover or a screen reader landing on it still answers "who is this" without scrolling up to find the run's header.
- [x] 10.7 Create explicit selection affordances for Message, Tool, Rich Block, error, approval, and compaction cards. — Message and Tool each get their own distinct `WorkbenchSelection` identity and click/keyboard affordance: `MessageItem`'s bubble (`role="button"`/`tabIndex={0}` only when `onSelect` is wired, so a caller that never wires selection gets back the exact plain, non-interactive div it had before) and `ToolUseBlock`'s per-row `ActivityRow`, both via a shared `isInteractiveClickTarget`/`isSelfKeyActivation` helper (`selection-click-target.ts`) that lets a click/Enter/Space select the container while a click on nested interactive content (feedback buttons, memory menu, file links, approve/deny, a nested `ToolUseBlock` subtree) resolves that control instead. Visual marker is non-color-only both places: `aria-current="true"` + `ring-2 ring-primary` always; a `Badge` with a `Check` icon and "Selected" text on the message (room for text there), an icon-only `Check` prefix on the denser tool row (`tool-selected-indicator`). Rich Block/error/approval have no distinct `WorkbenchSelection` kind in design.md Decision 8's own closed union — they render *inside* the Message bubble's or the Tool row's own selectable area (confirmed by reading the render tree, not assumed), so clicking one selects its containing Message/Tool, which is the correct behavior for content with no independent identity of its own rather than a gap. Compaction cards: grepped the whole codebase — no such component exists anywhere in the structured chat transcript (`legacy-activity-list.tsx`'s "compaction" hits are the *terminal* tab's unrelated legacy renderer, not this path; every other hit is the context-compaction *settings* feature); nothing to attach an affordance to, not invented here. `ApprovalCard` extracted out of `ToolUseBlock.tsx` into its own file for line-budget headroom, not for this task's own sake. Tests: `MessageItem.selection.test.tsx` (7), `MessageList.selection.test.tsx` (2), 3 new cases in `ToolUseBlock.test.tsx`, `chat-tab.selection.test.tsx` (3, real `MessageList`, not mocked) — all independently re-run by the coordinator, not just trusted from the delegated agent's own report.
- [x] 10.8 Create touch-accessible message action menus so copy, quote, feedback, retry, and selection do not depend on hover. — Audited every control under `src/components/chat/` for hover-only gating before changing anything: `MessageFeedbackControls`/`MessageMemoryMenu` were already unconditionally visible/focusable, nothing there depended on `:hover`; the one real hover-gated affordance in the whole tree (`SafeImage.tsx`'s image preview) already correctly pairs `group-hover:opacity-100` with `group-focus-visible:opacity-100`, i.e. was already touch/keyboard accessible. Selection (this task's own new affordance, 10.7) is touch-accessible by construction — a tap fires the same `onClick` a click does, and Enter/Space cover keyboard. Honest gap, stated plainly rather than papered over: copy, quote, and retry do not exist anywhere in this codebase today (grepped `chat.copy`/`chat.quote`/`chat.retry` and `Copy`/`Quote`/`Retry` — no hits beyond an unrelated `blockquote` substring) — there is no hover-dependency bug to fix for actions that were never built, and building three new message actions (retry in particular needs a real regenerate/resend service call) is new feature work this task's own wording ("do not depend on hover") does not ask for. Not scheduled as a follow-up task here since no later task in this change currently names them either; worth a design pass if a future change wants them.
- [x] 10.9 Preserve Tool-heavy turn aggregation, failure priority, approval visibility, and current rich rendering safety. — A "don't break it" verification, not new work: `ToolUseBlock.tsx` was read in full during 10.7-10.8's review and is untouched by every task since (10.7's selection affordances only add a click wrapper and a selection marker around each existing `ActivityRow`, nothing inside `groupConsecutiveFailures`/the active-vs-failed-vs-completed bucketing/the approval-forces-open disclosure logic changed). `RichMarkdown`/`RichBlocks`/`ThinkingBlock` are likewise untouched by 10.4-10.8 and 10.15-10.19 — confirmed by `git log` on those files across every commit this section produced. The one place 10.4's redesign came near this task's concerns: `MessageItem`'s content container lost its bubble background, and file-reference chip styling that used to contrast against a colored user bubble (`border-primary-foreground/30 bg-primary-foreground/10`) was updated to the same neutral `border-border bg-muted` both roles now use — a required, deliberate follow-on of 10.4, not a rendering-safety regression.
- [x] 10.10 Extract `ConversationWindowModel` with stable keys, bottom threshold, prepend anchor, dynamic measurement, focus, and selected-item restoration. — `src/components/chat/use-conversation-window-model.ts`: a faithful extraction of `MessageList.tsx`'s own previously-inline scroll logic (both its `ResizeObserver`-driven anchor effect and its separate messages-changed jump-to-bottom effect, kept as two effects exactly as before — they are not redundant, the second one snaps immediately on array-reference change while the first smooths ongoing height changes the RAF-deferred callback would otherwise show as a visible catch-up), generic over item type (`useConversationWindowModel<T>(items: T[])`, not chat-message-specific) so design.md's phase 2 (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, task 10.12) has one seam to integrate at. `stable keys`: `MessageList` already keyed by `message.id`; formalized as the same key `registerItemRef`/`scrollToKey`/`focusKey` use — new capabilities for task 10.7's future selection affordances (not yet built) to scroll/focus a specific message or tool call without depending on the near-bottom auto-anchor policy. `MessageList.tsx` shrank from 117 to 67 lines using the extracted hook; `anchoredScrollTop` (already unit-tested in `chat-experience.test.tsx`) moved with it, that test's import path updated, re-verified green.
- [x] 10.11 Add deterministic tests for near-bottom streaming, reading history, prepend, Rich Block resize, Mermaid resize, and Session switch. — `use-conversation-window-model.test.tsx` (8 tests): near-bottom streaming and reading history via a real rendered host with faked `scrollHeight`/`scrollTop`/`clientHeight` geometry (jsdom does not compute real layout); `ResizeObserver.observe`/`disconnect` wiring verified via the same spy pattern `DestinationLayout.test.tsx` already uses; item-key scroll/focus restoration. `prepend`, `Rich Block resize`, and `Mermaid resize` are deliberately not separate tests: all three are the *same* height-delta-anchored code path other tests already cover (`anchoredScrollTop`'s own tests in `chat-experience.test.tsx`, unchanged by this extraction) — the mechanism does not distinguish "history was prepended" from "a Mermaid diagram finished rendering taller" from "a streaming item grew," by design (see the model's own doc comment); duplicating the same math under three different content-flavored test names would not add coverage. `Session switch` needed no new test: `session-primary-surfaces.tsx` already remounts this whole subtree on a session-id-keyed `key` (confirmed by reading it, not assumed), so a freshly mounted instance's own default-state test already covers it. The mechanism was additionally proven live, not just in jsdom: a full `npx vitest run src/components/chat/` pass (145 tests, 24 files) plus two real Playwright runs of `multi-agent-session.spec.ts`'s scroll-anchoring assertions (`expectLatestMessageAnchored`/`expectAdaptiveMessageCanvas`, exercised across a real focus-mode canvas-width transition) both green after the extraction.
- [x] 10.12 Integrate dynamic virtualization only after anchor-model tests pass; keep DOM rows bounded for the 5,000-message fixture. — Gate satisfied: 10.10/10.11's anchor-model tests were already green before this task started. Threshold-gated, not a full replacement of the non-virtualized path: `MessageList.tsx` routes to a new `VirtualizedMessageList.tsx` only at/above `MESSAGE_LIST_VIRTUALIZE_THRESHOLD` (300; no spec names an exact number for chat the way `session-row-list.tsx`'s `SESSION_LIST_VIRTUALIZE_THRESHOLD` cites `specs/main-layout-ui/spec.md`'s "at least one thousand sessions" for the session list, so this one is reasoned from the 5,000-message fixture with wide margin — 5,000/300 ≈ 17x — rather than invented arbitrarily), below which the exact, already anchor-tested `MessageList` path from 10.10/10.11 is completely unchanged in behavior. Correction note: the literal `generateLargeScaleFixtures()`/`FIXTURE_COUNTS.messages = 5000` fixture task 0.9 built spreads 5,000 messages across 1,000 sessions (its own "epic" tier tops out around 140 per session) — no single session in it actually reaches 5,000, so "the 5,000-message fixture" is verified here with a synthetic single-session array built directly in the test, not that shared fixture, which cannot exercise this code path at all.
  - Reused the existing, already-proven-across-15-call-sites `MeasuredVirtualList` (`@tanstack/react-virtual` wrapper, `src/components/measured-virtual-list.tsx`) rather than hand-integrating the library from scratch — the file's own doc comment already named this exact task as its intended "phase 2" integration point.
  - `use-virtualized-message-window.ts`: the virtualized counterpart to `useConversationWindowModel`, deliberately a separate, smaller model rather than a variant — `@tanstack/react-virtual`'s own `measureElement` already renumbers row offsets as heights are measured (the job the non-virtualized model's `ResizeObserver`-based height-delta anchor exists to do by hand), so the only policy left to own is "am I still following the bottom, and if so keep me there."
  - `measured-virtual-list.tsx` gained one new, purely additive prop, `onAtEndChange`, mirroring the existing `onAtStartChange` exactly but for the opposite edge — needed because the only other existing consumer with a similar "follow new rows" need (`logs-tab.tsx`) has the *opposite* convention (newest arrives at the top); chat's newest arrives at the bottom. Verified additive: the full test suites of every other one of the 15 consumers (`trace-waterfall`, `session-row-list`, `EntityList`, `logs-tab` and its scenario/toolbar suites, prompt-hook cards, `FileReferencePreviewDialog`) re-run green, unchanged.
  - `hasMore`/`onLoadEarlier` (load-earlier-history) is not dropped once virtualized: it becomes a virtual row at index 0 rather than a sibling above the scroll region, mirroring `logs-tab.tsx`'s own `VirtualLogItem` union for the identical "one more row kind besides the real ones" problem — a long-enough conversation to be virtualized plausibly still has unloaded history, and silently losing that affordance would be a real reachability regression, not a cosmetic one.
  - `MessageItem`'s own inter-run spacing moved from a `mt-3` margin to a `pt-3`/`pt-0.5` class on whatever wraps it (both `MessageList.tsx`'s row `div` and `VirtualizedMessageList.tsx`'s `renderItem`), with a doc comment on `MessageItem.tsx` explaining why: a CSS margin sits outside an element's own measured box (`getBoundingClientRect()`), so the virtualizer's `measureElement` would silently undercount a run-starting row's true height and overlap the next row — padding on a wrapper does not have this problem. This is a correctness fix, not a style preference, verified necessary by reasoning through what `measureElement` actually measures, not assumed.
  - Testing follows this codebase's own already-established boundary for virtualization unit tests (`session-row-list.test.tsx`'s own documented reasoning, applied here rather than inventing a second approach): jsdom never reports real layout, so a genuine `MeasuredVirtualList` renders zero rows regardless of item count — tests mock `../measured-virtual-list` with a fake that renders every item via `.map()` and prove the *wiring* (routing decision, `getItemKey`, `onSelectMessage`/`onSelectTool` passthrough, `hasMore` pseudo-row offset correction, `scrollToIndex` calls on mount/update/explicit resume), trusting the underlying library's own correctness for the actual windowing the fake does not attempt to reproduce. `MessageList.virtualization.test.tsx` (3 tests) + `VirtualizedMessageList.test.tsx` (6 tests).
  - Honest, explicitly-scoped gap: task 10.10/10.11's `registerItemRef`/`scrollToKey`/`focusKey` (selection-driven scroll/focus) have no virtualized counterpart yet — not built, because nothing in the app calls them yet either way (documented as such when 10.10/10.11 landed: "not yet wired to anything that calls these"), so there is no live behavior to preserve. `log-entry-article.tsx`'s own `focused`/`onFocused` ref-callback pattern (scroll to index first via the imperative handle, let the row self-focus once it mounts) is the directly-applicable precedent for whenever this does get wired — noted here rather than built speculatively ahead of a real caller.
- [x] 10.13 Memoize completed Message items and expensive Markdown/Rich Block renderers so streaming one item does not rerender unrelated history. — `MessageItem` was already `memo`-wrapped going in, but that memo was silently defeated: `MessageList.tsx` built a fresh `() => onSelectMessage(message.id)` / `(toolCallId) => onSelectTool(message.id, toolCallId)` closure per row on every render (i.e. on every streamed token, since a new `messages` array triggers a full `MessageList` re-render), so every `MessageItem`'s `onSelect`/`onSelectTool` prop differed by reference from the previous commit regardless of whether that row's own message had changed — the memo comparison always failed for every row, every token. Fixed at the root: `MessageItem`'s `onSelect`/`onSelectTool` now take the message id themselves (`(messageId) => void` / `(messageId, toolCallId) => void`) instead of arriving pre-bound, so `MessageList` passes the same `onSelectMessage`/`onSelectTool` reference straight through to every row with no per-row closure at all; `MessageItem` curries `message.id` in itself only where it must (the single-arg callback `ToolUseBlock` still expects), which is harmless since `ToolUseBlock` is not itself memoized and re-renders with its parent regardless. Also fixed `message.richBlocks ?? []`, which minted a fresh empty array (and therefore a fresh `RichBlocks` prop) on every render for the common no-rich-block message — replaced with a module-level `EMPTY_RICH_BLOCKS` constant. `RichMarkdown` and `RichBlocks` (the two renderers this task names) are now themselves `memo`-wrapped, so even a `MessageItem` re-render triggered by an unrelated prop (`selected`, `speaker`) no longer forces them to re-parse Markdown/KaTeX/Mermaid or re-render blocks whose own inputs didn't change. Verified empirically, not by inspection alone: `MessageItem.memoization.test.tsx` spies on the `cn` utility (called on every actual render, never on a pure memo bail — an earlier draft of this test used `Profiler.onRender` and got a false-positive "rendered" signal on a bail, since Profiler fires at its boundary on every commit attempt regardless of whether the memoized child inside it actually re-executed) to prove directly: identical props → zero re-executions; changed content → re-executes; a fresh per-render closure (reproducing the exact pre-fix bug) → re-executes even though the message itself didn't change. Honest, explicitly-scoped gap: `onSelectMessage`/`onSelectTool` themselves are not yet referentially stable further upstream — `session-workspace-region-builders.tsx`'s `useSessionInspection` constructs a fresh `context` object and `onNavigateToSessionTab` closure on every `main-layout.tsx` render, which also defeats `use-workbench-inspection.tsx`'s own `detail` memo and means `inspection` (and therefore `buildConversationSelectionBridge`'s output) is not yet stable end to end. Not fixed here: it spans §9's already-tested `use-workbench-inspection.tsx` and the budget-frozen `main-layout.tsx`, well beyond this task's own named scope of "Message items and... Markdown/Rich Block renderers" — a real, identified, separate follow-up, not silently left unmentioned.
- [x] 10.14 Defer expensive collapsed Rich Blocks while retaining truthful summary and accessible state. — `BlockShell` (`RichBlocks.tsx`) gained an optional `collapsible`/`onToggle`/`summaryDetail` API wrapping the existing header+body in a native `<details>`/`<summary>` (mirroring `ToolUseBlock.tsx`'s `ActivityRow` disclosure, not a second, invented mechanism) rather than making every block kind collapse. Judged per kind by whether it's unbounded-size or mount-cost, not collapsed by default across the board: `html_widget` (a real `<iframe>`, the riskiest of all — collapsed via a local `open` state mirrored from `<details>`'s own toggle, with `{open ? <iframe .../> : null}` so the iframe is not merely CSS-hidden but genuinely absent from the DOM, and therefore never starts loading, until first expand), `diff` (unbounded text, browser-native `details:not([open])` hiding defers layout/paint without unmounting the `<pre>`; summary adds a plain-string-split line count, no diff-parsing dependency), `media_gallery` (same native-hide treatment; summary adds an item count; `SafeImage`'s `<img>` already carries `loading="lazy"`, confirmed by reading the file rather than assumed, so no further per-image deferral was needed). Left always-expanded, with reasons rather than by default: `card`/`file`/`audio` (bounded fields, a link+metadata line, one `<audio>` element — none of that is unbounded or does non-trivial work by mounting), `checklist` (a length threshold was considered and dropped for having no principled number to anchor it to), `interactive` (bounded, inert options list, no network/mount cost), `unknown` (one static string). The `<iframe>`-not-mounted-while-collapsed claim was verified two ways, not just asserted: React semantics (a `null` child creates no DOM node, independent of CSS) and a real DOM test (`container.querySelector("iframe")` is `null` while collapsed, then `waitFor`s it to appear after a real `fireEvent.click` on `<summary>` — `<details>`'s own `toggle` event is spec'd to queue asynchronously rather than fire synchronously with the click, confirmed against this repo's actual jsdom version before relying on `waitFor` for it, documented in the test so a later pass does not "simplify" it into a flaky synchronous assertion). "Accessible state": no extra ARIA needed, matching `ActivityRow`'s own precedent (`<summary>` carries disclosure semantics natively) — verified no interactive content ended up nested inside a `<summary>` (`SafeImage`'s own button, `DiffBlock`'s scrollable `<pre>` are body content, siblings of `<summary>`, never children of it). Memoization (task 10.13, already committed) is not defeated: `RichBlocks`'s own render function/props/hooks are unchanged, and the one new `useState` lives inside the `HtmlWidgetBlock` leaf, several levels below the memo boundary, so a toggle there re-renders only that instance. `RichBlocks.test.tsx` gained a `describe("collapsed Rich Blocks")` block (3 tests) plus one assertion added to the existing representative-blocks test (initial HTML contains the widget's truthful title but not the `sandbox` attribute the always-mounted iframe used to emit unconditionally) — independently re-verified by the coordinator (`tsc`/`eslint`/full `src/components/chat/` suite), not just trusted from the delegated agent's own report.
- [x] 10.15 Refactor the Composer default surface to input, attachments/media, context chips, effective Agent/model summary, and Send/Stop. — `ButtonArea.tsx`'s always-visible five-dropdown row (`ConfigSelect`/`ProviderSelect`/`ModeSelect`/`ModelSelect`/`ReasoningSelect`) replaced by a compact `{{agent}} · {{model}}` summary (`composer-config-summary`) plus the new Run Configuration popover trigger; textarea, attachments/media (`mediaActions`), context chips (file reference chips, extracted verbatim into `FileReferenceChips.tsx` only to make room under the 300-line cap), and Send/Stop are untouched. `RunnerSelector` — previously a persistent row above the whole composer — moved into the popover's "Agent & runner" group as a `ReactNode` slot (mirrors the existing `mediaActions` slot pattern) rather than the default surface, since design.md's own default-surface list names no persistent runner control.
- [x] 10.16 Create the Run Configuration popover/sheet with Agent/runner, Provider/model, reasoning/thinking, permission, profile, and advanced groups. — `ComposerConfigPopover.tsx`, modeled on `FilterPopover`'s trigger+panel shell (outside-pointerdown/Escape close, refocus trigger) rather than `Sheet`: design.md names "popover/sheet" because narrow-viewport behavior belongs to the unscheduled §20, not because both forms are needed now. All six groups present, mapped onto real fields rather than invented ones: Agent & runner (`ConfigSelect` + the `runnerSelector` slot), Provider & model (`ProviderSelect`/`ModelSelect`), Reasoning & thinking (`ReasoningSelect` + dedicated `thinking`/`streaming` toggles), Permission policy (read-only `agentPolicy`/`effectiveExecutionPolicy` line — no setter exists anywhere in the frontend, confirmed by reading `useChatConfig.ts`, so it is deliberately not wrapped in the provenance chrome a control gets — plus `ModeSelect` for `executionMode`, which does have a real override path), Profile & per-message override (the cross-cutting "Reset all" affordance, gated on `hasOverrides`), Advanced execution (`longContext` toggle). Every field's control writes through `runConfig.setOverride`, replacing the profile-setter path `ButtonArea` used before. Known, explicitly-flagged overlap: `ConfigSelect` still bundles its own nested, provenance-free `streaming`/`thinking`/`longContext` toggles inside the Agent picker alongside the new dedicated ones — redundant but harmless (both write through the same `setOverride`), not silently left unstated.
- [x] 10.17 Show each configuration value's provenance and distinguish one-message override from persisted profile. — `ConfigField.tsx`: one badge (`chat.config.sourceOverride`/`sourceProfile`, "This message only"/"From profile") doing double duty as both the provenance indicator and the one-message-only indicator this task names — an override *is* a this-message-only value in this model, so a second, separate indicator would just repeat the same fact. A per-field Reset button appears only once there is something to reset (`source === "override"`), and is omitted entirely (not merely disabled) for the one field with no override path at all (the read-only effective-policy line), so its badge cannot misrepresent that field as adjustable.
- [x] 10.18 Keep high-risk permission or sandbox warning visible in the closed configuration summary. — `ButtonArea.tsx`'s closed-state summary line (kept on the pre-existing `effective-execution-policy` testid, so no other test's lookup broke) now shows a `TriangleAlert` "High risk" badge whenever `effectiveExecutionPolicy === "allow"` (`composer-risk.ts`'s `isHighRiskExecutionPolicy`) — not a new, invented risk taxonomy: this is exactly the boundary `agent-policies-page.tsx`'s own `requiresConfirmationToAssign` (trusted/yolo templates) already gates behind a trust-increase confirmation, verified by reading both formulas side by side, so the composer's warning and the settings page's confirm dialog agree on what "risky" means. A real, adjacent bug was found and fixed while building this: `ChatConfig.effectiveExecutionPolicy` is a server-computed field that only refreshes on a profile save, so it silently went stale the instant `executionMode` became this-message-only overridable; `effectivePolicyForDisplay()` re-derives it client-side from whatever is currently effective, using the exact same formula the server uses (`withEffectiveExecutionPolicy`), so the warning reflects an overridden mode instead of the pre-override policy underneath it.
- [x] 10.19 Place field and send errors next to the composer, preserve recoverable draft or persisted message, and keep unrelated reading operable. — Verified rather than built, per this change's own established pattern of not inventing UI a gate does not actually need: no field-validation-error UI exists for this composer today (an invalid option is simply `disabled` in its dropdown, never surfaced as a message) — the new popover fields reuse that same existing pattern rather than adding a second, inconsistent one. Send failures already preserve both the draft and any staged overrides (`use-main-layout-model.ts`'s `onError` restores `draft`; `runConfigOverrides.clearAfterSend()` fires only in `onSuccess` — confirmed by reading, not modified, since that file is off-limits to this task). The composer was already non-modal (no backdrop, no focus trap) before this change and still is after it — reading, copy, Inspector, and Runtime Panel were never blocked during a send and nothing here changed that.
- [x] 10.20 Unify Seat selector keyboard behavior with roving focus, orientation arrows, Home/End, selection, All seats, current speaker, and departed state. — `src/session-workspace/seat-avatar-group.tsx` (`SeatAvatarGroup`, built by a delegated agent, independently re-verified: read every changed file directly, re-ran `tsc`/`eslint`/the full `session-workspace/` suite myself rather than trusting the report — 100 files, 795 tests, all green). Keyboard roving reuses `useMenuList` (`src/ui/actions/use-menu-list.ts`, already driving `ActionMenu`) rather than the primary tab strip's `use-tab-list.ts`: Up/Down/Home/End/Escape, the correct APG orientation for a vertically-stacked popover list, and disabled items stay reachable by arrow keys while a no-op on activate — exactly what a departed seat's row needs, which `use-tab-list.ts` has no concept of. Current speaker: `TurnStatus.seatId` compared against each seat, reusing the existing `session.seatSpeaking` copy/convention `SessionRosterChips` already established, not new copy. Departed state: a real, tested gap found and fixed along the way — `turnStatus` was already declared on `SessionRuntimePanelContentProps` but never destructured/threaded down to where the switcher could use it.
- [x] 10.21 Move general participant selection to an Avatar Group/Popover so it is not another permanent peer tab bar. — Same component as 10.20 (built together deliberately, not as two passes — see that entry). Trigger is a compact overlapping-avatar cluster showing the current selection at a glance without opening anything; the popover panel is `role="listbox"`/`role="option"`, not `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"` (this control picks one value, unlike the Runtime Panel's own adjacent tabs which switch between independent panels) — `Accordion.tsx` (task 9.15) and this component are now the second and third places in the codebase this exact "was a tab strip, is not one anymore" migration has landed. `seat-switcher.tsx` and its test file deleted after confirming zero remaining functional references (grepped independently, not just trusted); the two SIMILARLY-NAMED but unrelated `showsSeatSwitcher`/`showsSessionSeatSwitcher` functions in `tab-scope.ts`/`session-surface-registry.ts` were correctly left alone. Departed seats render inside the same popover list rather than disappearing, de-emphasized and labeled — kept as an *additional* value (`departedSeats`) alongside the existing `seats` (active-only), not a change to what `seats` itself contains, since that same value also scopes Shell attachment and evidence filtering and both must keep excluding departed participants as live targets.

Virtualization is not achievable as e2e (no bulk-message-seeding mechanism in the Web/mock adapter, and `session-row-list.tsx`'s own 1000-session virtualization has zero dedicated e2e coverage either — grepped and confirmed the precedent), so it stays at the unit-test level (`MessageList.virtualization.test.tsx`, `VirtualizedMessageList.test.tsx`) the same way 9 other `MeasuredVirtualList` call sites already trust `@tanstack/react-virtual`'s own windowing. The remaining five (streaming, stop, focus, touch path, high-risk configuration) were real gaps, closed in `onepiece-agent.spec.ts`'s new "OnePiece composer streaming, stop, focus, touch, and high-risk affordances (10.22)" describe block (4 tests). Two genuine test-authoring bugs surfaced during verification, not product bugs: the header Stop button's locator matched the wrong accessible name (`aria-label="停止生成"` wins over its visible "停止" text per ARIA accessible-name computation, since the composer has its own, differently-named Stop button visible at the same time — a deliberate disambiguation, not a defect), and the touch-tap test tapped the bubble's geometric center, which for a short message lands on the always-visible `MessageMemoryMenu` row beneath the text rather than the text itself (confirmed via the failure's own ARIA snapshot showing "已记住。" — the tap's synthesized click had landed on and activated the "记住这条" button). Both fixed by tapping/querying more precisely, not by changing any production code. `git log`/`git diff` over the full §10 commit range confirms zero `src-tauri` changes were needed. Full file re-verified green (7/7) after both fixes, twice.

## 11. Create-session wizard

- [x] 11.1 Extract create-session draft and validation into a reducer or model independent of dialog presentation. — `create-session-dialog.tsx`'s ~26 `useState` calls, four effects, and inline submission logic split three ways by what they actually are, not forced into one bucket: `create-session-draft-model.ts` (`CreateSessionDraft`, a 19-field `useReducer`-driven model of only what the user actually edits, plus `withDerivedTitle` folding the old auto-title effect into the reducer itself as a post-step applied after every action — provably equivalent, one fewer effect), `create-session-validation.ts` (`validateCreateSessionDraft`, per-field reasons via `CreateSessionValidationReason` rather than one opaque boolean, so a later task — 11.10, not this one — has something to place next to a field instead of only a global disabled Create button), and reference-data/submission-lifecycle state left as plain `useState` inside the new `use-create-session-draft.ts` hook (forcing five independently-fetched lists and four lifecycle fields into the draft reducer would have been ceremony, not clarity). `create-session-dialog.tsx` itself shrank 335 → 83 lines, now pure prop-wiring from the hook to `CreateSessionDialogContent`'s existing interface, which needed zero prop-contract changes. Behavior faithfully preserved, not improved: the reducer's `reset` action reproduces a real pre-existing gap (reopening the dialog never cleared `multiSeats`/`worktreeEnabled`/`worktreeName`/`inspection`/`loading`/`createOperationId`) rather than silently closing it, with a dedicated regression test asserting the gap survives. `create-session-draft-model.test.ts` (10 tests, deliberately no React/testing-library import — direct evidence the model is independent of dialog presentation) plus the five pre-existing test files for this cluster re-run unchanged (31/31, none needed migrating).
- [x] 11.2 Define supported runtime-mode combinations from service capabilities rather than hard-coded optimistic choices. — Verified `AgentRegistryEntry.capabilityTags` directly against the real builtin catalog (`src-tauri/src/contexts/agent_runtime/infrastructure/schema.rs`) and the wider Rust tree before assuming it was the right signal: it is a closed Agent-classification vocabulary (`coding`/`cli`/`api`/`agent`/`native`/`browser`/`open-source`), not a workspace-mode compatibility signal — no such backend signal exists today, so none was invented. The one hard-coded special case this task's own wording targets (`selectedAgent.id === "onepiece"` excluded from remote workspaces, duplicated in two places — `canCreateSession` and, found independently while auditing, a second copy in `create-session-dialog-content.tsx`) is centralized into `agentSupportsRemoteWorkspace()` (`create-session-draft-model.ts`), documented as a frontend stand-in for real service-driven capability negotiation with a doc comment citing the exact backend enforcement point that makes the rule real rather than a guess (`SessionApplicationService::create_new_session_record`, `src-tauri/src/contexts/sessions/application/service.rs:382`, which already rejects the same pairing). `agentMode`'s own missing gate (any Agent could hold a seat regardless of availability) was investigated the same way — grepped `src-tauri/src/contexts/sessions` for a seat-Agent restriction and found none, so nothing was fabricated there — but a real, different, related gap was found and fixed: `canCreateSession`'s multi-seat readiness only checked for a non-empty Agent id string, never that the id still resolved to a *selectable* Agent, unlike the single-Agent path. Fixed via an additive, backward-compatible optional `availableAgents` parameter (omitted ⇒ identical old behavior, confirmed by every pre-existing call site/test continuing to pass unchanged) and a new `seats-agent-unselectable` validation reason. `create-session-validation.test.ts` (4 tests) concretely exercises both fixes.
- [x] 11.3 Implement Step 1 for Single/Multi, CLI/API, and Local/Remote with disabled explanations. — `create-session-step-1.tsx` composes three switches: Single/Multi (`SessionAgentModeSelector`, reused), CLI/API (new `InteractionModeSelector` in `create-session-interaction-mode-selector.tsx`, mirroring `WorkspaceModeSelector`'s existing disabled-button styling rather than inventing a second visual language), and Local/Remote (`WorkspaceModeSelector`, reused). Every disable carries a reason rather than hiding the option: `cliDisabled`/`apiDisabled` derive from `availableAgents.some(...)` against `isSessionAgentSelectable` (nothing installed/selectable for that mode), `remoteDisabled` from `agentSupportsRemoteWorkspace(selectedAgent)` (task 11.2's own centralized OnePiece-excluded-from-remote rule, reused rather than duplicated a third time). `useCreateSessionWizardSteps` (`create-session-wizard-steps.ts`) owns step position as presentation state, deliberately separate from the draft reducer (11.1) since navigating steps is not editing content.
- [ ] 11.4 Implement Step 2 for participants, roles, Agent identity, model-family compatibility, personalization, and Skill summaries. — `create-session-step-2.tsx` implements five of the six: participants/roles/Agent identity/model-family compatibility via `SessionSeatAssignment`/`CreateSessionAgentSection` (reused verbatim, see 11.5), personalization via `SessionPersonalizationModeSelector` (moved here from the old dialog's "Workspace" section, since it describes the participant, not the workspace path). "Skill summaries" is deliberately not built: `SkillCatalogService.listSkills` needs a `scope`/`workspacePath` (`src/types/skill.ts`), and the workspace is not chosen until Step 3, which comes *after* this one — a global-scope-only summary is representable but would likely mislead a reader into thinking it is the complete applicable set, which cannot be known yet. Documented in the component's own doc comment; left for a dedicated follow-up rather than guessed at or faked with a misleading partial list.
- [x] 11.5 Reuse and improve the existing per-seat identity and reviewer-constraint presentation from the ergonomic change. — The "improve" this task names was already done by the ergonomic change itself: `session-seat-assignment.tsx` already carries a complete reviewer-constraint presentation (`recommendReviewerAgents`, cross-family required/unavailable messaging judged against the seat above the one being assigned) predating this wizard. This task's job inside the redesign is carrying it forward intact — done via `create-session-step-2.tsx` reusing `SessionSeatAssignment` verbatim (re-homed, zero prop-contract changes), verified through the multi-seat e2e coverage in `multi-agent-session.spec.ts` now exercising seat assignment through the new Step 2.
- [x] 11.6 Implement Step 3 for recent/discovered project, remote workspace, branch, worktree, availability, and trust. — `create-session-step-3.tsx` reuses `LocalWorkspaceSection`/`RemoteWorkspaceSection` verbatim (re-homed only), which already cover recent/discovered project (`knownProjects`), remote workspace, branch/worktree (`worktreeEnabled`/`worktreeName`), and availability (`ProjectInspection.isGit`/`gitRoot` driving the git-vs-folder distinction). "Trust" has no backing concept anywhere in this app's data model — grepped `ProjectInspection`, `RemoteWorkspace`, and the SSH connection types for a trust/verification field and found none, so nothing was invented; a real trust signal (e.g. an unrecognized-host warning for a remote connection) needs its own design and backend support, documented as a known gap in the component's own doc comment rather than silently dropped.
- [x] 11.7 Implement Step 4 Review with runtime, participant, workspace, override, risk, and resource-consequence summary. — `create-session-step-4.tsx`: runtime/participant/workspace are direct restatements of Steps 1-3's choices. The other three needed honest interpretation since none names an existing draft field: "override" is the session title made genuinely last-mile editable right here via a real `<input>` (`onTitleChange`) — discovered mid-build that my first draft only *displayed* the title, dropping the old single-screen dialog's separate "Identity" section's editing capability entirely, caught via `session-helpers.ts`'s e2e helper failing to find the input and fixed before it shipped. "Risk" has no session-creation-time policy/permission field to summarize (that is post-creation, Settings-level — task 10.18's `effectiveExecutionPolicy` has no draft-time equivalent); the one concrete fact the draft does know — a remote workspace means executing on another host — is shown as a plain note, not invented urgency. "Resource-consequence" is stated as the literal side effects submission causes that the draft already knows about (a Git worktree created, an SSH connection saved, N Agent processes started for N seats), not a generic disclaimer. End-to-end verification: the wizard restructuring broke session-creation flow in every e2e spec that drives it to completion — 13 spec files identified via `getByPlaceholder(/code.*project/)`/`getByPlaceholder("新会话")` cross-referenced against wizard-navigation presence, all 13 fixed and independently re-run green (`session-helpers.ts`, `multi-agent-session.spec.ts`, `agent-plan-exit.spec.ts`, `agent-user-question.spec.ts`, `session-workspace-console.visual.spec.ts`, `onepiece-agent.spec.ts`, `file-reference-picker.spec.ts`, `local-media.spec.ts`, `onepiece-retrieval.spec.ts`, `slash-commands.spec.ts`, `usage-statistics.spec.ts`, `session-personalization-mode.spec.ts`, `skills-settings.spec.ts` — the last of which also uncovered and fixed an unrelated pre-existing gap where a test still targeted the old flat 9-tab "Skill" workspace tab §9's Session Overview migration had already replaced with an Inspector accordion section). A 14th file surfaced later via a full-suite run: `application-locales.spec.ts` asserted an orphaned `createSession.description` i18n key the wizard's step-counter text replaced (fixed; the dead key removed from all 5 locale files). That same full-suite run also re-surfaced `session-workspace-console.visual.spec.ts` with two more pre-existing, `redesign-unified-workbench-ui`-unrelated gaps in the same "predates §8/§9" family as the Skill-tab one above (confirmed via `git diff` that neither failing line was ever touched by this task group): its own "终端记录" click still targeted the primary workspace tablist instead of the closed-by-default Runtime Panel, and — once that was fixed — its content locator (`[id^="session-tab-panel-"]`) didn't match `RuntimeSurfaceShell`'s content at all, needing `getByRole("tabpanel", ...)` instead; separately, `holds the narrow layout under the longest localized labels` asserted a stale `toHaveCount(9)` against the primary tablist (now 4). Both fixed and confirmed via a clean single-worker re-run: the 2 desktop variants now pass; the 2 narrow variants and the Korean narrow test remain blocked by an already-documented, independently-confirmed-pre-existing bug unrelated to any of this session's work (`fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-black/50` session-sidebar Sheet scrim lingering after `createSession()` and intercepting a later click at narrow viewport widths) — left as-is rather than fixed, since it is out of this task's scope and already tracked.
- [x] 11.8 Allow backward navigation without losing valid draft values and reset only fields invalidated by an explicit mode change. — Structurally guaranteed, not just conventionally true: `useCreateSessionWizardSteps()` (11.3) takes zero arguments and returns pure step-position state, so `goBack`/`goNext`/`goToStep` cannot touch the draft reducer even by accident — there is no reference to it to touch.
