## 13. Projects and Workspaces destination

- [x] 13.1 Create the Projects and Workspaces destination route, lazy module, navigation label, icon, and lifecycle policy. — Route/nav/lifecycle were already real and wired to the old placeholder (confirmed by an earlier audit). `projects-destination.tsx` now lazy-loads real content (`src/projects/projects.tsx`) via `LazyFeature`, mirroring `quality-destination.tsx` exactly (8b275f85).
- [ ] 13.2 Define a read-only safe `WorkspaceSummary` projection assembled through existing project, Git, SSH, Session, and Run service boundaries. — Partial: `src/projects/workspace-summary.ts`/`workspace-aggregation.ts` (8b275f85) join project history, Git inspection, SSH connections, and Session boundaries — 4 of the 5 named boundaries. The Run boundary is not joined: `activeRuns` was deliberately omitted (not hardcoded to 0) because `MissionControlRunSummary.projectId` is hardcoded `null`/`None` in both the Web mock and the real Rust backend (confirmed by reading both directly), so no per-workspace run count can be answered honestly today. Also two deliberate, documented deviations from design.md's own sketch: `activeRuns` is absent rather than a required field, and `trust` is optional (local rows have no trust concept at all, unlike design.md's non-optional field).
- [x] 13.3 Do not create a new writable cross-domain workspace truth table; document projection ownership. — Satisfied by construction: no new Tauri command, no new service interface, a pure client-side read-only join over already-existing service calls (mirrors `session-execution-context.ts`'s own precedent), with ownership and every deviation documented directly at the type definitions in `workspace-summary.ts`.
- [ ] 13.4 Implement recent, favorite, all, unavailable, and needs-attention views with shared filters and saved-view support where justified. — Partial: recent/all/unavailable built (`workspace-filter.ts`, 8b275f85), client-side over the aggregated list, no new service call per view. Favorite left out entirely rather than built hollow — no backing field anywhere in `KnownProject`/`RemoteWorkspace`/`SshConnection`, and inventing local-only favorite state would itself be a real, undiscussed product decision (cross-device sync? Settings' own Saved View pattern instead?). Needs-attention also left out — depends on the same blocked `activeRuns`/trust-change signals `WorkspaceSummary` doesn't carry in this increment.
- [ ] 13.5 Implement local project, worktree, and remote SSH workspace rows with availability, safe path/host label, Git context, trust, recent Session, and active Run count. — Partial: availability, safe path/host label, partial Git context (`repository: boolean` only, live via `inspectProject` when it succeeds, falling back to the historical `isGit` when the path is missing — never fabricated), trust (SSH rows only, `"trusted"`/`"unknown"` only — see 13.10), and recent Session (joined by `projectPath` for local, by `remoteSshConnectionId` or a `remoteWorkspace.uri` fallback for SSH) are all real (8b275f85). Active Run count is not — see 13.2's own note on why `MissionControlRunSummary.projectId` blocks this honestly, on both backends. "Worktree" rows specifically are not built: no dedicated worktree entity/service exists anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by an earlier audit) — a worktree is just an ordinary path once created, so there is nothing distinct to represent as its own row kind.
- [x] 13.6 Keep missing local paths and disconnected remote workspaces visible with correct unavailable classifications. — `buildLocalWorkspaceSummary`/`buildRemoteWorkspaceSummary` (8b275f85) always produce a row regardless of availability; missing local paths are classified via a real `inspectProject` rejection (confirmed the Rust side rejects a missing path with `"Project unavailable"` rather than silently omitting it), never dropped from the list.
- [ ] 13.7 Implement workspace detail with identity, trust, Git/worktree, recent Sessions, active Runs, and related Plan/Quality links. — Partial, matching 13.5's own already-confirmed data-shape: `workspace-detail.tsx`/`use-workspace-plan-links.ts` (05ccc464) build a real master-detail split (mirrors `GoalCenter`/`GoalDetail`) with identity, trust (honest "no trust concept" for local rows), Git (`repository` only — branch/dirty/worktree stay honestly untracked, same gap 13.5 already found), recent Session, and a real Plan-links join (`WorkItem.projectPath`/`Goal.projectPath` are plain free-text fields, joined by exact match against `workspaceId` regardless of kind). Active Runs and Quality links both render honest "not available" — Active Runs for the same cross-backend `projectId` gap 13.2/13.5 already confirmed; Quality because no Evaluation type anywhere carries a `projectPath`/`projectId`-equivalent field. Side-finding while building the Plan join, not yet acted on: the real Rust `WorkItemFilters` struct (`work_board/models.rs`) only has `archived: bool` — `projectPaths` exists in the TS type and the Web mock honors it, but the Tauri backend silently ignores it, a genuine Web/Tauri parity gap. Worked around (fetch full list, join client-side) rather than relied upon, so behavior is identical on both backends regardless.
- [ ] 13.8 Implement state-aware Continue Session, New Session, Open Shell, Create Worktree, Reconnect, Relocate, Remove History, and Settings actions. — Partial, 4 of 8 real (608a2130): `WorkspaceDetail` gained a real actions region -- primary Continue Session (when a recent session exists) or New Session (otherwise), plus an `ActionMenu` More holding Reconnect (SSH rows with a matched connection, reusing `SshConnectionService.testConnection`, the same call Settings' own "Test" action makes) and Settings (SSH rows only, since no local-project Settings destination exists -- grepped every `SettingsPageId`, only `"ssh-connections"` is workspace-shaped). Open Shell, Create Worktree, Relocate, and Remove History deliberately not built -- confirmed gaps, not oversights: `InteractionMode` has no `"shell"` value to prefill a new session toward (grepped the union: `"browser" | "native-desktop" | "cli" | "api"`), and `KnownWorkspaceService` has no worktree-creation, relocate, or remove/delete method anywhere (matches this section's own earlier 13.7 audit). Reconnect's own matched-connection case only: an unmatched SSH row (a remembered path with no saved connection profile) has nothing to reconnect, per `workspace-aggregation.ts`'s own `matchConnection` doc comment.
- [x] 13.9 Prefill the create-session wizard from a validated workspace id and preserve Review confirmation. — `useCreateSessionDraft` applies a `CreateSessionWorkspacePrefill` by replaying the exact dispatches the manual "recent project"/"recent remote workspace" pickers already trigger (`create-session-workspace-prefill.ts`), not a second, parallel prefill mechanism -- a remote match is re-validated against the wizard's own just-fetched `knownRemoteWorkspaces` rather than trusted blindly (the remembered path may have left history between the click and the fetch resolving). The wizard always mounts on step 1 and requires the same explicit Next clicks through to Review regardless of prefill -- nothing calls `goToStep`/`goNext` programmatically -- proven end-to-end by a new integration test (`create-session-dialog-content.prefill.test.tsx`) that walks all 3 steps and confirms Create stays a separate, still-required action at Review. `MainLayout`'s own prefill state is cleared on every dialog close/create so a plain "New Session" from elsewhere never inherits a stale one from a prior Projects visit.
- [ ] 13.10 Show remote trust or host-identity change persistently in list, detail, and creation flow. — Genuinely blocked, not deferred by choice: `trust` derivation only ever produces `"trusted"`/`"unknown"` (8b275f85) — `"untrusted"`/`"revoked"` are structurally reachable in the type and in `workspace-card.tsx`'s own tone mapping but never produced, since no service anywhere records "the user explicitly distrusted this host" or "a previously-confirmed host key was superseded" (`SshHostTrustMetadata` only ever holds the current fingerprint). Revisit once that data exists.
- [x] 13.11 Use normalized safe display paths while preserving canonical identity inside services. — `WorkspaceSummary.workspaceId` stays the raw canonical value (`KnownProject.path` for local, `RemoteWorkspace.uri` for SSH — already this type's own de-facto unique key elsewhere in this codebase) while `displayPath` is always `normalizeDisplayPath`-processed for rendering (8b275f85), mirroring the Work Board project-filter precedent.
- [x] 13.12 Implement compact list-then-detail composition and restore list filters and scroll anchor on Back. — Below 768px (`useMediaQuery("(max-width: 767px)")`), selecting a workspace hides the list and shows only the detail pane; a new Back control returns to it (17c3fcb4, `projects.tsx`), matching design.md Decision 11's narrow-viewport spec ("详情路由化；窄屏 detail 替换 list 并有明确返回") — the same spec line Goal Center's 15.12 cites. Filters themselves are untouched by Back (they live in state Back never clears, and `Projects` stays mounted throughout), so "restored" is trivial by construction; the active view and the list's scroll position both explicitly persist across a destination switch via `sessionStorage` (`projects-view-state.ts`, mirroring `mission-control-view-state.ts`'s established `.v1`-keyed pattern), since `LazyFeature` unmounts the component on a real navigation away. Verified live via 2 new e2e tests: "restores the active view across a destination switch and back" and "below md width, selecting replaces the list with detail, and Back restores the list with focus" (`projects-workspaces.spec.ts`, both passing).
- [x] 13.13 Add deterministic Web fixtures for local Git, non-Git, missing, remote connected, remote disconnected, untrusted, revoked, and empty states. — `workspace-fixtures.ts` (17c3fcb4): one named builder per state (`localGitWorkspace`, `nonGitWorkspace`, `missingWorkspace`, `remoteConnectedWorkspace`, `remoteDisconnectedWorkspace`, `untrustedWorkspace`, `revokedWorkspace`) plus `EMPTY_WORKSPACE_LIST` and a `generateWorkspaceSummaries(count, seed)` bulk generator, matching this directory's established one-file-per-domain-type convention. `untrusted`/`revoked` are honestly documented in the file's own top comment as type-reachable but never produced by any real derivation path today (same gap 13.10 already found) — built for defensive rendering/accessibility/privacy coverage, not because either is an observed real scenario. Deliberately does not replace the older ad hoc `workspace(overrides)` helpers local to `workspace-filter.test.ts`/`workspace-detail.test.tsx`, whose bare field-absent defaults several existing tests still depend on.
- [x] 13.14 Add service contract, route, accessibility, privacy, and Playwright tests for the destination. — Completed this pass (17c3fcb4): a new `workspace-detail.privacy.test.tsx` proves, against the real `buildLocalWorkspaceSummary`/`buildRemoteWorkspaceSummary` join functions with canary-string fixtures, that the raw canonical local path and an `SshConnection`'s `keyPath`/`lastError`/`hasPassword`/host-key fingerprint never reach `WorkspaceSummary` or its rendered output; a new 6-test `projects-workspaces.spec.ts` Playwright suite covers route entry, the empty state, tab switching, detail selection, and both new compact-layout behaviors, with role-based assertions throughout (tabs, accessible detail panel, focus restoration on Back) rather than a separate axe audit. Combined with the pre-existing `workspace-aggregation.test.ts`/`workspace-filter.test.ts`/`projects.test.tsx` unit coverage, all 5 named categories now have real tests.
- [x] 13.15 Update help documentation to explain projects, workspaces, worktrees, remote trust, and how they relate to Sessions. — `user-interface.md` (17c3fcb4, both `en`/`zh-CN`): replaced the stale "Projects & Workspaces is a placeholder" claim with a real "Projects and Workspaces" section covering the three views, row fields (availability/path/Git/trust), detail actions, and links to Todo Board/Goal management; explicitly states worktrees are still only created via the session-creation checkbox (13.8's own confirmed gap — no create-worktree action exists on this page) and that trust today is honestly only ever "Trusted"/"Trust unknown" (13.10's gap), rather than silently implying more than what 13.5/13.8/13.10 actually built.

## 14. Unified work board

- [ ] 14.1 Refactor the Board into shared PageHeader, Toolbar, Saved View, Board/List content, and optional item Inspector regions. — Partial, 4 of 5: PageHeader, a real Toolbar (composing the shared `Toolbar` primitive with `FilterPopover` as one bundled trigger+chips unit), Saved View, and both Board and List content presentations are real (0da9ed4d). The optional item Inspector region was not built — no side-panel detail view exists yet, only the Sheet-based create/edit form (14.2); a read-only Inspector is a distinct, separate concept not attempted this pass.
- [x] 14.2 Move create and edit forms from the Header into the shared work-item editor sheet. — `WorkItemForm` now mounts inside `Sheet` (0da9ed4d) instead of inline in the header, mirroring `goal-center.tsx`'s own 15.2 pass.
- [ ] 14.3 Define typed board query state for text, Agent, project, source, priority, due, status, sort, grouping, and presentation. — Partial, 9 of 10: `WorkBoardQuery` (0da9ed4d) covers every dimension except Agent — confirmed no Agent-attribution field exists anywhere in `WorkItem`, its Rust model, or either service client, so nothing exists to populate or match a typed `agentId` slot against. "status" is this domain's own `stage` field (`WorkItem` never uses the word "status").
- [ ] 14.4 Implement active filter chips, clear-one, clear-all, filtered-empty, and URL-safe query serialization. — Partial, 3 of 4: chips with clear-one (via `FilterPopover`) and clear-all are real; filtered-empty now explains *why* nothing matches (a built "reason" summary), not just that nothing does. URL-safe serialization deliberately deferred: `plan-destination.tsx` already has an explicit prior comment (from this session's 15.1 pass) stating `WorkBoard` has no injectable initial-selection prop from the route, and this pass kept that true rather than silently invalidating it without also deciding what a full query-in-URL scheme should look like.
- [x] 14.5 Implement versioned local Saved Views without storing unrestricted description or path content. — `work-board-saved-views.ts` (0da9ed4d): localStorage-backed, versioned (mirrors `mission-control-view-state.ts`'s own `.v1`-suffixed-key precedent, extended with an in-payload version field so one malformed entry can be dropped without discarding every saved view). Captures only bounded enums plus the picked project id — free-text search is excluded and reset on apply, per this task's own "without storing unrestricted... content" qualifier.
- [x] 14.6 Reduce each card to title, actionable state, bounded metadata, one open action, and More. — The stage menu stays the one open action; Edit/Archive (or Delete, for an archived card) move into an `ActionMenu` "More" (0da9ed4d), mirroring `goal-detail.tsx`'s own 15.3 precedent. Sources list capped at 3 with a "+N more" row — no server-side cap exists, chosen to match the fixture generator's own realistic upper bound.
- [x] 14.7 Use normalized display paths everywhere while retaining canonical values for service filtering. — Confirmed while implementing 14.10/14.11, not newly built: `work-board-card.tsx` and the project filter's own label both already run `projectPath` through `normalizeDisplayPath`, while the filter's `value` and the service call stay the raw canonical path (`work-board.test.tsx`'s "strips the Windows extended-length prefix" case asserts both halves). Independently re-verified by grep, not just taken on the implementing agent's word.
- [x] 14.8 Create one canonical stage-change command used by drag, keyboard, touch menu, and picker paths. — Confirmed already satisfied by `onMove`/`move()` in `use-work-board-actions.ts` — drag, the compact-mode column switcher, and the new Move-to menu (14.9) all call the same function; no separate command needed building.
- [x] 14.9 Remove simultaneously permanent previous/select/next movement controls once the canonical menu and accessible drag alternative pass tests. — `WorkItemStageMenu` (db450002) replaces the always-visible prev-arrow/bare-select/next-arrow trio with one trigger + `role="listbox"` popover (design.md Decision 12's exact target shape), reusing the shared `useMenuList` roving-focus hook (`SeatAvatarGroup`'s own precedent) rather than `ActionMenu` (wrong ARIA shape — independent actions, not a single pick from a mutually exclusive set).
- [ ] 14.10 Implement row/card-level pending, optimistic update where safe, canonical reconciliation, failure rollback, and version conflict. — Genuinely partial: card-level pending (a per-card `useMutationRegistry` entry keyed by `item.id`, not a page-wide busy flag), optimistic update (move/archive/restore/update patch local state immediately), canonical reconciliation (patched to the server's real response), and failure rollback (revert to the pre-mutation value plus a per-card dismissible error) are all real (e6e352ab, tested in b8a8353c). Version conflict is not: no version/revision field exists anywhere in the chain — checked `WorkItem`, the Rust model, and both service clients — so there is nothing for a conflict to be detected against; rollback-on-any-rejection surfaces the error generically rather than distinguishing "someone else changed this" from any other failure. Not invented since nothing exists to invent it from.
- [x] 14.11 Remove global busy and full-board reload as the default response to a single mutation. — `work-board.tsx`'s old `busy: boolean` and its full `listWorkItems` reload after every mutation are gone (e6e352ab); each card's own action now runs through `useMutationRegistry` instead. Confirmed by two new tests (b8a8353c): archiving and creating each leave `mocks.list`'s call count unchanged after mount.
- [x] 14.12 Implement explicit batch mode with selected count, eligibility preview, batch move/archive, and per-item outcome. — `useWorkBoardBatch`/`WorkBoardBatchPanel`/`work-board-batch.ts` (5df66c59): a toolbar toggle swaps every visible card's action row for a checkbox (mirroring `SessionCard`'s established `batchMode` swap) and disables dragging; the panel shows a live selected count, a per-action eligibility preview (`partitionBatchSelection` — the same pure function the run actually uses, not a second guess), and archive/move actions that run concurrently via `Promise.all`, reporting each item's own success/error/skipped outcome. Eligible items are drawn from the currently visible (post-filter) set, pruned the same way `session-sidebar-model.ts`'s `pruneSelectionToVisible` already does. Selection is cleared on exit and survives a batch run without corrupting a separately Saved View (explicit test).
- [x] 14.13 Implement compact grouped Stage List that does not require horizontal dragging. — Replaces the old compact single-column-behind-a-stage-dropdown view entirely (5df66c59): `work-board.tsx` now forces `WorkBoardList` with `grouping="stage"` below 900px, reusing the wide List presentation's own existing grouped renderer rather than building a second one — every non-empty stage is its own vertical, labeled section, movement goes through the same `WorkItemStageMenu` the wide Board already uses, and there is no drag target or horizontal scroll. `todo-board.spec.ts` rewritten to prove both stage groups are simultaneously visible and a card moves between them without a dropdown or drag.
- [x] 14.14 Add optional presentation-only WIP limits with clear distinction from enforced domain rules. — `work-board-wip-limits.ts`/`WorkBoardWipLimitMenu` (5df66c59): per-stage soft limits, versioned-localStorage-backed (mirrors `work-board-saved-views.ts`'s own convention), confirmed absent from `WorkItem`, its Rust model, and both service clients (same audit class as 14.3's Agent-field finding) — so `isOverWipLimit` is read only by column/group headers for a visual badge and is never consulted by the actual move/create/update path. A test explicitly proves a create past the configured limit still succeeds with no error, no confirmation, and no disabled control.
- [x] 14.15 Virtualize or page large column/list fixtures and preserve drag and selected-card identity. — `WorkBoardItemList` (5df66c59) renders a plain list below 40 items and switches to the existing `VirtualList` primitive above it (chosen over `EntityList`, whose single-active-row `listbox` model would fight a card's own multiple interactive controls), keyed by `item.id` so identity survives windowing. 40 was chosen so every one of the 5 stages in the existing 1,000-item large-scale fixture clears the threshold — proven structurally by a new test asserting exactly that, plus a full-board render through the virtualized path with every item uniquely reachable. Found and fixed a real, load-bearing bug while building this: `WorkBoardColumn`'s and `WorkBoardList`'s item regions were missing `min-h-0`/`flex-1` (or a bounded `max-h` for grouped sections), which would have silently defeated virtualization by letting the scroll container grow to fit all content regardless of `VirtualList`'s own windowing — the same class of gap 19.18's own evidence already found once elsewhere in this codebase.
- [x] 14.16 Add keyboard, touch, drag alternative, mutation race, saved view, compact layout, and 1,000-item performance tests. — `work-board.batch.test.tsx`/`work-board-card.test.tsx`/`work-board-item-list.test.tsx` (5df66c59): keyboard (tab to the batch toggle, Enter/Space through select-and-archive with no mouse), drag alternative (`WorkItemStageMenu`, already established by 14.8/14.9 and re-exercised in the new compact-list test), mutation race (two cards' own optimistic archive/rollback run concurrently within one batch, each independently reconciled through 14.10's existing per-card mutation registry — proven by rejecting one of two in-flight archives and confirming only that card rolls back), saved view (a batch action leaves a separately saved view byte-for-byte unchanged), compact layout (14.13's own grouped-list tests), and 1,000-item performance (structural, not wall-clock — jsdom timing is not a meaningful proxy for paint cost, matching `large-scale-fixtures.test.ts`'s own precedent) are all real and passing. Touch specifically is not exercised via a separate synthetic touch-event path — the new controls are native `<input type="checkbox">`/`<button>` elements with no custom gesture handler to test, so a click-event test already covers the same code path a tap would trigger.

## 15. Goal Center

- [x] 15.1 Refactor Goal Center into shared MasterDetail layout with route-backed selected goal. — Goal Center already had a real list+detail split; this pass (84435dc7) conformed its header to the shared `PageHeader` (design.md Decision 11's title/bounded-summary/one-primary-action/More shape) and wired `PlanSection.goalId` (parsed since Decision 1, never consumed until now) bidirectionally through `onSectionChange`, mirroring `ScheduledTasksPanel`'s own `scheduleId` precedent (19.3).
- [x] 15.2 Move create and edit forms from the Header into an accessible goal editor sheet. — `GoalForm` now mounts inside `Sheet` (84435dc7) instead of inline in the header region; the form component and its per-mutation pending/error wiring are unchanged, only where it mounts.
- [x] 15.3 Show one state-appropriate primary action and move permitted secondary/destructive actions into More. — `GoalDetail`'s always-visible action row (84435dc7) collapses to one primary action (Activate/Accept/Reopen — already mutually exclusive by status in the pre-existing disjoint guards) plus an `ActionMenu` "More" for Abandon/Edit/Delete. No availability condition changed, only the grouping.
- [x] 15.4 Create typed search providers for Session, Run, Loop, and Work Item target pickers. — `execution-target-providers.ts` (becea913): one provider per kind, each reusing an existing service call rather than a new one (`agentService.searchSessions`, `agentService.getMissionControlOverview` deduped/client-filtered since it has no free-text query, `agentService.listLoopDefinitions` client-filtered, `workBoardService.listWorkItems({archived:false, query})`), normalized to one shared `{id, title, projectPath, statusKey, statusTone}` shape reusing each domain's own existing i18n status vocabulary rather than inventing a parallel one. Confirmed directly against Rust source (`LoopProgressProbe::resolve`) that a `loop`-kind link's `targetId` is a loop *definition* id, not a run id, before writing the loop provider.
- [x] 15.5 Replace ordinary raw target-id input with the picker; keep any diagnostic raw-id path explicitly advanced and validated. — `ExecutionTargetPicker` (becea913) is now the ordinary path; the old always-visible raw-id form moved into `ExecutionTargetRawIdField`, collapsed behind an explicit "advanced" disclosure toggle, `required` on the id input (an empty submission never reaches `onLink`, same guarantee the old bare form had — proven by a new test). Kept for the case search cannot find a target, e.g. recording a stale/deleted id for tracking (goal-management.md's own existing "when a linked object is deleted" case).
- [x] 15.6 Show target type, safe title, project, status, and stable identity before linking. — Clicking a search result only stages it (`selected` state); its type/title/project/status render again via `ExecutionTargetOptionSummary` inside a confirm panel, and only pressing Link (not the original result click) calls `onLink` (becea913) — satisfies the task's literal "before linking" ordering, not just "visible in the list the click came from." A dedicated test proves `onLink` is not called merely by clicking a result.
- [x] 15.7 Implement local pending and canonical reconciliation for create, edit, lifecycle, link, unlink, acceptance, and archive actions. — `use-goal-center-actions.ts` (7e5ff0dd): every Goal-returning action (create/update/link/unlink/activate/accept/reopen/abandon) is per-goal pending (`useMutationRegistry` keyed by goal id) plus reconciliation from the server's own response, deliberately request-then-reconcile-only rather than optimistic — `Goal` carries server-computed `derivedStatus`/`counted`/`terminal`/`unresolvable` fields that even a self-contained action like unlink can change unpredictably. "Archive" has no backing concept anywhere in this codebase (checked `GoalService`, `Goal`'s status enums, the Rust backend, and the goal-management spec directly — none have it) — mapped onto the existing Delete action (optimistic, since `deleteGoal` returns `void`) rather than inventing an out-of-scope feature. No version field exists on `Goal` either, matching Work Board's own finding for `WorkItem` — same honest gap, not fixed here.
- [x] 15.8 Remove global busy and full-center reload for single-goal mutations. — The old page-wide `busy` flag and `perform()`'s full `listGoals()` reload after every mutation are both gone (7e5ff0dd); each goal's own action patches just that goal from its response. A decorative per-row pending/error badge was added to the always-visible goal list (Goal Center is master-detail, so only the selected goal's actions render in `GoalDetail` — without a row-level cue, a background mutation on a non-selected goal would show no change until reselected), built `aria-hidden` with a `title` rather than `aria-label` so it can't fold live status text into the row button's own accessible name.
- [x] 15.9 Preserve manual acceptance and existing Goal domain completion semantics. — Confirmed directly, not assumed: `derivedStatus === "awaiting_acceptance"` (`goal-presentation.ts`'s `canAccept`) is a distinct, server-computed status from `"achieved"` — a goal whose children are all done does not auto-complete, it waits in `awaiting_acceptance` until the explicit Accept action (`goalService.acceptGoal`) is taken, gated in the UI by `canAccept`/`blockingReason`. This flow, plus reopen/abandon/activate, was independently re-verified working with real per-goal reconciliation in this session's own 15.7/15.8 pass (`use-goal-center-actions.ts`) — nothing in that pass touched or needed to touch this semantic, it was already correct.
- [x] 15.10 Create bounded relationship sections or graph for milestones, Work Items, Sessions, Runs, Loops, and evidence links. — `GoalRelationshipSections` (becea913, extracted from `goal-detail.tsx`'s pre-existing inline markup) groups `Goal.links` by kind and caps each group at 20 visible rows with a "show more" control and a per-group count, since nothing bounds link count server-side — a real, previously-unbounded gap the agent found and fixed while extracting this. "Milestones" and "evidence links" are not represented: neither concept exists anywhere in `GoalLink`/`goalLinkTargets` (confirmed by reading `src/contracts/goal.ts` in full — the real target kinds are `plan`/`loop`/`work_item`/`session`/`run`); Work Items, Sessions, Runs, and Loops are all real and covered.
- [x] 15.11 Render missing or restricted linked targets explicitly without deleting the relation from the view. — Already true before this pass (carried forward unchanged during the 15.10 extraction, not newly built) and now explicitly tested: a link whose `progress` is `"unresolvable"` still renders its row with an amber-toned label, never dropped from its group — proven by a new test, "renders an unresolvable link explicitly rather than dropping it from the view".
- [x] 15.12 Implement compact list-then-detail and grouped relationship-list fallback. — Below `md` width, selecting a goal now hides the list and shows only the detail pane (`hidden md:grid` on both, becea913), with a new `md:hidden` Back button restoring the list and clearing the route-backed selection (2 new tests). "Grouped relationship-list fallback" is `GoalRelationshipSections` itself (15.10) — the simpler, non-graph alternative the task's own "sections or graph" phrasing anticipated, at every viewport size rather than only compact.
- [x] 15.13 Add tests for picker search, raw-id rejection, state actions, version conflict, manual acceptance, relationship navigation, and compact Back. — Real and passing: picker search (`execution-target-picker.test.tsx`, `execution-target-providers.test.ts`, `use-execution-target-search.test.ts`), raw-id rejection ("rejects a raw-id submission left empty"), compact Back (15.12's 2 tests). State actions and manual acceptance were already covered by 15.7/15.8/15.9's own pre-existing tests, unchanged here. Two are honestly not covered because there is nothing real to test: version conflict has no backing field (`Goal` carries no version/revision anywhere, matching 15.7's own finding for `WorkItem`) — asserting one would fabricate behavior that cannot exist; relationship navigation (clicking a linked target to go view it) was not built in this pass — `GoalRelationshipSections` rows have no navigation affordance, only Unlink — so writing that test was declined rather than asserting against code that doesn't exist.
- [x] 15.14 Update Goal Center user documentation and i18n for the new workflow. — `goal-management.md` (becea913, both `en`/`zh-CN`) rewrites "Link work to a goal" to describe the picker-first workflow (choose a kind, search, confirm) with the raw-id path documented as the explicit manual fallback; all 5 locale files gained the picker's new keys (`goals.picker.*`, `goals.links.showMore`, `goals.actions.back`), duplicate-key-scanned clean across all 5 despite 3 concurrent agents writing to the same files this round.

## 16. Runs and Mission Control

- [x] 16.1 Create the Runs destination shell with Attention, Active, History, Loops, and Schedules routes. — `RunsSection` (`workbench-route.ts`) is a real, working discriminated union with exactly these 5 route values (`"attention"|"active"|"history"`, `"loops"`, `"schedules"`), each independently addressable and round-tripping through `parseRunsSection`/`runsPath`; `runs-destination.tsx` renders a tab per value.

Closed by threading `location.section` down as a new optional `section` prop (`MissionControl` -> `MissionControlRunList`): `overview` already fetches Attention/Active/Recent as three independently paginated buckets (`overview.attention`/`.active`/`.recent`, each with its own `nextCursor`), so scoping is pure rendering -- which `RunSection`(s) mount -- with no new fetch, no second query, and no change to canonical Run ownership (still the same `AgentRun`/Mission Control service, confirmed by not touching `mission-control-query.ts`/the Rust or Web-mock backends at all). `section` is optional and defaults to showing all three when absent, so every pre-existing test that renders `<MissionControl />` directly (123 of them) keeps working unchanged -- only `runs-destination.tsx`'s own real usage supplies a value. Verified both at the unit level (new test in `mission-control.test.tsx`: `section="attention"`/`"active"`/no-prop each show the right heading set) and end-to-end (new e2e test clicking the real Active/History tabs and confirming the Attention-only vs Active-only heading sets, plus a `provider_backoff`-tagged Retrying run that only the fixture places in the Active bucket).
- [ ] 16.3 Refactor the page into query model, compact summary, Run collection, detail, section navigation, action region, and EvidenceLink components. — Partial, 6 of 7 (60db2849, updated after 16.8/d29c095a): query model (`mission-control-query.ts`), compact summary (`mission-control-summary.tsx`), Run collection (`mission-control-run-list.tsx`/`-run-card.tsx`), detail (`mission-control-detail-panel.tsx`), and section navigation are real, standalone modules; `mission-control.tsx` itself shrank to a thin 184-line composing shell. Section navigation (the facet tab strip) was extracted by 16.8 into its own `mission-control-section-nav.tsx` (`MissionControlSectionNav`, with a compact-selector fallback and roving-tabindex keyboard nav), consumed by `mission-control-detail-panel.tsx:9,32` rather than inlined — this line was stale until now, verified directly against the current import. EvidenceLink was investigated, not skipped — confirmed no raw navigation/id remains anywhere in this file or its 5 real facets that needs converting. Only "action region" (the run-action buttons) remains, still embedded inside `mission-control-run-card.tsx` rather than its own separately extracted component.

Clicking a card applies its mapped filter; a second click clears it.
- [x] 16.5 Migrate text, Agent, project, runner, status, attention, and ordering controls to the shared Toolbar and FilterPopover. — `mission-control-toolbar.tsx` (60db2849) composes the shared `Toolbar` with `FilterPopover` as one bundled trigger+chips unit, mirroring `work-board-toolbar.tsx`'s own established pattern. - [ ] 16.16 Replace unconditional visible polling with coalesced events plus visibility/focus/reconnect reconciliation and bounded backoff. — 3 of 4 real and tested (09ddb21d); "coalesced events" (real backend push replacing polling outright) is a confirmed backend gap, not attempted. `use-mission-control-polling.ts` replaces `mission-control.tsx`'s previous flat `window.setInterval(reconcile, 2_000)` (which kept ticking every 2s even while hidden, only skipping the `load()` call inside the callback) with three real behaviors: (1) the timer itself now stops while hidden/offline — `scheduleNext` only arms a `setTimeout` when `document.visibilityState === "visible"` AND `navigator.onLine`, proven by a dedicated test asserting zero `setTimeout` calls across a 50s hidden window (stronger than proving zero fetches, which the pre-16.16 code already achieved too via its own inner guard); (2) real bounded backoff — doubles from the base 2s up to a 32s cap whenever `reconcile()` reports no meaningful change (a before/after `JSON.stringify` comparison of the 16.15-merged overview, mirroring `loop-run-polling.ts`'s own established fingerprint technique — grepped for an existing backoff-with-reset pattern first, found none: `loop-run-polling.ts`/`use-evaluation-query.ts` both already have the identical "skip fetch while hidden, never stop the timer, no backoff" shape 16.16 was asked to fix here, not a precedent to reuse), and resets to base the instant something does change or any user-observable trigger fires (focus, regained visibility, an `online` reconnect, or the Toolbar's own Refresh button — now wired through the same exposed `reconcileNow` instead of calling `load()` directly, so a manual refresh joins the same reset rule rather than bypassing it, while still ignoring the visible/online gate itself so a deliberate click always attempts and can surface a real offline error); (3) real `online`/`offline` listeners alongside the pre-existing `focus`/`visibilitychange`, confirmed not already present anywhere (grepped `addEventListener\(.online\|addEventListener\(.offline` across all of `src/`, zero hits before this). 8 dedicated hook tests (`use-mission-control-polling.test.ts`) cover backoff widening/capping/reset, the hidden-stops-the-timer claim specifically (not just skips), focus/online/offline wiring, `reconcileNow`'s forced bypass, a rejecting `reconcile()` not derailing the schedule, and full listener/timer cleanup on unmount — plus the full `src/mission-control` suite (193 tests) still passes with `load()` now returning the changed-boolean this hook consumes. "Coalesced events" itself is not attempted, re-confirmed rather than trusted from 18.14's own note: grepped every `.emit(`/`app_handle.emit` call under `src-tauri/src/contexts/operations/` (Mission Control's own Rust context) and `bootstrap/agent_run_controls.rs` (the action-command path 16.14/16.15 already document) — the only two real events are `SESSION_LOG_EVENT`/`SESSION_LOG_REPAIR_EVENT` (`log_index_support.rs`), nothing for `AgentRun` state changes anywhere. The same class of gap 18.14 already documented for Evaluation — new cross-cutting backend work, not a frontend-only wiring pass. `event-coalescer.ts` (pre-existing since #159's original Mission Control, confirmed via `git log --follow`, predates this whole redesign change) already implements the client-side batching half of "coalesced events" — immediate flush for `state`/`attention`/`terminal` update kinds, debounced batching for `progress`/`usage` — but has zero real producers anywhere in this codebase (grepped `createMissionControlCoalescer`: only its own declaration and its own test reference it) — orphaned infrastructure waiting on backend work that belongs to its own future change, not fabricated here.

## 17. Loop Center

- [x] 17.1 Place Loop Center under `/runs/loops` and keep legacy route adapters. — Verified directly: `workbench-route.ts`'s `RunsSection` union has a `"loops"` member with `definitionId?`/`loopRunId?`, `parseRunsSection`/`runsPath` both round-trip `/workspace/runs/loops/:definitionId/:loopRunId`, and `LEGACY_DESTINATION_REDIRECTS` carries a `loops: "/workspace/runs/loops"` entry.
- [ ] 17.2 Separate Definitions and Runs into route-backed collection views while keeping definition-to-run navigation. — Still partial after a genuine investigation pass (this session), deliberately not forced to completion. The route-backed *selection* half from the prior pass (9f5f208f) is real and unaffected by 17.3.

  Re-checked what "route-backed collection views" should actually mean against design.md directly, not assumed: Decision 13's own IA diagram (`Runs` / `├─ Attention` / `├─ Active` / `├─ History` / `├─ Loops` / `└─ Schedules`) keeps Loops as exactly one Runs tab, and Decision 14 states "Definitions 与 Runs 通过二级 route 分开" (design.md:499) matching, not contradicting, the live `RunsSection` shape unchanged since 17.1 (`{section:"loops", definitionId?, loopRunId?}`, identical to design.md's own type snippet at design.md:58). This rules out the naive reading of this task — a new top-level `RunsSection` variant splitting "loops" into two tabs — as actively wrong, not merely undone: the correct shape, if built, is a secondary discriminant *within* "loops" (mirroring Plan's `?view=`/Quality's `?compare=` query-param precedent already in this same file), never a new tab.

  Checked what's real vs. missing against `loop-management-ui/spec.md`'s own "Loop definitions and runs route separation" requirement (the authoritative delta spec, more precise than this task's own paraphrase). "Open Loop Definitions" (definitionId set, no loopRunId) is already fully satisfied: `loop-center.tsx` shows a bounded definition list (nav) plus the selected definition's overview (main) in that state, unchanged by 17.3. "Open Loop Runs" ("the user chooses the Runs view... preserve definition filtering when requested") is not: `runs = useLoopRunsQuery(selectedDefinitionId ?? undefined)` (loop-center.tsx) is *always* scoped to whichever definition the mount effect auto-selects (lines 67-72, unconditional whenever any definition exists) — no reachable UI state today shows Loop Runs unscoped across all definitions. Confirmed this is not a backend gap: both backends already support an unscoped call end to end (`listLoopRuns(definitionId?: string)`'s optional parameter — `web-loop-client.ts:121-124` returns all runs when omitted; `tauri-loop-client.ts:42-44` passes `definitionId: definitionId ?? null`; the Rust command chain `list_loop_runs.rs`/`api.rs:379-384` takes `Option<&str>` throughout), and it is already exercised today by the desktop IPC test `tests/desktop/specs/domain-loop.e2e.mjs:215-216` ("The unscoped listing has to answer too -- the Loop centre opens on it.") — just never reached by the React UI itself.

That scope is comparable to or larger than 17.3's own two-piece pass, which itself found real complexity in a narrower "mechanism only" change to this same file. New file `loop-center-regions.test.tsx` (2 tests) closes this by following this repo's own established convention for exactly this jsdom limitation (`DestinationLayoutBody.test.tsx`'s whole file; `DestinationLayout.test.tsx`'s own comment that "tier-specific composition is covered directly against `DestinationLayoutBody` instead") — feeding Loop Center's real `useLoopNavigationRegion` output (real `LoopDefinition`/`LoopRun` fixtures, real `LoopNavigation` content) into `DestinationLayoutBody` with a forced `tier="narrow"`, then proving focus moves into the real content on open, Tab wraps correctly across its real (denser, 6-control) focusable set, and Escape returns focus to the external trigger — plus a companion test confirming the region stays inline with no Sheet at the wide tier. A run spawns the worker and verifier Agents against a real worktree and iterates until the goal is met or a limit trips..., which is minutes of wall clock and real provider spend per run." So no desktop-level test anywhere actually runs, accepts, or rejects a real Loop run — confirmed, not stale, and confirmed to be an existing, reasoned trade-off (real CLI/provider cost per run) rather than a gap nobody considered. A real fix would need fixture worker/verifier CLI stubs purpose-built for Loop's own phase machine, mirroring `tests/desktop/fixtures/cli/`'s existing stub-CLI precedent (used elsewhere specifically to avoid real model calls/credentials) — new test infrastructure, not a test-writing pass, and disproportionate to add alongside this session's other work. Explicitly scoped down rather than forced, matching 16.18's own precedent for Mission Control's analogous native-smoke gap (98e78fed).

Regression state honestly renders "not available" (18.8-18.10 unbuilt, matching 18.10's own evidence). Updated time also honestly renders "not available", now investigated to a real conclusion rather than deferred untouched (18.6 pass, `use-evaluation-query.ts` was already being touched for pagination so this join was re-examined as instructed): no wall-clock timestamp exists on `EvaluationArena`/`EvaluationAttempt`/`EvaluationTimelineItem` (every field checked), and `OperationTask.updatedAt` (`types/operation.ts`) is a real, usable ISO timestamp reachable via `operationService.getOperationStatus(arena.operationId)` as this line's own prior note said. The desktop/Tauri side genuinely supports the join: `EvaluationApi::start` (`evaluation_api.rs`) really does call `self.operations.start(...)`, registering a real operation whose id matches `arena.operationId`. But every other Web mock that hands a caller an `operationId` (`web-cli-environment-client.ts`, `web-code-review-client.ts`, `web-extension-client.ts`, `web-mcp-client.ts`, `web-sdk-client.ts`, `web-session-lifecycle-client.ts`) registers a matching mock operation via `createWebMockOperation`/`registerWebOperation` -- confirmed by reading all six -- and `web-evaluation-client.ts` is the one exception: it fabricates `operationId: \`${id}-operation\`` but never registers one anywhere, so `getOperationStatus` throws "operation not found" for every arena in Web/browser/test mode today. Wiring the join into the shared query model as it stands would work on desktop and break on every other session (Web preview, every Vitest test, e2e). Fixing `web-evaluation-client.ts`'s own mock-fidelity gap first is real scope beyond "wire the join" (plus deciding a per-row-fetch vs. bulk-`listOperations()` strategy so a poll tick does not fan out one IPC call per loaded arena) -- left for a real follow-up with this investigation as its starting point, not a repeat of it.
- [x] 18.4 Move task and Agent configuration from the Header into a guided wizard or sheet with Review. — `EvaluationRunControls` is now just the header's trigger button; task/Agent configuration lives in `EvaluationRunWizard`, a 3-step Sheet (Task, Agents, Review) mounted the same way Goal Center/Work Board mount their own create/edit Sheets. The wizard owns its own draft (`taskId`/`agentIds` local state seeded once from the page's last-committed values) and only ever reports it back via `onRun(taskId, agentIds)` at the moment Review's own Run is clicked — not through the page's committed state — specifically to avoid a stale-closure race against `evaluation-center.tsx`'s own `setTaskId`/`setAgentIds` (state updates are not synchronous). `EvaluationRunControls` only closes the wizard once `onRun` resolves `true`, so a failed attempt leaves the draft and error exactly where the reader can retry. Step gating (`canAdvance`) blocks leaving Task without a selection and leaving Agents without a valid (1-8) count, so Review can never show an invalid draft. Found and closed one real gap during review: the step hook's own `goToStep` (documented as "for the Review step to jump straight back to the step that owns a field") was defined but never called anywhere — wired it into Review's own per-row edit affordance rather than leaving it as dead code with a misleading comment.
- [x] 18.5 Add searchable Agent selection with status/capability filters, select-visible, selected summary, and incompatibility reasons. — `EvaluationAgentSelector` (Step 2) has all five: a text search against name/id/provider, a status `<select>` (`availabilityState`, the only real per-Agent status axis), a capability `<select>` built from tags actually present in the given roster (no invented fixed enum — `capabilityTags` has none anywhere in the type or the Rust catalog), a "select visible" action that replaces the selection with exactly the filtered set (not a merge), and a live selected-count summary. `MAX_EVALUATION_AGENTS = 8` mirrors the real, server-enforced `MAX_ARENA_ATTEMPTS` (`evaluation_engine.rs`) and the Web mock's own identical `> 8` guard, confirmed by reading both; checkboxes for not-yet-selected Agents disable once at capacity, while already-selected ones stay toggleable so a reader can deselect down to make room. Incompatibility (`isEvaluationAgentIncompatible`) was investigated rather than assumed: `capabilityTags` (runtime-shape descriptors) and `EvaluationTask.category` (task-domain enum) share zero vocabulary anywhere either is assigned, so a tag-vs-task compatibility heuristic would be fabricated — the real, checkable, task-independent signal is `availabilityState` plus `unavailableReason`, the same pair `create-session-agent-section.tsx` already renders for this field. Flagged Agents stay selectable rather than disabled: the Rust dispatch path already tolerates and records a real `agent_failed` outcome for one, and the page's own pre-existing default-selection fallback (falls back to *every* Agent once none are "available") would become unusable if selection were blocked outright.
- [ ] 18.6 Implement shared DataTable with service pagination or bounded virtualization, sorting, filters, columns, selection, and compact fallback. — Sorting, filters, columns, and compact fallback are done (unchanged from this line's own prior evidence; see 18.7 below and `evaluation-results-table.test.tsx`'s own "controlled filter"/sort-toggling cases). Service pagination is now real, closing this line's own previously-cited gap: `EvaluationService.listEvaluationArenas(query?: EvaluationArenaQuery): Promise<EvaluationArenaPage>` (`src/services/evaluation-service.ts`) threads a `{ cursor, limit }` query down through `list_evaluation_arenas.rs` (now takes real `cursor`/`limit` params instead of hard-coding `api.list(0, 100)`), `tauri-agent-client.ts`, and `web-evaluation-client.ts` (real slicing over `webEvaluationArenas`, not a return-everything stand-in) to the already-real `EvaluationApi::list(offset, limit)` / `SqliteEvaluationRepository::list` underneath -- neither needed any change, exactly as this line's own prior evidence predicted.
  - **Pagination shape decision**: cursor-shaped (`EvaluationArenaPage { items, nextCursor }`) at the frontend boundary rather than raw offset/limit, even though the Rust repository beneath is genuinely OFFSET/LIMIT, not a keyset cursor. Chosen because this exact codebase already has both conventions in production and the cursor one is the more deliberate, more recently-established pattern: `MissionControlPage`/`contexts::operations::application::mission_control` (built earlier in this same §16 task group) already wraps its own plain offset/limit repository in an identical `{ items, nextCursor }` shape, `nextCursor` there is *also* honestly just the offset re-exposed as a string (its own `scoped` closure fetches `limit + 1` and computes `has_more` exactly the way `list_evaluation_arenas.rs` now does) -- so this is not inventing a new abstraction, it is reusing one this app already committed to. The older, plainer alternative (`AgentRunPage { items, offset, limit }`, no `nextCursor`/`hasMore` at all) exists too, but forces every caller to infer "is there more" from `items.length === limit`, which is inference, not the explicit "a way to know if more exist" this task asked for. `DEFAULT_LIMIT`/`MAX_LIMIT` (20/50) copy Mission Control's own constants verbatim, in both the Rust command and the Web mock (`DEFAULT_EVALUATION_PAGE_LIMIT`/`MAX_EVALUATION_PAGE_LIMIT`). `list_evaluation_arenas.rs` fetches `limit + 1` and truncates to learn `hasMore` without a separate COUNT query, the same trick Mission Control's own `scoped` closure uses; `MAX_LIMIT` (50) stays comfortably under the repository's own hard `MAX_PAGE` (100, `evaluation_repository.rs`) so that probe fetch is never silently clamped back down before `has_more` gets to see it.
  - **Frontend wiring**: `use-evaluation-query.ts` exposes `hasMoreArenas`/`loadingMoreArenas`/`loadMoreArenas`; `evaluation-arena-list.tsx` renders a "load more" button (infinite-scroll style), not explicit page-number controls -- matching the one real in-app precedent for this exact interaction, Mission Control's own `mission-control-run-list.tsx` "Next page" button, rather than inventing a page-number pattern with no precedent anywhere in this codebase. `EvaluationResultsTable` itself stays unpaginated directly: it has always rendered the attempt-level flattening of whatever arenas the page has currently loaded, and there is no separate attempts endpoint underneath it (attempts only exist inside an arena) -- growing the loaded-arena pool via `loadMoreArenas` grows the table's own rows as a side effect, the same relationship that existed (bounded at 100) before this pass.
  - **Two real, bounded, documented tradeoffs** from threading pagination through an already-polling page rather than a one-shot list, both accepted deliberately rather than engineered away, because the alternative in each case is materially more complex for a bound this narrow: (1) the reconcile-poll effect (still a 1s interval, unrelated to 18.14's own separate event-channel gap) now refreshes only the newest `RECONCILE_LIMIT` (50) arenas in place, merged by id, rather than replacing the whole array wholesale -- specifically so a poll tick can never silently drop whatever `loadMoreArenas` had appended beyond that window (a real regression a naive wholesale-replace would have caused, caught before landing). A still-non-terminal attempt loaded past that 50-arena window stops reconciling until it re-enters the window or a full reload happens. (2) `loadMoreArenas` de-duplicates its response against what is already loaded, by id, because offset pagination over a newest-first list that a new arena can prepend to can otherwise hand back one item the reader already has (the classic offset-pagination-under-insertion overlap: a page fetched at a stale offset after an insertion shifts one already-seen row into the new page's range) -- de-duplication absorbs this cleanly without needing a true keyset cursor the Rust repository does not actually implement.
  - Row selection remains not wired, unchanged from this line's own prior evidence and out of this pass's scope: no bulk row action exists yet for a selection checkbox column to drive. Bounded virtualization was not attempted either -- moot, since the task's own "service pagination *or* bounded virtualization" already reads as satisfied by the real pagination above.
- [x] 18.7 Define default result columns and move raw ids and low-frequency fingerprints into detail or optional columns. — Audited, not assumed: `configurationFingerprint` was already detail-only and no raw ids (`attempt.id`/`arenaId`/`canonicalRunId`) were ever shown in the table — nothing needed to move. Deliberately did not wire `DataTable`'s own `visibleColumnIds`/`ColumnVisibilityMenu`: every current column (Agent, Outcome, Tests, Tokens, Time) is core identity/at-a-glance information, and the primitive has no concept of a locked column, so enabling the menu would let a reader hide the Agent/Outcome identity columns too, for no field that actually needed hiding.
- [x] 18.8 Add eligible Baseline selection with task/version/configuration compatibility rules. — Investigated, not assumed: no stored/marked "baseline" concept exists anywhere (grepped every "baseline" hit in `src/`/`src-tauri/src/`; design.md's own Decision 15 lists a "Baseline selector" as a UI page element, not a service contract or stored field). Built as a reader-driven pairing (`evaluation-comparison-panel.tsx`): either of two picked attempts can play either role. `checkEligibility` (`evaluation-comparison.ts`) checks, in priority order, same-attempt / different `taskId` / different `taskVersion` / either side not yet terminal. `agent.configurationFingerprint` is deliberately not a hard gate -- the two most common real comparisons (different Agents on one task; the same Agent re-run after a config change) both need it to work across *different* configurations, and a same-Agent regression check needs it to work across the *same* configuration too, so a hard gate either way would break a real use case. Disclosed instead as `sameAgentConfiguration` on every eligible result, matching `isEvaluationAgentIncompatible`'s (18.5) own "stay selectable, disclose the real reason" precedent.
- [x] 18.9 Compute and present outcome-tier, metric, reliability, and evidence deltas only when provenance is comparable. — All four real, gated by 18.8's own eligibility check so nothing renders for an incomparable pair. Outcome-tier reuses the existing `OUTCOME_RANK` ordering and `deriveArenaState` rather than a second ranking; `cancelled` on either side yields `notRankable` rather than a guessed direction, per `OUTCOME_RANK`'s own established reasoning that it "is neither a pass nor a verdict." Metrics diff same-name/same-unit pairs with real, non-`unavailable` values on both sides; every name that cannot be diffed is classified with a real reason (missing on one side, unit mismatch, unavailable quality/null value), never silently dropped. Reliability is honestly narrower than the task's own word choice suggests: a real `flaky: bool` exists server-side (`evaluation_verifier.rs`) but is folded into `outcome` and never persisted -- confirmed by reading `evaluation_api.rs`'s own `attempt_aggregate`, which hard-codes `flaky: false` when reconstructing a *stored* attempt, meaning even the backend treats it as permanently unknown post-storage. Check pass rate (`checkRatio`, already used elsewhere on this page) is what is honestly left to report, disclosed as such in code, not presented as true flakiness. Evidence deltas are check/artifact count only, deliberately without a good/bad verdict -- more recorded evidence is not inherently an improvement.
- [x] 18.10 Mark regressions and improvements with icon/text/reason rather than color alone. — Unblocked once 18.8/18.9 landed, as already anticipated by this task's own prior note. Reuses the existing `StatusBadge` primitive (already used for outcome/arena-state elsewhere on this page), which structurally requires a visible `label` alongside `tone`/`icon` -- a `VerdictBadge` pairs `TrendingUp`/`TrendingDown`/`Minus`/`CircleHelp` with a translated verdict label, plus a separate visible reason sentence under every verdict, and an ineligible pair shows its own real reason via a `TriangleAlert` + text card, mirroring `evaluation-agent-selector.tsx`'s own incompatibility rendering.
- [x] 18.11 Implement 2–4 experiment comparison with aligned compatible task rows and immutable configuration snapshots. — Built the way 18.8-18.10's own reasoning pointed: the existing, already-tested pairwise `compareEvaluationAttempts` run once per additional candidate against the one reader-picked baseline (`evaluation-comparison-matrix.ts`'s `buildComparisonMatrix`), not new N-way eligibility/delta math — every rule 18.8/18.9 already proved out (cancelled-is-`notRankable`, the `-1` `checkRatio` sentinel, unit-mismatch classification, the disclose-don't-gate treatment of `configurationFingerprint`) keeps its exact existing behavior. The original 2-attempt baseline/candidate flow in `evaluation-comparison-panel.tsx` is untouched byte-for-byte (same testids, same `EvaluationComparisonResultView`, every pre-existing `evaluation-comparison-panel.test.tsx` case still passes unmodified); on top of it a reader can now check up to `MAX_ADDITIONAL_CANDIDATES = 2` more attempts (the picker excludes whatever is already baseline/candidate), which switches the rendered output to `EvaluationComparisonMatrixView`'s aligned rows/columns — 2 to 4 total experiments, baseline column plus one column per candidate, never both views shown for the same pick. Rows: header (identity + real ineligibility reason once, per column), outcome, configuration, reliability, checks/artifacts counts, then one row per metric name — baseline's own metric names first in baseline's own order (the one fixed reference every column shares), then any name a candidate introduces that baseline never recorded; an ineligible column shows its own real reason in its header and a dash for every row rather than a fabricated delta, and contributes zero candidate-only metric names of its own. "Immutable configuration snapshot": confirmed, not assumed — read `web-evaluation-client.ts` end to end: `EvaluationAgentSnapshot` (`agent` on every `EvaluationAttempt`) is built exactly once, at attempt-creation time (`webEvaluationAttempt`/`webDispatchFailedAttempt`), and never reassigned afterward (`cancelEvaluation`'s own update spreads only `outcome`; every service method returns a `structuredClone`) — the existing type already *is* the immutable snapshot this task asks for, so the matrix's own Configuration row (raw fingerprint for baseline, same/different disclosure per column) and every column header (agent id via the now-shared `attemptOptionLabel`, moved out of the panel into `evaluation-comparison-matrix.ts` so both it and the new view reuse one definition) just expose it — nothing new needed building for that half. `MAX_ADDITIONAL_CANDIDATES`/`MAX_COMPARISON_ATTEMPTS`-style caps (2 + baseline + candidate = 4) mirror `MAX_EVALUATION_AGENTS` (18.5)'s own real-cap-plus-disclose-plus-disable-at-capacity pattern.
- [x] 18.12 Expose deterministic checks, thresholds, measured values, optional judge role, failure classification, and bounded reason in detail. — Deterministic checks (`EvaluationCheck.passed`/`.summary`) and measured values (`EvaluationMetric.value`/`.unit`/`.quality`/`.source`) were already real and rendered; the other four were genuinely built or investigated this pass, still inline in `evaluation-center.tsx`'s own detail pane — re-verified 18.2's "Detail Inspector remains un-extracted" note is still accurate for this half specifically (only the comparison half it was written before has since been extracted, by 18.8-18.10). Failure classification: the detail pane never surfaced which of the 9 `EvaluationOutcome` values an attempt actually has (only the results table did) — added an outcome `StatusBadge` plus a real, one-sentence per-outcome explanation (`evaluation.outcomeExplanation.*`, 9 keys), each grounded in reasoning this codebase had already established elsewhere rather than invented (e.g. the benchmark_error/cancelled explanations restate `evaluation-results-table.tsx`'s own `OUTCOME_TONE` comment). Thresholds: investigated exhaustively before building, not assumed absent — `EvaluationCheck`/`EvaluationMetric` carry no target/threshold field of their own anywhere in `evaluation.ts`; the one real threshold-like value in the whole domain is `EvaluationTask.timeoutSeconds`, now looked up per selected attempt (`tasks.find` by `taskId`/`taskVersion`) and shown, omitted — never a fabricated "Unavailable" line — when the catalog has no matching task/version, mirroring `findTaskPrompt`'s own established "omit rather than fabricate" choice. Optional judge role: confirmed dead before building, not fabricated — re-grepped `\.judge\b|judge:|JudgeRole|judgeRole` across all of `src/`, zero hits outside the type's own declaration; `judge?: unknown` has no producer or consumer anywhere. Rendered as an honest, dedicated disclosure sentence (`evaluation.judgeUnavailable`), the same "stay visible, disclose the real absence" precedent 18.13 already set for artifact preview — not a fabricated value, and not a silently-omitted field either. Bounded reason: `check.summary` is free-text with no length constraint in the type, and the checks list previously had no cap at all — a "+N more" cap (Work Board's own `MAX_VISIBLE_SOURCES` precedent) was considered and rejected specifically because checks are the primary pass/fail signal and hiding some behind it could hide a *failing* check from view; scroll-bound instead (`max-h-48 overflow-auto`, mirroring the artifact list two sections below in the same pane, which already used this pattern), each summary additionally `line-clamp-2`-truncated with the full text preserved in `title`. Fixing this pass's own new outcome badge required scoping two pre-existing test assertions (one unit, four e2e) that had been relying on the outcome label being unique on screen — it no longer is, once a row is selected and the same outcome now also shows in the detail pane — to the results row/table specifically instead of the whole document; documented inline at each fix.

Not independently re-confirmed to the same depth as 16.16 was — flagged for whoever picks this up to verify before assuming.
- [x] 18.15 Add Web fixtures for pass, deterministic failure, Agent failure, timeout, stuck, cancelled, benchmark error, missing metrics, flaky, and artifact-unavailable states. — New `src/testing/fixtures/evaluation-attempt-fixtures.ts`, following `workspace-fixtures.ts`'s established convention (one named function per real state, `Partial<EvaluationAttempt>` overrides, fresh object per call) rather than retrofitting the pre-existing `evaluation-fixtures.ts`, which is a bulk/scale generator (task 0.9's 10,000-row structural test) with no named single-attempt entry points of its own — its field-shape conventions were read for reference but not called directly, since its `buildAgentSnapshot`/`buildChecks`/`buildMetrics`/`buildTimeline` are seeded-rng bulk helpers, not a fit for named determinism. 7 of the 10 states map directly to a real `EvaluationOutcome`: `passedAttempt`/`deterministicFailureAttempt`/`agentFailureAttempt`/`timedOutAttempt`/`stuckAttempt`/`cancelledAttempt`/`benchmarkErrorAttempt`. Missing metrics (`missingMetricsAttempt`): every metric entry present with `quality: "unavailable"`/`value: null` rather than an empty array — chosen because an empty array reads as "nothing was ever measured" while this reads as "expected and attempted but genuinely unavailable," the more informative of the two real, reachable shapes `MetricQuality` itself already distinguishes. Flaky: confirmed NOT a real per-attempt field before building, matching 18.9's own already-written evidence above verbatim (`flaky: bool` exists server-side but is folded into `outcome` and never persisted; `attempt_aggregate` hard-codes `flaky: false` on read-back) — no field was added to the fixture. Represented compositionally instead (`flakyAttemptPair`, deliberately taking no `overrides` param — a single override applied identically to both members could silently collide their `id`s): two attempts sharing `taskId`/`taskVersion`/Agent but landing on different outcomes, the real pattern a reader would call "flaky" by comparing repeated runs side by side, disclosed as a composed scenario in its own doc comment rather than faked as a field. Artifact-unavailable (`artifactUnavailableAttempt`): investigated first, not assumed — grepped how `artifactIds` is consumed (`evaluation-center.tsx`, `evaluation-comparison.ts`), confirmed via 18.13's own evidence above that no artifact-preview/resolution target exists anywhere yet, so *every* non-empty `artifactIds` array already renders as `unavailable` unconditionally today; a populated array is therefore the one real "artifact-unavailable" state this app currently has to offer, not a fabricated distinct failure mode — the fixture differs from `passedAttempt` only in carrying non-empty `artifactIds`. `judge` is left unset on every fixture (never fabricated, matching its confirmed-dead status; asserted directly in the test). `evaluation-attempt-fixtures.test.ts`: 15 cases covering every builder's real outcome, override application, fresh-object-per-call, judge absence across all nine builders, and the missing-metrics/artifact-unavailable/flaky-pair shapes specifically.

Backward compatible, every other (currently nonexistent) caller is unaffected. Selection checkboxes gained `stopPropagation` so row-click and row-select can't both fire once a row click handler exists.

**Tests**: `evaluation-results-table.test.tsx` (new, 7 cases: rendering, row-meta/testid contract, export-click stops row-select propagation, default order matches the ranked arena verbatim — load-bearing for the desktop WDIO suite's own assertion — sort toggling per column, controlled filter, empty state). `evaluation-center.test.tsx` (added a required `ResizeObserver` stub `DataTable` needs, 2 new cases for `EvidenceLink` artifact rendering, extended its i18n-locale-coverage test). `DataTableBody.test.tsx` (2 new cases for `getRowMeta`).

There is no live legacy entry point today, confirmed by grepping `ScheduledTask`/`Dialog` outside `src/scheduled-tasks/` (only unrelated matches). Nothing to redirect or remove. Flagged honestly: the dialog's removal predates full parity (19.4-19.6/19.11-19.13/19.15 remain unbuilt), a mild deviation from this task's own ideal sequencing — but it is a pre-existing fact from an unrelated commit, not something introduced by or fixable within this pass.

Detail is a placeholder showing only already-fetched fields (name/agent/frequency/enabled/next-run/status) — occurrence preview and history need 19.6/19.11's own new service calls, not attempted here. First real consumer of `RunsSection.scheduleId`, wired bidirectionally through `onSectionChange` so Back/forward/reload restore the selected task.
- [x] 19.4 Implement bounded search and filters for enabled, Agent, recurrence, status, attention, project/workspace, and next-run range when supported. — `ScheduledTaskFilters`/`scheduled-task-query.ts` (68254755): bounded search (name/content substring over the already-fetched list, never a second server round trip, matching this change's own 6.3-6.5 precedent) plus real filters for enabled, Agent, recurrence (kept as "frequency", this codebase's own established term, matching 19.15's "don't introduce a second name" precedent), status, and a next-run range bucket (overdue/next24h/next7d/later) over the real `nextRunAt` field. Attention and project/workspace confirmed absent, not built: independently re-confirmed on both `ScheduledTask` (`types/agent.ts`) and `dto::ScheduledTask` (Rust) that neither field exists -- a scheduled task has no project/workspace association at all (unlike Loop definitions' real `projectPath`) and nothing marks one as needing attention (unlike Mission Control's runs) -- exactly the fabricated-field trap `mission-control-query.ts`'s own "Attention" investigation (16.5) already documented and avoided; this task's own "when supported" qualifier covers this case.
