Timezone and attention confirmed absent as per-task concepts (same investigation as 19.4/19.13): scheduling is always this device's own OS-local clock, identical for every row, so a per-row timezone column would misleadingly imply a per-task setting that does not exist -- added a shared, once-per-list caption instead ("All times shown in {{zone}}"), reusing 19.13's own `Intl`-derived fact rather than a fabricated per-row value.
- [x] 19.6 Create route-backed task detail with configuration, future occurrence preview, capability notice, latest Run, and history. — `scheduled-task-detail.tsx` (f4c2e733) composes all five: configuration (already real from 19.3), occurrence preview (19.12, `ScheduledTaskOccurrences`), capability notice (`ScheduledTaskCapabilityNotice`, an honest Agent-availability check against the real registry using Evaluation Center's own established `availabilityState !== "available"` test, plus a `"missing"` case for a durable `agentId` reference that has since been uninstalled entirely -- a real case a scheduled task can hit that Evaluation's own live-picker can't), latest Run (extended in place -- `latestRunAt`/session link/error added to the existing Run-now button, not duplicated), and history (19.11, `ScheduledTaskHistory`).
- [x] 19.7 Create editor sheet for Create and Edit with adjacent validation and final review. — `ScheduledTaskEditorSheet` (22857ac9): Create and Edit share one Sheet-mounted editor over a plain `ScheduledTaskDraft`, replacing the former create-only, always-inline form. Adjacent validation: `ScheduledTaskForm`'s per-field error sits as a sibling of each `<label>`, linked via `aria-describedby` (not nested inside it, which would fold the error text into the input's own accessible name). Final review: `ScheduledTaskReview` restates name/agent/frequency/content live as the draft changes, matching `EvaluationReviewStep`'s/`CreateSessionStep4`'s own "nothing new to compute" shape. Edit's version-conflict recovery mirrors `loop-definition-dialog.tsx`'s `baseline` split: the draft (what the reader typed) is never silently overwritten, only `baseline` (what a retry is checked against) moves forward on conflict, kept local to the sheet so a concurrent delete elsewhere is explained (Save disables) rather than the sheet just vanishing.
- [x] 19.8 Add or adapt version-aware `updateScheduledTask` to Tauri and Web/mock service contracts. — Full stack, both backends (5501457b): migration 95 adds `scheduled_tasks.version`; the Rust command guards `WHERE id = ? AND version = ?` as the actual race-free check, rejecting a stale version with a stable code (`scheduled-task-version-conflict: expected X, stored Y`) rather than prose — `CommandError`'s own `Serialize` impl only ever sends the message string across the Tauri boundary, so this follows `personalization-revision-conflict`'s established precedent (already used by three other conflict paths) rather than Loop Center's own locale-fragile substring match. Web mock mirrors the same contract verbatim. No UI trigger added — deliberately scoped to the service-contract layer per this task's own literal wording; `scheduled-task-detail.tsx` is an explicit placeholder and `scheduled-task-form.tsx` is create-only, so wiring either now would risk conflicting with 19.7's own explicitly-scoped editor sheet. A real bug was found and fixed on both backends during review: `next_run_at` was being recomputed unconditionally on every edit, including one that never touched `frequency` — a rename alone would have silently pushed back the task's next scheduled run; now only recomputes when the frequency actually changed, mirroring `set_scheduled_task_enabled`'s own existing conditional recompute in the same file.
- [x] 19.9 Add Duplicate that opens a disabled reviewed draft and does not copy run history. — Duplicate opens `ScheduledTaskEditorSheet` in create mode, prefilled from the source task via `duplicateScheduledTaskDraft` with an adjusted name (`scheduledTasks.copyName`, mirroring `loops.definition.copyName`'s exact convention). Reading "disabled" as "not immediately committed" rather than literal read-only fields (this task's own instruction to whoever implemented it explicitly steered this interpretation, contrasting it with Loop Center's own Duplicate, which creates immediately after a bare rename confirm): the reader sees and can still edit the full form and Review before anything is created, going through the plain `create()` call, never `update()` against the source row — so no `scheduled_task_runs` history is ever copied, since the duplicated row simply never existed until that call creates a brand-new one.
- [x] 19.10 Add Run now returning stable operation or Run identity without changing recurrence. — Full stack, both backends (6a47001f): new `run_scheduled_task_now` Tauri command + Web mock + service method + a state-aware button. Reuses `run_one_task` (the sweep's own dispatch function, moved into `contexts/sessions/infrastructure/scheduled_tasks.rs` rather than duplicated) so the on-demand and swept paths can't drift into two different dispatch behaviors. Recurrence is provably untouched: `run_one_task` itself never writes to the database (only its callers' `mark_task_succeeded`/`mark_task_failed` do), and the new path calls neither — `record_manual_run` instead inserts one already-complete `scheduled_task_runs` row, which the sweep's own `completed_at IS NULL` completion query can structurally never pick up. Verified by a dedicated test running both paths interleaved and confirming neither cross-contaminates the other's row. Returns `{run, operationId: null}` matching `MissionControlActionReceipt`'s own shape — a session id wrapped in the existing `ScheduledTaskRun` concept, no new operation concept invented.
- [x] 19.11 Expose existing durable run history with pagination, trigger classification, timestamps, safe failure, and Session/Run links. — All 5, both backends. Timestamps, safe failure, and Session/Run links were already real (f4c2e733, see this line's own prior evidence). Trigger classification stays honestly partial by design, not a build gap: `backfilled`/`backfill_running` are real, distinguishable status values the sweep itself writes, so "caught up at startup" is shown; a manual "Run now" dispatch and a normal on-time sweep run both write `status: 'succeeded'` with no other distinguishing field anywhere in `scheduled_task_runs`, so they render identically rather than a guessed label the data cannot actually support.
  Pagination -- the one real gap this pass closed: `list_scheduled_task_runs` (`contexts/sessions/infrastructure/scheduled_tasks.rs`) previously hard-coded `LIMIT 100` with no offset parameter anywhere. Now takes real `offset: usize, limit: usize`, binds them as `LIMIT ?2 OFFSET ?3` (clamped to a `MAX_RUN_HISTORY_PAGE` = 100 ceiling at the SQL layer itself, mirroring `evaluation_repository.rs`'s own `MAX_PAGE`), proven by a dedicated test (`list_scheduled_task_runs_pages_with_real_offset_and_limit`, deliberately checking that concatenated pages reproduce the unpaginated order exactly rather than asserting a specific row per page, since `ORDER BY started_at DESC, id DESC` can genuinely tie within a tight loop with no artificial delay) plus a clamp test. The Tauri command (`commands/sessions/scheduled_tasks.rs`) takes `cursor`/`limit`, resolves/clamps via its own `DEFAULT_RUN_HISTORY_LIMIT`/`MAX_RUN_HISTORY_LIMIT` (20/50, mirroring `list_evaluation_arenas.rs`'s identical constants and its own "+1 probe row" has-more trick), and returns `dto::ScheduledTaskRunPage { items, nextCursor }`.
  Pagination-shape decision: matched `listEvaluationArenas`'s own `{ items, nextCursor }` cursor contract from 18.6 exactly (`ScheduledTaskRunPage`/`ScheduledTaskRunQuery` in types/agent.ts, mirroring `EvaluationArenaPage`/`EvaluationArenaQuery`) rather than diverging to a bespoke shape -- every other paginated list surface in this app (`MissionControlPage`, `EvaluationArenaPage`, `ContextQualityHistoryPage`, `ContextEvidenceManifestPage`, personalization memory, token usage, builtin tools) already reads that exact shape, so Scheduled Tasks' own run history joins that convention instead of being the one surface that hands a caller a raw offset/limit pair to reconstruct paging from itself. The cursor is honestly just the offset re-exposed as an opaque string on both backends (never a real keyset cursor), same as Evaluation's own precedent, including the same "an invalid hand-typed cursor throws rather than silently resetting to page one" behavior in the Web mock.
  Threaded end-to-end: `scheduled-task-service.ts`'s `listScheduledTaskRuns(taskId, query?)` now returns `Promise<ScheduledTaskRunPage>`; `tauri-agent-client.ts` passes `cursor`/`limit` through to `invoke`; `web-scheduled-task-client.ts` does real `Array.prototype.slice` pagination over its per-task in-memory run history (`pageScheduledTaskRuns`, mirroring `web-evaluation-client.ts`'s own `pageEvaluationArenas`). `useScheduledTaskHistory` (the hook `scheduled-tasks-panel.tsx` already had wired to the detail view) gained `hasMore`/`loadingMore`/`loadMore()`, reusing its own existing `generation`-guarded race protection so a stale `loadMore` response for a task the reader has since navigated away from -- or one whose history was just `reload()`-ed -- is discarded by the same guard rather than a second one. `ScheduledTaskHistory` replaced its old "note when landing exactly on the 100-row cap" heuristic with a real "Load more" control (`scheduled-task-history-load-more` testid, `scheduledTasks.history.loadMore`/`loadingMore` i18n keys added to all 5 locales in place of the now-inapplicable `cappedNote` key removed from all 5), matching `EvaluationArenaList`'s own control exactly. `ScheduledTasksPanel` wires `history.hasMore`/`history.loadingMore`/`history.loadMore` into the detail view, and a dedicated panel test clicks the real button and asserts the second page's row renders.
  Verified with unit/component tests across all seven touched TS/TSX files (hook, history component, detail, panel, Tauri-client adapter parity, Web mock, service contract) plus five new Rust tests (two pagination/clamp tests on the infrastructure query, three cursor/limit-helper tests on the command mirroring `list_evaluation_arenas.rs`'s own three). No dedicated Playwright e2e coverage exists for this feature, matching that 18.6 did not add one for Evaluation's own identical pagination either; not attempted in this pass.
- [x] 19.12 Add or adapt next-five-occurrence preview using the same timezone and recurrence semantics as execution. — `computeNextScheduledOccurrences` (`scheduled-task-recurrence.ts`) is a pure loop feeding `computeNextScheduledRun`'s own result back in as the next call's `from`, so the preview is provably the same single-step function the due-task sweep and every `next_run_at` recompute already use, not a second calculation that could drift out of sync. `ScheduledTaskOccurrences` anchors the 5-occurrence chain at the task's own already-computed `nextRunAt`, not "now" -- for `minutes`/`hours` frequencies `nextRunAt` sits on a grid set by whenever the task last ran or was edited, so anchoring at render time would silently disagree with the value already shown one line above it. A disabled task shows an honest explanation instead of a preview chained from its frozen, stale `nextRunAt` (`set_scheduled_task_enabled` only recomputes on enabling, never on disabling).
- [x] 19.13 Display explicit configured timezone and daylight-saving policy in editor and detail. — Investigated, not assumed: confirmed by reading every recompute site in `scheduled_tasks.rs` that scheduling is OS/process-local (`chrono::Local::now()`), there is no per-task timezone field anywhere in `ScheduledTask`/`ScheduledTaskFrequency` on either the Rust or TS side, and DST handling is exactly one silent crash-avoidance fallback (`next_daily`'s `.single().unwrap_or(from)`), not a stated policy. This task's own literal wording ("explicit configured timezone and daylight-saving policy") cannot be honestly built as worded -- there is no configuration and no policy to display. Matching this change's own established pattern for a requested surface confirmed not to exist server-side (16.5's "Attention" filter, correctly not built rather than faked), `ScheduledTaskExecutionNotice` shows the real fact instead: this device's own live IANA zone (`Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`), with copy explicitly stating there is no separate timezone setting -- never a `<select>` implying stored per-task configuration that does not exist. Shared verbatim by the editor Review and the detail view's capability notice so the two surfaces cannot drift into disagreeing about the same real behavior.
- [x] 19.14 Replace hard-coded weekday and frequency labels with synchronized locale resources and locale-aware formatting. — `formatScheduledTaskFrequency` returns `{key, ...params}` for `t()` interpolation instead of a hard-coded English sentence, keeping `scheduled-task-recurrence.ts` framework-agnostic; weekday names (summary sentence and the weekday `<select>`) come from a new `Intl`-derived `formatAppWeekdayNames` rather than a hand-maintained translated array (05922481). Uses this codebase's established `_one`/`_other` plural convention, already enforced by `i18n-resource-parity.test.ts`. Disclosed behavior change: singular is now "Every 1 minute" instead of the old "Every minute", matching every other plural pair already in this codebase.
- [x] 19.15 Display the application-open and at-most-one catch-up execution model in page, editor Review, and capability-driven help. — The pre-existing `scheduledTasks.runtimeHint` key already stated the application-open + at-most-one-catch-up model precisely (shown during editing, `ScheduledTaskForm`) and is reused verbatim -- not a second, slightly-different paraphrase -- in two more places: the editor Review and the detail view's own capability notice (this task's own "capability-driven help" surface). Reusing one key across all three means they cannot quietly drift into stating the same real behavior three different ways. Codebase vocabulary note: `scheduled_tasks.rs`/its logs call this "backfill" throughout, but the pre-existing UI copy this task built on top of already said "caught up" -- kept that established user-facing term rather than introducing a second one.
- [x] 19.16 Put Delete in More, add consequence-aware confirmation, and keep Enable/Disable and Run now state-aware. — Delete moved from the row's own bespoke inline Trash2 + Cancel/Confirm pair into `ActionMenu`'s built-in `confirmation`, alongside Edit/Duplicate, matching `work-board-card.tsx`'s own per-card `ActionMenu` precedent (many independently actionable rows, not Goal Center's single-detail-pane shape). Confirmation text is honest, not over- or under-claiming: "This permanently removes the task. It stops running on its schedule and cannot be recovered" — confirmed by reading `delete_scheduled_task` that it never touches `scheduled_task_runs`, but nothing exposes that history for a deleted task id either (19.11, unbuilt), so the description makes no claim about it either way. Enable/Disable and Run now stayed state-aware (unchanged in substance, now sharing the same per-task registry slot as Delete/Edit — see 19.17).
- [x] 19.17 Use target-local pending and preserve task collection and history during every mutation. — `useScheduledTasksActions` replaces the panel's former single page-wide `error`/`saving`/`confirmingDeleteId`/`runningTaskId` state with one `useMutationRegistry`, keyed by task id, matching `use-goal-center-actions.ts`'s/`use-work-board-actions.ts`'s own adoption. Closes a real grouping bug: Enable/Disable and Delete failures on any row previously funneled into the create form's own single error slot, with no visible connection to the row that actually failed. Enable/Disable, Delete, Update (Edit's save), and Run now deliberately share one slot per task id (not four independent ones) — a genuine race worth preventing, since Run now dispatching a task's stale content while its own Edit save is in flight would be a real bug, not just noise. Create/Duplicate key off a separate fixed `SCHEDULED_TASK_CREATE_MUTATION_KEY`, since a not-yet-created task cannot race any existing row. Selection, the open editor sheet, and every other row's own state all survive any one row's mutation untouched, verified by dedicated tests.
- [x] 19.18 Add recurrence, timezone, DST, update race, duplicate, run-now, history, capability notice, compact layout, Web parity, and Tauri scheduler tests. — Audited before adding anything (68254755): recurrence, timezone, update race, duplicate, run-now, history, and capability notice already had real coverage from 19.7-19.17's own earlier passes, confirmed by name rather than assumed or duplicated. Genuinely added: a live compact-viewport (900px) e2e test, which caught and led to fixing a real layout bug (a `flex`-based restructuring had starved the list's own grid row to a measured 0px height, confirmed live before the fix, not assumed from CSS alone); a Web/Tauri adapter parity test (`scheduled-task-adapter-parity.test.ts`, this domain had none before, unlike Goal/Loop/Token-usage); and two tests for `bootstrap/scheduled_tasks.rs`'s own `log_scheduled_task` (the one real gap in that file's own coverage; the sweep/bookkeeping it calls into is already exhaustively tested in `contexts::sessions::infrastructure::scheduled_tasks`). Two sub-items honestly declined rather than faked: a DST test (`next_daily` hardcodes `chrono::Local` with no injectable timezone, so a real transition cannot be forced deterministically across the Windows/macOS/Linux CI matrix without a dependency or production refactor -- matches 19.13's own "cannot be honestly built as worded" precedent) and a due-task dispatch-loop test through `run_due_tasks` itself (needs a real `SessionsApi`+`AgentRuntimeApi`, a ~100-line port-double harness on the scale of `native_lifecycle_tests.rs`'s `LifecycleHarness`, disproportionate to what would newly be covered given the due-scan query it calls is already tested).

## 20. Responsive, accessibility, localization, and themes

- [x] 20.1 Define container thresholds and minimum work-surface sizes in one responsive module; remove duplicated magic breakpoints from migrated pages. — Extracted the genuinely duplicated MECHANISM, not the `useMediaQuery` breakpoint values (those stay per-surface design decisions, per this task's own scope: `work-board.tsx`'s 900px, `create-session-dialog-content.tsx`'s 640px, `skills-page.tsx`'s 1280px, `prompt-hook-card-list.tsx`'s 768px, and `projects.tsx`'s 767px are legitimately different needs, left untouched). New `useContainerCompactMode` (`src/hooks/use-container-compact-mode.ts`) is the one ResizeObserver-based container-width hook now behind both `useTableCompactMode` (`src/ui/data-table/use-table-compact-mode.ts`, now a thin named wrapper preserving its existing export) and Mission Control's section-nav compact switch (`mission-control-section-nav.tsx`, whose own private `useSectionNavCompactMode` duplicate was removed entirely). Each caller keeps its own threshold constant and reasoning comment (640px for both today, coincidentally, not a shared constant). New `use-container-compact-mode.test.ts` (5 cases) is the first real test of this mechanism's ResizeObserver callback actually firing — every existing consumer test only ever asserted the jsdom-stubbed non-compact default.

No other destination has a nav/Inspector column of this shape.

Confirmed this is not the "clipped column" failure mode 20.3 guards against: it is a dismissible floating overlay (`absolute`, outside-pointerdown/Escape close), not a persistent column, and neither `Toolbar` nor its containing `<div>` in either destination sets `overflow-hidden`/`overflow-x-auto` that could clip its dropdown panel (checked both call sites directly). `min-w-48` (192px) comfortably fits inside this app's own smallest tested width (640px, task 20.2's matrix) regardless of where its trigger sits in a `flex-wrap` toolbar row.

Read `ApplicationDialog` (`src/components/ui/application-dialog.tsx`) directly to confirm this is not a gap: it is already width-responsive on its own at any viewport (`w-full` capped by `maxWidth`, `p-3 sm:p-5` outer gutter, `max-h-[calc(100vh-1.5rem)]` plus internal `overflow-y-auto` body scrolling) -- it reflows and scrolls, it does not clip or force horizontal scrolling, so Decision 11's "Sheet 或 Dialog" allowance is genuinely satisfied by either choice, not just Sheet.

  **List-Detail panes** (Decision 11's `master-detail`/`collection` shell, "窄屏 detail 替换 list 并有明确返回"): checked Goal Center's list+`GoalDetail` (15.12) and Projects' list+`WorkspaceDetail` (13.12) against this exact decision text, since the orchestrating brief suspected both might be "just a conditional render" rather than a real accessible fallback. Both already match Decision 11 precisely -- detail replaces list entirely (not stacked, not clipped) with an explicit Back control -- which is a *different*, equally-valid compact-width answer from Navigation/Inspector's Sheet, not a lesser one; design.md itself draws this exact line, so treating list-then-detail-swap as non-compliant would be holding it to the wrong decision. `GoalRelationshipSections` (nested inside `GoalDetail`) was checked too: bounded per-group rendering (`VISIBLE_LINKS_PER_GROUP`) plus `truncate` on long ids, no fixed-width overflow -- and it only ever renders at the detail pane's *full* width (compact swap gives it the whole pane, never a squeezed sliver), so nothing here needed touching.

  **Real gap found and fixed**: Mission Control's own list+detail (`mission-control.tsx`) is the same `master-detail` shape as Goal Center/Projects but did not follow Decision 11's swap rule -- it forced `grid-cols-1` below 900px while still rendering *both* `MissionControlRunList` and the detail `<aside>` simultaneously, stacked, inside a container set to `overflow-hidden` with neither pane given a bounded per-row height. A reader with more than a screenful of Runs had no way to scroll to, or even know about, the selected Run's own detail panel below the fold -- a genuine clipped column, confirmed empirically (not just reasoned from CSS semantics) via a new failing-then-passing test. Fixed to match Goal Center/Projects' own established swap pattern exactly: added `compact` (`useMediaQuery("(max-width: 899px)")`, matching the grid's own pre-existing `min-[900px]:` breakpoint), `showingList`/`showingDetail` booleans, and a `Back to Runs` button (new key in all 5 locales) that clears `selected`. Also carried over Projects' own identical scroll-restore fix while in there: the run-list's scroll-position-restore effect now re-fires when `showingList` flips back to `true` (a compact Back can unmount-then-remount the list), not just once on initial load, the same gap `projects.tsx` had already found and fixed for its own analogous effect. New `describe("compact layout (20.3)")` in `mission-control.test.tsx` (3 tests, mirroring `projects.test.tsx`'s own "compact layout (13.12)" suite and its `stubMatchMedia` helper): list-only until selected, selecting swaps to detail-with-Back (checked against a *different*, non-selected Run's testid, since `MissionControlDetailPanel` legitimately renders its own copy of the selected Run's `RunCard`, same `data-testid`, as part of the detail itself), Back returns to the list. All 19 tests in the file pass; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint src/mission-control` both clean.

It still does not match Decision 11's swap-with-Back pattern though, which is a real, disclosed inconsistency (a fourth master-detail shell doing a third thing) rather than a silently-accepted one -- not fixed here since it was not part of this pass's named scope and "requires scrolling" is a materially smaller gap than "requires scrolling in a container that cannot scroll."
- [x] 20.4 Ensure Board becomes Stage List and DataTable-heavy pages become summary-list/detail flows at compact widths. — Verified already real and already tested on both halves; no gap found, nothing built. Board: `work-board.tsx`'s own 14.13 comment confirms a compact width forces `grouping="stage"` (the vertical, no-drag-target Stage List) regardless of the reader's own grouping/presentation preference, replacing the side-by-side Kanban columns entirely. DataTable: `DataTable` (`src/ui/data-table/DataTable.tsx`) has exactly one real production consumer, `evaluation-results-table.tsx` (confirmed by grep for the exact identifier; `evaluation-arena-list.tsx` and `mcp-scope-section.tsx` only mention `DataTable` in comments explaining why they deliberately don't use it). `DataTableBody.test.tsx`'s "falls back to a stacked card list when compact" test forces `compact` directly against the presentational component (jsdom has no ResizeObserver, so `DataTable.test.tsx` itself documents delegating compact-mode composition testing to `DataTableBody` this same way) and asserts no `role="table"`, a real `role="list"`, and `<dl>` key/value card rows — a genuine summary-list, not a horizontally-scrolled table.
- [ ] 20.5 Ensure virtual keyboards do not cover Composer, field errors, dialog/sheet footer, or decision actions. — Verified genuinely non-applicable to this app's real target platform, not silently skipped and not force-built. Confirmed rather than assumed: `grep -rniE "visualViewport|keyboard-inset|virtual.?keyboard|env\(keyboard"` across `src/` and `tests/` finds zero existing virtual-keyboard-aware code anywhere -- the only hit is a *disclaiming* comment in `create-session-wizard.spec.ts` (task 11.12) stating this exact concern "is not independently provable in Playwright (no real on-screen keyboard to trigger)."

  This app's own real target platform argues against building speculative handling here: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`'s one window is `minWidth: 1100, minHeight: 700`, and `bundle.targets` is `["nsis", "app", "dmg", "deb", "appimage"]` -- Windows/macOS/Linux desktop installers only, no mobile Tauri target. Critically, that `minHeight: 700` is OS-enforced on the real window itself: a reader can never actually shrink this app below 700px tall, which is exactly the axis a virtual keyboard would squeeze. A Playwright test that force-sets a shorter viewport to simulate a keyboard would be exercising a window state the shipped app can never actually reach -- synthetic, not a real regression guard, and arguably misleading if reported as coverage.

  Checked the one existing touch-capable precedent named in the brief rather than assuming it implies a keyboard scenario: `onepiece-agent.spec.ts`'s `test.describe("touch path")` (`test.use({ hasTouch: true })`) tests tap-vs-click hit-testing parity on a touch-capable *desktop* (e.g. a touchscreen laptop/all-in-one) -- it says nothing about an on-screen keyboard, and a touch-capable Windows desktop overwhelmingly still has a physical keyboard attached (Windows only auto-invokes its on-screen keyboard in tablet mode with no physical keyboard present, itself a narrow edge case for a developer tool whose primary interaction is sustained code/chat text entry). This precedent does not establish a real virtual-keyboard scenario for this app the way it might for a phone-first product.

  Checked the actual CSS shape of the 4 named surfaces anyway, as due diligence independent of the "does this scenario occur" question -- confirmed none of the risky pattern (a `position: fixed`/`absolute` element pinned to a stale `100vh`) is present: Composer (`ChatInputBox.tsx`)'s own container is a plain normal-flow `shrink-0` flex child, not `fixed`/`absolute` -- normal-flow content reflows with whatever height is actually available, no special handling needed. Dialog footers (`ApplicationDialog.tsx`) already scroll their body internally (`overflow-y-auto`) under a `max-h-[calc(100vh-1.5rem)]` cap rather than pinning the footer past the visible edge (task 11.12 already flagged `100vh`'s mobile pitfall here directly and routes narrow-width usage to `Sheet` instead). Sheet footers (`Sheet.tsx`) render inside the same `fixed inset-0` box as the rest of the sheet, never detached from it. Decision actions (`LoopAcceptancePanel`, named in task 20.9's own evidence) are `sticky`, not `fixed` -- sticky elements are positioned relative to their scrolling ancestor's actual current size, not a snapshotted viewport unit, so they do not carry the same stale-`100vh` risk. No field-error-specific footer/positioning exists to check separately: per-field errors render adjacent to their own field (19.7's `ScheduledTaskForm` precedent), so they are exactly as visible as the field itself, not independently coverable.

  Conclusion: building `visualViewport`-driven CSS/JS here would be inventing handling for a scenario this specific app cannot actually enter (OS-enforced 700px minimum height, no mobile bundle target, no evidence any real user path attempts on-screen-keyboard text entry), matching this session's own established discipline against fabricating narrow-platform handling that doesn't apply (the brief's own cited standard). Left unchecked rather than force-closed, since no artifact here actually raises assurance the way the other 20.x fixes do -- this is a verified-not-applicable finding, not a shipped mechanism.
- [ ] 20.6 Apply minimum pointer target and spacing rules to icon-only controls, resize handles, row menus, drag handles, and tab close actions. — Real, scoped audit across Work Board, Loop Center, Mission Control, Goal Center, and Projects (the destinations most touched this session), not a full-app pass — left `[ ]` deliberately, not exhaustive. `min-h-11`/`min-w-11` (44px) is this codebase's own already-established convention (19 pre-existing files); applied it as the bar. Fixed genuine violations found: `ActionMenu` (`src/ui/actions/ActionMenu.tsx`) — the shared "row menu" trigger behind 10 real call sites across Work Board/Loop Center/Goal Center/Projects/Settings — was `h-8 w-8` (32px), now `min-h-11 min-w-11`. `SplitPane` (`src/ui/split-pane/SplitPane.tsx`) — the shared "resize handle" primitive — painted only `w-2`/`h-2` (8px) with the pointer/keyboard handlers on that same 8px box; kept the visual bar thin (a real 44px gutter would eat pane space) and widened only the *invisible* hit area via a `before:` pseudo-element with negative insets (pseudo-elements are not DOM nodes, so a pointerdown anywhere in the slop still targets the real `role="separator"` element — all 7 pre-existing `SplitPane.test.tsx` cases still pass unchanged). `loop-iteration-row.tsx`'s nested "Evidence timeline" disclosure toggle — flagged earlier this session (17.13/17.3 rounds) as found-but-reverted at ~16.5px because it surfaced mid an unrelated flaky-test investigation — confirmed still unset (`no min-h at all`) and fixed with `min-h-11`. Five more icon-only `size="icon"` (36px) `Button` close/delete triggers directly inside the 5 named destinations, each bumped via an added `min-h-11 min-w-11` className (CSS `min-height`/`min-width` deterministically floors a smaller fixed `h-*`/`w-*` regardless of Tailwind class order, so this is safe without re-verifying each site visually): `work-board-saved-view-menu.tsx`, `mission-control-saved-view-menu.tsx` (delete saved view), `loop-definition-dialog.tsx`, `loop-preflight-dialog.tsx` (dialog close), `goal-relationship-sections.tsx` (unlink). Checked and confirmed *not instantiated* anywhere in the 5 destinations or the shared tab-strip primitives, so nothing to fix: a dedicated small "drag handle" icon (Work Board/Session cards drag the whole card via the native `draggable` attribute, not a grip icon) and an icon-only "tab close" X (`session-tab-bar.tsx`'s 4 fixed tabs have no close action at all; `shell-strip.tsx`'s close is a full text-labeled button, not icon-only, and sits outside the 5 named destinations anyway; `RuntimePanel.tsx`'s own tabs are likewise not individually closeable). Disclosed rather than fixed, to keep this pass bounded: `session-card.tsx`'s row-actions trigger (`h-6 w-6`, 24px) and `Inspector.tsx`/`RuntimePanel.tsx`'s panel-close/maximize buttons (`p-1.5` + small icon, ~26-28px) are real and the same shape as the fixes above, but live in shared `main-layout/`/`src/ui/` primitives reaching well beyond the 5 named destinations — a fuller audit should cover these next. `WorkItemStageMenu` (Work Board card footer, `h-8`/32px, sits directly beside the now-fixed `ActionMenu`) was left alone: a labeled listbox trigger, not icon-only, not one of the 5 named categories — the resulting minor height mismatch in that footer row is an accepted, disclosed tradeoff.
- [x] 20.7 Implement and test correct Tablist, Toolbar, Menu, Listbox, Tree, Grid, Dialog, and Sheet keyboard models. — Investigated all 8 named patterns by grepping every real `role="..."` instance in `src/`, not sampling; fixed every real gap found, reusing the two pre-existing shared hooks (`useTabList`, `useMenuList`) rather than inventing new ones, and gave an honest doesn't-exist verdict for the two patterns with no real instance in this app.

  **Tablist**: `useTabList` (`src/ui/runtime-panel/use-tab-list.ts`) was confirmed correct and already used by exactly 2 consumers (`RuntimePanel.tsx`, `mission-control-section-nav.tsx`, both cited not rebuilt). Grepping every other real `role="tablist"` found 14 more instances across 12 files with either zero keyboard model (each tab its own plain Tab stop, no arrow keys at all) or a half-built one (roving `tabIndex` set but no `onKeyDown` wired, or Arrow-only with no Home/End): `runs-destination.tsx`, `plan-destination.tsx`, `session-skills-pane.tsx`, `projects.tsx`, `execution-record-toolbar.tsx`, `session-files-surface.tsx`, `shell-strip.tsx` (its own doc comment already promised "a keyboard user navigates them with arrow keys" — genuinely never wired), `skill-dialogs.tsx` (2 tablists), `skill-detail-body.tsx`, `onepiece-configuration-panel.tsx`, `prompt-hooks-page.tsx`, `prompt-hook-detail-panel.tsx`, and `cli-details-drawer.tsx`. All 14 now go through `useTabList`. Two pre-existing bespoke-but-already-fully-correct implementations found and deliberately left alone rather than migrated: `session-tab-bar.tsx` (own complete Arrow/Home/End + roving tabindex) and `personalization/view-tabs.tsx` (same, plus ArrowUp/Down, a genuine superset `useTabList` does not support) — not gaps, migrating working code for its own sake risked regressing two already-tested surfaces for no behavioral gain.

  **Menu**: `useMenuList` (`src/ui/actions/use-menu-list.ts`) confirmed as the one real shared primitive, already correctly used by `ActionMenu.tsx`, `WorkItemStageMenu` (Board's own Listbox realization, see below), and `seat-avatar-group.tsx` — all cited, not rebuilt. Grepping every other real `role="menu"` found 5 more instances with only Escape-to-close and (in most) an initial autofocus, but zero Up/Down/Home/End navigation between items: `SelectorDropdown.tsx`, `session-context-panel.tsx`, `conversation-overflow-menu.tsx`, `folder-opener-control.tsx`, and `cli-config-profile-list.tsx`'s per-row actions menu. Fixed all 5. Three reused `useMenuList` directly (`SelectorDropdown`, `conversation-overflow-menu`, `folder-opener-control`); `session-context-panel.tsx`'s item count varies (a conditional recover entry, one item per real session category) so it got a small local handler that queries the live DOM at keypress time instead — the same technique `use-focus-trap.ts` already uses for Tab-wrapping, avoiding a hand-counted index that would silently drift out of sync with the conditional JSX. `cli-config-profile-list.tsx`'s per-row menu needed extracting into its own `ProfileActionsMenu` component (hooks cannot be called a variable number of times inside the parent's `.map()`); its native `<details>/<summary>` looked like the smallest fix but live testing proved React's `onToggle` never actually fires for a real user-driven `<details>` toggle in this test environment (`details.open` itself does flip, the event never reaches the handler — confirmed via a throwaway debug build before rewriting, not assumed), so it was converted to a plain trigger + conditional popup matching `ActionMenu.tsx`'s own shape instead, which is both correct and actually testable. Building that fix surfaced a real, reproducible race in the first three: resetting the roving index in a `[open]`-keyed effect that ran before a second, separately-keyed focus-follow effect meant the second effect could still read the previous render's stale index, and that stale `.focus()` call's own `onFocus` handler then re-asserted the old index — reopening a menu after arrowing to a later item left focus stuck there instead of resetting to the first item. Fixed in all 3 (plus built correctly from the start in the 4th) by moving the reset into the same click handler that opens the menu, one batched update, no intermediate stale read.

  **Listbox**: `WorkItemStageMenu` (Work Board's own "Move to…" popup, `role="listbox"`/`role="option"`) confirmed as the shared-primitive realization named in this task's own brief, already correct and tested (14.8/14.9, re-exercised this session by 20.19's keyboard-only Board-movement e2e test) — cited, not rebuilt. Five more real instances (`command-center.tsx`, `settings-search-box.tsx`, `ui/entity-list/EntityList.tsx`, `content-search-panel.tsx`, `quick-open-dialog.tsx`) are a second, equally valid WAI-ARIA realization — a composite textbox that owns real focus while ArrowDown/Up move a virtual selection, each confirmed to already have real local Arrow-key handling wired to a real input. `components/chat/ComposerMentionCompletion.tsx` is a third instance of that same composite pattern: confirmed `ChatInputBox.tsx`'s own textarea carries `aria-activedescendant`/`aria-controls`/`aria-expanded` pointing at it, with dedicated existing coverage in `ChatInputBox.mention-keyboard.test.tsx` — real focus never leaves the textbox by design, which is correct for this shape, not a gap. No gap found anywhere in this pattern.

  **Tree, Grid**: confirmed genuinely absent from this app, not force-built. Grepped `role="tree"`, `role="treeitem"`, `role="grid"`, `role="gridcell"` across all of `src/` — zero matches for any of the four. `DataTable` (20.4's own subject) renders a native `<table>` (implicit `role="table"`), never `role="grid"` — correct for a read-only display, not an editable 2D-navigable grid, so it does not count as a Grid instance either.

  **Toolbar**: confirmed the WAI-ARIA Toolbar pattern (a single Tab stop, arrow keys roving between a row of otherwise-independent controls) has no real instance in this app, the same honest verdict as Tree/Grid. The one literal `Toolbar` component (`src/ui/toolbar/Toolbar.tsx`) has zero real JSX consumers anywhere in `src/` (grepped `<Toolbar[ />]`; only its own `Toolbar.test.tsx` references it) and does not itself set `role="toolbar"`. Grepping `role="toolbar"` across the whole of `src/` also returns zero matches — none of the ~20 other `*-toolbar.tsx`-named files (`work-board-toolbar.tsx`, `mission-control-toolbar.tsx`, etc.) are ARIA toolbars either; they are conventionally-named filter/action bars whose controls are correctly independent Tab stops (a search box mixed with buttons, which the WAI-ARIA toolbar pattern itself does not cleanly cover for a text input anyway). Building roving-tabindex-across-heterogeneous-controls handling for a pattern with no real target here would have been exactly the fabrication this task's own brief warns against.

  Verified the mechanism first, not just individual instances: theming in this codebase is pure CSS custom-property scoping (`:root[data-theme="futuristic"]`/`:root[data-theme="minimal"]` in `styles.css`), matching design.md Decision 19's own rule ("禁止为主题建立两套 JSX" — no two JSX trees per theme). Grepped all of `src/` for `useTheme(`/`theme === "futuristic"`/`theme === "minimal"`/`resolvedTheme`: the only match outside test files is `theme-provider.tsx` itself; the 2 other `data-theme` references (`agent-terminal-tab.tsx`, `shell-surface.tsx`) only *observe* the attribute to resync xterm's own imperative color config, never branch React JSX structure. So no feature component (Work Board, Goal Center, Mission Control, or otherwise) reads theme to decide its own structure at all — structural equivalence between themes is an architectural guarantee by construction, not a per-component claim needing individual proof.

Added `@axe-core/playwright` (`^4.13.0`, matching this repo's own `@playwright/test` peer range) as a new devDependency, plus one shared `expectNoSeriousAxeViolations` helper (`tests/e2e/a11y-helpers.ts`) used by every scan below — one real axe-core scan, filtered to `serious`/`critical` impact (this task's own named bar; `minor`/`moderate` findings are real too but out of this increment's scope, stated explicitly rather than silently widened).

  First increment, not "every route" (deliberately, per this task's own scope-down framing): 4 of this app's most-trafficked, most-recently-stabilized destinations, each scanned in a populated/realistic state (not the empty initial page) across **both** shipped themes — 8 real scan cases total. Evaluation Center's axe test landed inside its own existing spec (`evaluation-center.spec.ts`, both themes, `openWizardAndRun` + a selected result). Mission Control, Work Board, and Goal Center landed in a new dedicated file, `tests/e2e/accessibility-axe-scan.spec.ts` (both themes each), not inside their own existing specs (`mission-control.spec.ts`/`todo-board.spec.ts`/`goal-center.spec.ts`) — those three were under sustained, real concurrent edits from other agents sharing this worktree for this entire task's duration (their own 20.2/20.17 work, confirmed repeatedly via `git status`/`git diff`); editing them directly risked losing or mis-attributing that in-flight work at commit time, which genuinely happened once to `mission-control.spec.ts` here (another agent's ~99-line addition got swept into a `git commit` by the shared lint-staged/husky hook picking up a file staged concurrently by their own process) and was caught via `git show --stat HEAD` immediately after every commit in this task and undone via `git checkout HEAD~1 -- <file>` + `commit --amend` before anything was pushed, restoring their working-tree content from a local backup so nothing was lost -- not silently risked again afterward.

  Both themes turned out to be load-bearing, not redundant coverage: the first real run of each destination's test found genuine, theme-specific `color-contrast` failures a single-theme scan would have missed entirely, all fixed at the design-token root cause (`styles.css`) rather than per-component or suppressed:
  - `aria-prohibited-attr` (app-wide, found via Evaluation Center but affects every page): `NotificationToastViewport`'s toast region is a bare `<div>` — implicit ARIA role `generic`, which does not permit `aria-label` per the ARIA-in-HTML spec. Given a real `role="region"`, the standard landmark for a nameable section, matching what it actually is.
  - `color-contrast`, futuristic theme: `--neutral`/`--blocked` (`.ucd-status-neutral`/`.ucd-status-blocked`) read 4.34:1/4.25:1 against their own soft-tinted background inside a panel, short of WCAG AA's 4.5:1 for normal text.
  - `color-contrast`, minimal theme: `--success`/`--warning`/`--running`/`--information`/`--attention` (same status-tone family) read 2.78:1-4.39:1; separately, `--primary` (paired with `bg-nav-active-soft` for Work Board/Goal Center's active-tab highlight) read 4.39:1 — a saturated brand blue this light cannot clear 4.5:1 against a near-white background without darkening the text itself, confirmed by the fact widening the background's own alpha only pulls it *toward* the same blue, which reduces contrast rather than adding it.
  - Every fix was a lightness-only nudge (hue/saturation preserved, so each tone's semantic color identity is unchanged), computed with a small throwaway WCAG contrast-ratio script (proper sRGB relative luminance + alpha compositing against both `--background` and `--panel`, targeting 4.6:1 rather than exactly 4.5 so real sub-pixel rendering can't land back under the line) rather than eyeballed -- then verified for real by re-running the actual Playwright + axe scan against the running app afterward, not just trusted from the script. `--danger` (both themes) and `--neutral`/`--blocked` (minimal) already cleared 4.5:1 pre-existingly and were left untouched; the bare `:root` (no `data-theme` attribute) theme block was checked and confirmed structurally unreachable in real usage (`SettingsProvider`'s `applySettings` always sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` from real settings on load) rather than audited, since nothing can ever actually render it.

  **Covered**: Evaluation Center (`/runs` quality section), Mission Control (`/runs` attention section), Work Board (`/plan` board section), Goal Center (`/plan` goals section) — all 4 in both `futuristic` and `minimal` themes, all 8 passing live (`npx playwright test tests/e2e/accessibility-axe-scan.spec.ts tests/e2e/evaluation-center.spec.ts`).

  **Not yet covered** (this pass's own honest scope boundary, real future work rather than silently implied "done"): Session Workspace (this app's own default/most-visited destination — a strong candidate for the next increment), Projects, Loop Center, Scheduled Tasks (the other two Runs sections), and Settings' ~20 pages (`settings-pages.ts`, task 12.9's own count) entirely. Within the 4 covered destinations, only one populated state each was scanned, not every reachable sub-state (e.g., Mission Control's own Loops/Schedules tabs, Work Board's Kanban-vs-Stage-List compact mode, Goal Center's Sheet-based create/edit forms) — deeper per-destination state coverage is also real future work, not claimed here.
- [x] 20.19 Perform keyboard-only end-to-end passes for Session creation, message send/stop, Run inspection, Loop acceptance, Board movement, Goal linking, Evaluation comparison, Schedule editing, and Settings search/save. — All 9 named flows now have a real, live-verified keyboard-only Playwright test: `.focus()` establishes each step's own entry point (the same house style `cli-parameters-settings.spec.ts`/`onepiece-agent.spec.ts` already used before this task), then only `page.keyboard.press`/`.type` drives the flow under test, no `.click()`/`.fill()` on the actual decision points. Session creation (`create-session-wizard.spec.ts`, new "creates a session end to end using only the keyboard" — the file's pre-existing keyboard usage was only Tab-to-blur validation and one Escape-blocked negative check, not a full wizard pass, so this genuinely closes it rather than double-counting). Message send/stop (`onepiece-agent.spec.ts`'s existing 10.22 streaming/stop describe block, new test — `ChatInputBox.tsx` submits on a plain Enter keydown and the composer's own Stop button carries no `disabled`, both confirmed by reading the component first). Run inspection (`mission-control.spec.ts`, new test — opens a Run's detail via a focused `RunCard` button + Enter, then drives the section-nav's `role="tablist"` variant with ArrowRight/Home/End at a 2200px viewport, wide enough to clear `MissionControlSectionNav`'s own 640px compact threshold; first E2E-level proof of 16.8/16.18's roving-tabindex nav, distinct from `mission-control-section-nav.test.tsx`'s component-level coverage). Loop acceptance (`loop-engineering.spec.ts`, new test — focus/Enter on "接受结果", then the confirm block's "确认" button). Board movement (`todo-board.spec.ts`, new test — drives `WorkItemStageMenu`'s real keyboard model, confirmed by reading `use-menu-list.ts` first: opening resets focus to index 0, ArrowDown reaches "已计划", Enter selects it via the option button's native click-on-Enter). Goal linking (new `goal-center.spec.ts` — Goal Center had no e2e spec at all before this pass; searches for a Loop via real keystrokes in `ExecutionTargetPicker`, selects the one result via focus+Enter, confirms the link via a second focus+Enter). Evaluation comparison (`evaluation-center.spec.ts`, new test — both `EvaluationComparisonPanel` pickers are native `<select>`s, so a focused ArrowDown changes the selection and fires a real `change` event without opening the dropdown). Schedule editing (`workspace-activity-bar.spec.ts`, new test — this flow had no dedicated spec file and was missed by this task's own pre-dispatch research entirely; drives the row's `ActionMenu` More-menu Edit item, whose `useMenuList`-based open already focuses it first since it is `moreItems[0]`, then edits the name field via `ControlOrMeta+a` + real keystrokes and saves). Settings search/save (search already had real keyboard selection via ArrowDown/Enter in `settings-search.spec.ts`'s pre-existing "ArrowDown moves the highlighted result..." test, confirmed rather than rebuilt; save had zero keyboard coverage in `settings-save-discard.spec.ts` until this pass's new "keyboard-only: toggling a draft field and saving it..." test, driving the real `role="switch"` button in `cli-parameter-control.tsx`).

  Found and fixed one real, user-facing bug while building the Goal linking test: `LazyFeature` (`src/components/lazy-feature.tsx`) picks its lazy component via `useState(() => lazy(loader))`, a lazy initializer React only evaluates on that instance's first mount. `plan-destination.tsx`'s Board/Goals ternary fed two different loaders into that one shared JSX slot, so switching from Board (Plan's own default section) to the Goals tab silently kept rendering Work Board forever — Goal Center was unreachable via normal tab navigation for mouse and keyboard users alike, not a keyboard-specific defect. Verified live before assuming it was this task's own new test at fault (a minimal repro with no Loop/keyboard setup at all reproduced it identically), then fixed by splitting Board and Goals into two separate, permanently loader-fixed `LazyFeature` call sites — the same established pattern `runs-destination.tsx` already uses correctly for its own Loops/Schedules split. All 6 pre-existing `plan-destination.test.tsx` cases still pass unchanged.

  Disclosed, not fixed (outside this task's scope): `loop-engineering.spec.ts`'s pre-existing first test ("runs a Loop through pause, inspection, feedback, and acceptance...") fails at its own "暂停" (pause) confirm-step assertion, reproducibly, including against the unmodified file restored via `git stash` — confirmed genuinely pre-existing/environmental (this worktree has multiple concurrent sessions actively committing to loop-center/ui files right now) and unrelated to this task's diff. The new Loop-acceptance keyboard test deliberately avoids the same `agent-run-status` assertion that failing test also depends on (that widget needs a session actually selected, which this simpler flow never sets up) — scoped to Loop Center's own state text instead, matching the already-robust "rejects an acceptance-ready Loop..." test's pattern.
